# Actual retained values: two endpoints paid, the middle remains open

Status: JOINT UNVERIFIED ANALYTICAL PROOF ATTEMPT, with a quantitative
partial terminal estimate. No formal gate was allocated or called. This
report does not establish a complete obstruction or a benchmark witness
for the frozen policy, and does not resolve the original minimax problem.

Project gd-exact-profile-20260926; worker max3; completed baseline60;
one round, completed ceiling61 / attempted ceiling62. TASK.md and latest
read guidance56f0c49630564f24 apply. Plan finding9569f5e8ea1c9a9e.
The full evidence84a4435a88a9f487 and a9423b621329882d was read. Those
drafts are provenance, not accepted premises. The construction and all
estimates used below are derived jointly here. The only accepted input
is the benchmark/ray conclusion of fact04219554e41d9750.

The new partial conclusion is that the FIRST and LAST actual circular
component values have vanishing original-benchmark normalization. Both
proofs use the actual ordered suffix, all preceding physical updates,
and state-dependent signs. They do not require the accumulated positive
charge X_m to be small. The middle k-2 components remain unpaid.

## 1. The unchanged word, function, and initialization

For integers m>=16 set

    lambda=1+sqrt(2), k=floor(sqrt(m)), J=m-k, beta=1/k,
    eta_word=1-1/(4m), c=lambda*(1+1/(4m)), a_word=eta_word/c,
    d=c-2, H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,b_j,H_j), b_j=1+a_word*c^j,
    S_j=sum H_j, n_m=2^m-1.

All children use the SAME enclosing m. Select precisely the first
occurrences of levels J,...,m-1, at zero-based positions
2^(J+i-1)-1, i=1,...,k. The word is

    (H_J,b_J,H_J,b_(J+1),H_(J+1),...,b_(m-1),H_(m-1)).       (1.1)

Let s_1=S_J, s_i=S_(J+i-2) for 2<=i<=k, and s_(k+1)=S_(m-1).
Selected step i is b_(J+i-1). No extra checkpoint, step deletion,
reordering, state reset, or modification of the function is made.

For 0<=q<=1 define

    p(q)=beta+sqrt((1+beta)^2-(q+beta)^2),
    C_beta=beta+sqrt(1+2beta), v(q)=(p(q),-q),
    K=conv({0} union {v(q):0<=q<=1}).

For a compact convex body C containing0 put

    E_C(w)=max_(g in C){<g,w>-||g||^2/2}.

The one-sided Huber function Hub_delta is0 on(-infinity,0], t^2/2
on[0,delta], and delta*t-delta^2/2 on[delta,infinity).

Here is the exact symbolic local definition, included to fix the SAME
static function. For each ordinary block a_1,...,a_N in its physical
order, put alpha_j=a_j/2. Starting from (u_N,-y_N)=(beta,-1), choose
q_(j-1) in[0,1) backwards by

    (q_(j-1)+beta)/(p(q_(j-1))-beta)
      =max{beta/(C_beta-beta),
               (y_j+(1-alpha_j)*beta)/(u_j-(1-alpha_j)*beta)},
    (u_(j-1),-y_(j-1))=(u_j,-y_j)+alpha_j*v(q_(j-1)).         (1.2)

The word has strictly positive steps. The ratio on the left increases
from beta/(C_beta-beta) to infinity, and the denominator on the right
is positive. The formula therefore uniquely specifies a finite set of
local data without executing a recurrence. Denote the starting local
coordinates for block i by u_i,y_i and put M_i=u_i+y_i/beta.

Set ell_1=0,D_1=1 and recursively define static constants

    T_i=b_(J+i-1)/2-1, sigma_i=D_i/M_i,
    ell_(i+1)=sigma_i*y_i, A_i=ell_i+ell_(i+1)/beta,
    D_(i+1)=sigma_i*T_i,
    Phi_i(X,Y)=E_(sigma_i K)(X-A_i,Y-ell_(i+1)).              (1.3)

For sufficiently large m all T_i are positive. The fixed function and
initial point are

    delta=D_(k+1)/(1+S_(m-1)),
    F(x)=1/2 sum_(i=1)^k Phi_i(x_i,x_(i+1))
                  +1/2 Hub_delta(x_(k+1)-ell_(k+1)),
    x_0=e_1 in R^(k+1).                                    (1.4)

The physical update is x^+=x-h grad F(x). Multiplying F by an arbitrary
advertised L>0 and dividing the physical gradient step by L gives the
same trajectory and normalized objective.

## 2. Geometry, chronology, and true radius, derived jointly

Completing the square shows grad E_C(w)=Pi_C(w), the Euclidean
projection onto C. Projection optimality at w and w' gives
||Pi_C(w)-Pi_C(w')||^2<=<Pi_C(w)-Pi_C(w'),w-w'>. Comparing the two
maximization formulas gives differentiability with this gradient.
Thus E_C is convex and1-smooth. It is nonnegative, and is zero exactly
on the polar cone of C: a positive support value remains positive in
the envelope at a sufficiently small positive multiple of the
supporting vector.

The horizontal sections of K are

    0<=q<=1, beta*q<=p<=p(q).                               (2.1)

The lower boundary is the segment joining0 to(beta,-1), and the
concave upper boundary is the displayed circular arc. The endpoints
are(C_beta,0),(beta,-1). In particular (p,0) belongs to K whenever
(p,-q) does. Its cone has those two extreme rays. Therefore

    E_(sigma K)(U,V)=0 iff U<=0 and beta*U-V<=0.              (2.2)

The disk center(beta,beta), radius1+beta contains0. Its outward radial
vector at(p(q),-q) supports K. In (1.2), u_j>=beta,y_j>=1 and the
q's are nondecreasing in forward order. Indeed, before a backward
step the uncapped supporting ratio is(y_j+beta)/(u_j-beta); the
ratio defining its predecessor reduces the numerator by alpha_j*beta
and increases the denominator by the same amount. At q=0 the capped
normal-cone inequality is y_j<=beta*(u_j-C_beta)/(C_beta-beta),
which is preserved by further capped backward steps. Formula(1.2)
puts the vector from the candidate projection to the new point on
the circular outward normal, or in the endpoint normal cone when
q=0. Projection optimality gives

    Pi_K(u_j,-y_j)=v(q_j),
    u_i=beta+sum_j alpha_j*p(q_(j-1)),
    y_i=1+sum_j alpha_j*q_(j-1), u_i>=beta*y_i.              (2.3)

The local states(A_i+sigma_i*u_j,ell_(i+1)-sigma_i*y_j)
start at(ell_i+D_i,0), follow every step -(a_j/2)grad Phi_i,
and have last gradient sigma_i*(beta,-1). The checkpoint produces

    (x_i,x_(i+1))=(A_i-beta*D_(i+1),ell_(i+1)+D_(i+1)).     (2.4)

At that instant Phi_i is zero by(2.2), but it may reactivate later.
The construction does not reset it.

For EVERY state write

    (P_i,-Q_i)=Pi_(sigma_i K)(x_i-A_i,x_(i+1)-ell_(i+1)),
    P_(k+1)=clip(x_(k+1)-ell_(k+1),0,delta), Q_0=0.

Then every physical update, including all prehistory, is

    x_i^+=x_i-(h/2)(P_i-Q_(i-1)), 1<=i<=k+1.              (2.5)

If x_(i+1)>=ell_(i+1), replacing(p,-q) by(p,0) improves the
projection objective by q*(x_(i+1)-ell_(i+1))+q^2/2 for q>0.
Consequently Q_i=0 in that region. If x_i<=ell_i and the output
is nonnegative, Phi_i is zero by(2.2). These two properties prove
inductively that the designated active pair follows its local states:
the previous component contributes no derivative in its input
coordinate; the next component and all subsequent components vanish.
Every earlier retired coordinate still follows(2.5). This proves the
chronology without substituting post-checkpoint snapshots for the
actual later states.

The map stacking adjacent pairs and the last coordinate has squared
operator norm at most2. The factor1/2 in(1.4) therefore makes F
globally convex1-smooth. Also F>=0 and F(0)=0. Its complete minimum
set is

    C_min={x: x_i<=A_i, x_(i+1)>=beta*(x_i-ell_i), 1<=i<=k;
                                         x_(k+1)<=ell_(k+1)}.

The point x_*=(A_1,ell_2,0,...,0) is feasible. Since
1-A_1=sigma_1*u_1, ell_2=beta*A_1=sigma_1*y_1 and
u_1>=beta*y_1, A_1<=1/(1+beta^2). For a feasible point with
x_1<0 the squared distance from e_1 is at least1. On[0,A_1] the
lower bound (1-x_1)^2+beta^2*x_1^2 decreases up to A_1 and is
attained by x_*. Thus the true closest radius is

    R_m^2=dist(e_1,C_min)^2
         =(1-A_1)^2+ell_2^2=(u_1^2+y_1^2)/M_1^2.            (2.6)

Convex1-smooth interpolation, in both directions and including all
samples and minimizers, follows directly: apply the smooth descent
inequality to F(z)-<grad F(y),z>, whose minimum occurs at y, to get
F(x)-F(y)-<grad F(y),x-y>>=||grad F(x)-grad F(y)||^2/2.
All trajectory Gram matrices are actual Euclidean Gram matrices.

## 3. Uniform scales and the accepted benchmark input

We give the local estimates because earlier unverified drafts are not
premises. In a backward block let w_j=(y_j+beta)/(q_j+beta).
At an uncapped point u_j-beta=(p(q_j)-beta)*w_j. A cross product
in(1.2) gives

    w_(j-1)=w_j*(1+
      [p(q_(j-1))*q_j-q_(j-1)*p(q_j)]
                  /[beta*(p(q_(j-1))+q_(j-1))]).             (3.1)

At a transition to q_(j-1)=0 the same identity follows from
y_(j-1)=y_j, and if both q's are zero it is equality. Its numerator
is the integral of p(q_(j-1))-q_(j-1)*p'(t) between the two q's.
After division by p(q_(j-1))+q_(j-1), this integrand is at most
max(1,-p'(t)). Multiplication of(3.1), w_N=1 and 1+z<=exp(z)
therefore yield

    y_i<=2 exp(2k)=:Y_k,
    integral_0^1 max(1,-p'(t))dt=sqrt(2)*(1+beta)-beta<2.     (3.2)

For the integral, split at t=(1+beta)/sqrt(2)-beta and integrate
-p' on the second interval. At an uncapped point,
u_j<=beta+2k*(Y_k+beta). Such backward steps form one contiguous
portion; their total u increment is at most2k*(Y_k+beta), and
their p is at least beta. The remaining steps have p=C_beta.
Thus their total deficit from C_beta times half the block mass is
at most4k^2*(Y_k+beta). Combining this with(2.3) and y_i/beta
gives, uniformly in i,

    |M_i-C_beta*s_i/2|<=K_k, K_k=20k^2 exp(2k).              (3.3)

The concave function p(q)+q/beta has smaller endpoint C_beta.
Consequently (2.3) also gives the useful exact lower bound

    M_i>=beta+1/beta+C_beta*s_i/2
        >=C_beta*(1+s_i/2).                                (3.4)

Put v_mass=a_word/d. One has0<v_mass<1, since c*(c-2)>1>eta_word.
Solving S_(j+1)=2S_j+b_j by substitution gives the exact identities

    1+S_j=v_mass*c^j+(1-v_mass)*2^j,
    b_j=d*S_j+c-1-d*(1-v_mass)*2^j.                         (3.5)

They imply uniformly for J<=j<=m-1 that S_j/(v_mass*c^j)->1,
k*K_k/S_J->0, and

    S_J/lambda^J->exp(1/4),
    S_(m-r)/lambda^(m-r)->exp(1/4) for fixed r>=1.           (3.6)

For these claims, the relative error in the first limit is bounded
by a constant times c^(-J)+(2/c)^J. The logarithm of k*K_k is
O(k+log k), whereas J log(lambda) is of order m. Finally use
0<=x-log(1+x)<=x^2/2 with x=1/(4m), and k/m->0.

The first transfer ratio T_1/M_1 equals(d/C_beta)*(1+o(1)); all
later ones equal(c*d/C_beta)*(1+o(1)). Their relative errors are
uniformly O(K_k/S_J+c^(-J)+(2/c)^J), and k times this bound
tends to zero. Thus

    D_(k+1)=(d/C_beta)*(c*d/C_beta)^(k-1)*(1+o(1))
            ->(lambda-2)*exp(-2),
    R_m^2->1, sigma_1~2/(C_beta*S_J).                        (3.7)

Here c*d=1+O(1/m), and log(C_beta)/beta->2, so C_beta^k->exp(2).
The radius follows from(2.6),(3.2),(3.3). For every i<k,

    sigma_(i+1)/sigma_i=T_i/M_(i+1)->lambda-2               (3.8)

uniformly. Eventually all these ratios lie in[1/3,1/2], so
sum_i sigma_i^2<=2sigma_1^2.

The ORIGINAL all-split benchmark is C_0=B_0=1 and

    C_n=max_(0<=r<n) Omega(C_r,C_(n-r-1)),
    B_n=max_(0<=r<n) G(C_r,B_(n-r-1)),
    Omega(x,y)=(x+y+sqrt((x+y)^2+4xy))/2,
    G(x,y)=2x+y/2+sqrt(y^2+8xy)/2.

Only fact04219554e41d9750 is used for

    0<Z_1=lim_m lambda^(-m)*B_(2^m-1)<infinity.              (3.9)

Its statement and proof Section7 were read. Its enclosure at b=1,j=0
also gives Z_1<5 from B_1=4. No seed assumption or unverified
construction is imported from that fact.

A growth estimate needed below is

    (B_(n_m)/R_m^2)*sigma_1^2*(1+K_k+2^m)->0.              (3.10)

Indeed B*sigma_1^2=O(lambda^(-m+2k)). Multiplication by K_k is
still exponentially decreasing in m because k=O(sqrt(m)); the term
2^m gives O((2/lambda)^m*lambda^(2k)), which tends to zero since
lambda>2. No numerical evaluation or recurrence replay is used.

## 4. Actual retired dynamics and screen I: the accumulated charge

Index the actual final suffix H_(m-1) by t=0,...,N,
N=2^(m-1)-1. Its input x(0) is the result of EVERY earlier step(2.5).
The last excess evolves as D_(k+1)-delta times half the mass already
read. It remains at least delta, and Q_k=0 by the output-isolation
property. The fixed function governing the first k coordinates is

    G_ret(x)=1/2 sum_(i<k) Phi_i(x_i,x_(i+1))
                     +1/2 Hub_(C_beta*sigma_k)(x_k-A_k).    (4.1)

The retired update is still(2.5), with Q_0=Q_k=0, and
P_k=clip(x_k-A_k,0,C_beta*sigma_k). Write

    r_i=x_i-A_i,
    z_i=beta*(x_i-ell_i)-x_(i+1), i<k,
    U_i=P_i-beta*Q_i for i<k, U_k=P_k,
    kappa=1+beta^2, theta=(1-beta)/(1+beta).

In the cone coordinates P=U+beta*Q, the exact maximization formula is

    Phi_i=max {U*r_i+Q*z_i-[(U+beta*Q)^2+Q^2]/2:
      0<=Q<=sigma_i,
      0<=U<=sigma_i*p(Q/sigma_i)-beta*Q}.                    (4.2)

Hence U_i>=0. If r_i<=0 then U_i=0 and

    Q_i=clip(z_i/kappa,0,sigma_i),
    Phi_i=Hub_(kappa*sigma_i)(z_i)/kappa.                    (4.3)

There are also the unconditional complementary sign facts

    U_i>0 implies x_i>A_i>=ell_i and Q_(i-1)=0;
    Q_i>0 implies x_(i+1)<ell_(i+1) and U_(i+1)=0.          (4.4)

The exact residual equations, including BOTH neighbors, are

    r_i^+=r_i-(h/2)(U_i+beta*Q_i-Q_(i-1)),
    z_i^+=z_i-(h/2)[kappa*Q_i-beta*Q_(i-1)-beta*Q_(i+1)
                                             +beta*U_i-U_(i+1)]. (4.5)

The retained value and the total normalized payoff are exactly

    V_m=2G_ret(x(N))=sum_(i=1)^k Phi_i(x(N)),
    B_(n_m)*2F(x_(n_m))/R_m^2=T_m+B_(n_m)*V_m/R_m^2,
    T_m=B_(n_m)*D_(k+1)^2/[2(1+S_(m-1))*R_m^2]
        ->T_infty=Z_1*exp(-17/4)/(2lambda).                 (4.6)

In the sum, Phi_k means exactly the last horizontal envelope, equal
to the original circular value at the actual state. The limit follows
from(3.6)--(3.9) and (lambda-2)^2=lambda^(-2). In particular
T_infty<5/64<1, using exp(17/4)>16 and2lambda>4.

Screen I assesses the previous sufficient NONNEGATIVE charge

    X_m=C_beta*sum_i sigma_i*(r_i(N))_+
         +(1/2)sum_t h_t sum_(i<k) sigma_i
                                     [theta*U_i(t)+U_(i+1)(t)]. (4.7)

The elementary cap estimate for its flux part is only

    X_flux <= (C_beta*S_(m-1)/2)
                   sum_(i<k) sigma_i*(theta*sigma_i+sigma_(i+1)).

The RIGHT SIDE after normalization has order lambda^(2k), by the
positive limiting ratio sigma_2/sigma_1 and(3.6)--(3.9). It does not
vanish or supply the required strict constant. This is a failure of
that upper comparison; it is NOT a lower bound on actual X_m.

Signed coordinate budgets do not automatically improve it. Define
H_i^U=(1/2)sum_t h_t U_i(t) and H_i^Q=(1/2)sum_t h_t Q_i(t),
with H_0^Q=H_k^Q=0. Then

    x_i(0)-x_i(N)=H_i^U+beta*H_i^Q-H_(i-1)^Q.              (4.8)

The telescoping positive weights that eliminate every H_i^Q are
proportional to beta^(i-1), since their adjacent ratio must be beta.
The bulk weights in(4.7) have adjacent ratios asymptotic to lambda-2,
with different harmless endpoint factors. Explicitly their coefficients
on H_i^U are theta*sigma_1, theta*sigma_i+sigma_(i-1) for
2<=i<k, and sigma_(k-1) at i=k. They are not those telescoping
weights. Converting between the two
can cost ((lambda-2)/beta)^O(k), and the signed endpoint displacement
has not itself been bounded on the required scale.

The crucial actual restriction is stronger than this budget for the
first coordinate. At its own checkpoint x_1=A_1-beta*D_2. Thereafter
x_1 never increases, since Q_0=0 and P_1>=0 in EVERY physical step.
Thus throughout the suffix

    r_1<=-beta*D_2<0, U_1=0.                               (4.9)

This is a property of the initialized trajectory, not invariance of
the entire input slab. Even after dropping U_1 from the cap estimate,
the sigma_1*U_2 charge retains the same lambda^(2k) scale in that
upper bound. The actual barrier will remove the U_2 charge from the FIRST
terminal component by a last-entry argument. We have not proved
that X_m itself fails to vanish, nor supplied a lower bound showing
that it overcounts by a nonvanishing amount. Its status remains open.
Only the unnecessary use of all-time U_2 to bound Phi_1 is eliminated.

## 5. Screen II: quantitative actual terminal bounds at both ends

This is the viable part of the round. A last entry uses an occurrence
of b_j in H_(m-1). Immediately following that occurrence lies an entire
H_j inside the remaining suffix. This property follows by induction
from H_(j+1)=(H_j,b_j,H_j), and keeps the original order and enclosing
m. It is used only on samples AFTER the last entry, so the claimed
region persists by the choice of the last entry, not by an assumed
invariance theorem.

### 5.1 First circular component: the apparent source switches off

By(4.9), formula(4.3) applies to component1 at EVERY suffix sample.
The exact scalar residual update is

    z_1^+=z_1-(h/2)(kappa*Q_1-P_2).                        (5.1)

It includes the full P_2=U_2+beta*Q_2. If z_1>kappa*sigma_1,
then Q_1=sigma_1 and

    x_2=beta*x_1-z_1
       <ell_2-beta^2*D_2-kappa*sigma_1<ell_2.

Therefore r_2<0, U_2=0 and P_2=beta*Q_2<=beta*sigma_2.
Define gamma_1=kappa*sigma_1-beta*sigma_2. In this region every
physical step loses at least h*gamma_1/2 in z_1, with no restriction
on h. In contrast, an entry step from z_1<=kappa*sigma_1 can add
at most h*C_beta*sigma_2/2. These are different, justified caps.

Eventually gamma_1>0 and

    d*C_beta*sigma_2<=gamma_1,                              (5.2)

because the ratio of the left side to sigma_1 tends to
(lambda-2)^2<1, whereas gamma_1/sigma_1 tends to1.

If z_1(N)>kappa*sigma_1 and there was an entry during the suffix,
take its last occurrence, at b_j. All later samples remain above the
threshold. Pay the entry with C_beta*sigma_2 and the following H_j
with gamma_1. Every subsequent step has nonnegative loss. Thus

    z_1(N)<=kappa*sigma_1
                      +(b_j*C_beta*sigma_2-gamma_1*S_j)/2
            <=kappa*sigma_1+(c-1)*C_beta*sigma_2/2,         (5.3)

where(3.5) and(5.2) pay every large bridge.

The case of no entry must use the ACTUAL full prehistory. Let
S_pre=S_(m-1)+b_(m-1) be the mass before the final suffix. From
(2.5), x_1(0)<=1 and x_2(0)>=-C_beta*sigma_2*S_pre/2.
Hence

    z_1(0)<=beta+C_beta*sigma_2*S_pre/2.                    (5.4)

If all samples from t=0 were above the threshold, summing(5.1)
would give z_1(N)<=z_1(0)-gamma_1*S_(m-1)/2. This is negative
eventually: S_pre/S_(m-1)->lambda-1 and

    C_beta*sigma_2*S_pre/(gamma_1*S_(m-1))
                  ->(lambda-2)*(lambda-1)=2-sqrt(2)<1,
    beta/(sigma_1*S_(m-1))->0.

So the no-entry case cannot leave a terminal positive residual above
the threshold. If the terminal residual is below the threshold, (5.3)
already bounds its positive part. Consequently, for every sufficiently
large m, on the prescribed actual trajectory,

    (z_1(N))_+<=kappa*sigma_1+(c-1)*C_beta*sigma_2/2
               <=2sigma_1,
    0<=Phi_1(x(N))<=2sigma_1^2.                             (5.5)

This is a full terminal bound for that component. No U_2 integral,
independent two-Huber trajectory, or alternative state was substituted.
By(3.10), B_(n_m)*Phi_1(x(N))/R_m^2 tends to zero.

### 5.2 Last circular component: the small cap mismatch is paid

Write L_k=C_beta*sigma_k for the horizontal gradient cap. The actual
checkpoint formula for the active kth coordinate gives

    r_k(0)=-beta*D_(k+1)<0.                                (5.6)

This statement concerns only coordinate k at its OWN checkpoint; it
makes no assertion about the other retired coordinates. During its
active ordinary block and checkpoint Q_(k-1)=0 by output isolation,
so preceding gradients do not alter this particular formula.

At any suffix sample with r_k>L_k, x_k>ell_k, whence Q_(k-1)=0
and P_k=L_k. Thus r_k loses h*L_k/2 there. At an entry from
r_k<=L_k its increase is at most h*sigma_(k-1)/2. If the terminal
residual exceeds L_k, (5.6) guarantees an entry. Its last occurrence
at b_j and its following H_j give

    (r_k(N))_+<=L_k+(1/2) max_(0<=j<=m-2)
                                      [b_j*sigma_(k-1)-L_k*S_j]_+.
                                                                  (5.7)

No assumption of distance descent is used. Put s=S_(m-2),
T=b_(m-2)/2-1 and Delta_k=d-C_beta*T/M_k. The EXACT ratio
sigma_k/sigma_(k-1)=T/M_k follows from(1.3). Thus(3.5) implies

    (r_k(N))_+<=L_k+(sigma_(k-1)/2)
                                      [c-1+(Delta_k)_+*s]. (5.8)

To bound the possible positive cap defect, expand it BEFORE estimating:

    d*M_k-C_beta*T
       =d*(M_k-C_beta*s/2)+C_beta*(3-c)/2
                   +(C_beta*d/2)*(1-v_mass)*2^(m-2).

By(3.3),(3.4), s/M_k<=2/C_beta. Therefore

    (Delta_k)_+*s
       <=(2d/C_beta)*K_k+3+d*(1-v_mass)*2^(m-2).            (5.9)

Define the explicit nonnegative error allowance

    A_err,m=c-1+(2d/C_beta)*K_k+3
                                  +d*(1-v_mass)*2^(m-2).

Since Hub_(L_k)(r)<=L_k*(r)_+, the last retained value satisfies

    0<=Phi_k(x(N))
       <=L_k^2+(L_k*sigma_(k-1)/2)*A_err,m.                 (5.10)

All constants c,d,C_beta remain bounded, and sigma_k,sigma_(k-1)
are at most sigma_1 eventually. Formula(3.10) pays (5.10), including
the changed cap ratio and its 2^(m-2) mass correction. Thus
B_(n_m)*Phi_k(x(N))/R_m^2 tends to zero uniformly along the frozen
growing policy. The bound is quantitative and applies to every physical
bridge; it does not require exact absorption of the final component.

## 6. Screen III: why the same comparison does not close the middle

This screen was rejected as a complete uniform comparison. The exact
failure is stronger than merely saying that the middle is coupled.

Fix 2<=i<=k-1 and put L_i=C_beta*sigma_i. At a sample with r_i>L_i,
output isolation gives Q_(i-1)=0. Maximizing over P at fixed Q in
(2.1), r_i>L_i forces the UPPER circular boundary, so

    P_i=sigma_i*p(Q_i/sigma_i),
    L_i-P_i=sigma_i*[sqrt(1+2beta)
               -sqrt((1+beta)^2-(beta+Q_i/sigma_i)^2)].     (6.1)

Unlike at i=k, this cap loss need not be zero. Concavity of p gives

    0<=L_i-P_i<=(C_beta-beta)*Q_i.                          (6.2)

After the last entry into r_i>L_i, let s_entry be its step index.
As long as that terminal excursion persists, its exact signed loss is

    r_i(N)=r_i(s_entry+1)
                   -(L_i/2) sum_(t>s_entry) h_t
                   +D_i^cap(s_entry),
    D_i^cap(s_entry)=(1/2)sum_(t>s_entry)h_t*(L_i-P_i(t)).   (6.3)

This is a tail-specific cap loss, not the all-time source X_m. The
entry is bounded by L_i+b_j*sigma_(i-1)/2. Its following H_j pays
the full horizontal-cap part exactly as in(5.7). However D_i^cap
has not been paid. If Q_i>0, formula(4.4) does give U_(i+1)=0,
but the next coordinate then follows

    x_(i+1)^+=x_(i+1)+(h/2)(Q_i-beta*Q_(i+1)).              (6.4)

Thus bounding the accumulated Q_i in(6.2) requires its actual
downstream negative excursions and the NEXT neighboring gradient.
Deleting beta*Q_(i+1), or assuming a frozen output, is not legitimate.
When Q_i=0, later horizontal motion of component i+1 can recreate a
negative output at a large bridge. No entry-and-persistence statement
for a fixed quadratic core or a fixed face has been proved here.

There are two explicit quantitative failures of a cap-only closure.

First, even the always-full-cap idealization cannot pay the available
incoming horizontal estimate for a middle coordinate. The full
prehistory bound from(2.5) is

    r_i(0)<=sigma_(i-1)*S_pre/2-A_i, i>=2.                 (6.5)

Dropping -A_i and comparing its leading coefficient to L_i*S_(m-1)/2
gives the limiting ratio

    sigma_(i-1)*S_pre/(C_beta*sigma_i*S_(m-1))
                   ->(lambda-1)/(lambda-2)=2+sqrt(2)>1.    (6.6)

The bound in(6.5) is not the actual value, and does not show that an
always-positive horizontal excursion occurs. It shows why one cannot
discard that branch from a last-entry proof without a stronger actual
prehistory estimate. The endpoint proofs avoided this: the first used
its permanent negative input, and the last used its own exact negative
incoming residual. Neither argument applies automatically to a middle
input after all later checkpoints.

Keeping the omitted A_i does not repair this particular comparison:
A_i=sigma_(i-1)*y_(i-1)+sigma_i*y_i/beta, so(3.2),(3.8)
give A_i/(sigma_(i-1)*S_(m-1))->0 uniformly. Thus the positive
leading allowance in(6.6) survives that exact static threshold.

Second, the best state-independent lower bound for P_i on r_i>L_i
from this same circular geometry is its infimum beta*sigma_i. To see
why a larger fixed lower bound is unavailable, fix r>L_i and q in(0,1).
For q sufficiently close to1 put

    t=(r-sigma_i*p(q))/(p(q)-beta)>0,
    w=sigma_i*q+t*(q+beta).

At the centered point(r,-w), the displacement from sigma_i*v(q) is
t*(p(q)-beta,-q-beta), an outward circular normal. Hence the actual
projection for the SAME envelope is sigma_i*v(q). Letting q approach1
gives P_i approaching beta*sigma_i while r remains fixed above L_i.
Other coordinates can be chosen with the permanent x_1 bound intact.
These are alternative states of the unchanged function, NOT states
proved reachable from initialization. Their purpose is only to test a
uniform cap-floor lemma, not to refute trajectory-specific control.

Using that valid floor in a last-entry comparison leaves the positive
coefficient

    d*sigma_(i-1)-beta*sigma_i.

For i=2 it is a fixed positive fraction of sigma_1 eventually, by
(3.8) and beta->0. The entry at level m-2 in the maximum of the
comparison therefore leaves an allowance of order
sigma_1*S_(m-2). Multiplying the horizontal-value allowance by
C_beta*sigma_2 and the original benchmark gives order lambda^(2k),
by(3.6)--(3.9). This comparison does not even give a bounded normalized
upper estimate, let alone one below1-T_infty. This is the scale of a
FAILED UPPER BOUND, not an attained terminal lower bound.

The cap-only method is therefore rejected without pretending that
(6.3) is a closed recursion. A viable replacement must simultaneously
pay the actual incoming middle residuals and the ordered cap losses
D_i^cap, or directly bound the complete actual middle value. Both
neighbors must be retained in that estimate. No universal distance
descent or large-step Markov positivity was assumed: expansion of
distance would retain h*(2-h) times the gradient-square term, with
its negative sign for h>2. The present endpoint proofs instead pay
each large step through its following physical subtree.

## 7. Exact new handoff and limits of the result

Let

    V_mid,m=sum_(i=2)^(k-1) Phi_i(x(N)),
    epsilon_m=(B_(n_m)/R_m^2)*
       [2sigma_1^2+L_k^2+(L_k*sigma_(k-1)/2)*A_err,m].

Every quantity uses the same unchanged static function and its actual
full-prehistory trajectory. The jointly derived estimates prove, at
the UNVERIFIED DRAFT level,

    0<=epsilon_m->0,
    T_m+(B_(n_m)/R_m^2)*V_mid,m
       <=B_(n_m)*2F(x_(n_m))/R_m^2
       <=T_m+(B_(n_m)/R_m^2)*V_mid,m+epsilon_m.             (7.1)

This is a quantitative removal of two terminal contributions from
the comparison, not physical deletion of either component. They
remain in the actual gradient throughout. One CANNOT repeat the
endpoint argument by renaming component2 as component1: U_2 has not
been shown zero, and its incoming Q_1 has not been removed. Similarly,
component k-1 continues to have its original moving output and Q_(k-1).

The remaining actual quantity is V_mid,m for k-2 growing middle
components. No bound below1-T_infty for its normalized limsup is
established, and no normalized lower witness is established. The
defect D_i^cap and the incoming r_i(0) are precise unpaid mechanisms
in the failed third comparison, not a claimed substitute theorem.
We have also NOT decided whether B_(n_m)*X_m/R_m^2 tends to zero.

Accordingly the frozen policy remains OPEN. The original question
over all words and all horizons remains OPEN even if this policy were
eventually decided. The assigned one round ends with the quantitative
endpoint progress and the explicit middle comparison failure; it does
not automatically renew the allocation.

## 8. Retrieval, resources, and persistence

Skills used: query-memory, direct-proving, construct-counterexamples,
search-math-results. The formal gate required by the usual worker
promotion workflow was explicitly disallowed by TASK.md and current
guidance, so every assertion in this report remains unverified.

Full shared evidence84a4435a88a9f487 and a9423b621329882d was read,
as was obstacle03228f06cb53bad3. The first broad recall displays were
truncated; required source evidence was then read in bounded complete
sections. Fact search found no accepted result for this coupled
growing prefix. The statement and relevant proof of04219554e41d9750
were read; no other accepted fact is used.

Retrieval stage: mature_subproblem. Search mode: repair, directed at
last-crossing bounds with neighboring flux and possible schedule
robustness. Matlas returned unrelated projected/noisy-subgradient,
DC, small-step self-contraction and line-search results. Their
hypotheses do not match this fixed long-step trajectory. Web search
located the existing Silver and composition literature, but supplied
no justified transfer to the perturbed word. No external theorem was
imported, and no new technical literature branch was opened. In
particular a smaller effective smoothness was not assumed to make
this word a certified Silver schedule.

Three comparisons were screened: (I) accumulated/signed flux;
(II) state-dependent terminal last entries, fully developed at the
two endpoints; (III) extension to middle components via cap loss,
rejected as a complete cap-only uniform comparison. No fourth
comparison, construction repair or additional checkpoint was used.

Mathematical programs0; numerical evaluations0; arithmetic replays0;
executed recurrences0; solvers0; benchmark DP0; formal submissions0;
new accepted facts0; installations0; consults0; subagents0.
Only administrative text reads/writes, memory operations, retrieval,
and file-integrity metadata checks were performed. All old grants
remain closed. New files are confined to the assigned absolute public
directory actual_retired_flux_20261001; existing private memory is
appended for continuation. No truth-store files were edited directly.

Expected completed count after return61; the official post-return

# Independent analytical audit of the complete actual two-family candidate

Worker: max4. One allocated audit round, after completed61, completed
ceiling62 / attempted ceiling63. Status: ANALYTICAL AUDIT ONLY; no
verifier verdict, accepted fact, or original-target completion.

## 1. Verdict, frozen source, and exact scope

**The full stated L=1 candidate survived this independent analytical
attack.** I found no substantive mathematical error or missing estimate
in its claimed two-family theorem. In particular, the new Section9A
discrete turning estimate and its deletion comparison survive changes
in adjacent levels and incoming-child sizes. PASS below means that this
analytical attack found no defect; it does not mean verifier acceptance.

There is one notation clarification outside the fixed-L=1 calculation:
the general-L scaling sentence must use the normalized payoff with L in
the denominator. This is separated from the theorem audit in
`notation_recommendation.md`. It does not alter a function, selection,
parameter domain, or constant in the assigned theorem. There is no
substantive repair recommendation and no iterative repair round.

Frozen source, read in its entirety (all 1223 lines):

    shared finding bfff4aa387a9d818, author max2
    /opt/danus/runtime/projects/gd-exact-profile-20260926/research/shared_evidence/bfff4aa387a9d818.md
    53449 bytes
    SHA256 bb0fb6c31e6f2711493fec2558266e896b5c0c42a8f70c76e83c4bcf7d767ca2

No source bytes were changed. Component drafts are provenance, not
accepted premises. The sole mathematical predecessor used by the frozen
candidate is accepted fact04219554e41d9750, for the original benchmark
and its certified saved interval table. I read its relevant statement,
ray proof, table-certificate scope, and the referenced saved row.

The physical word is defined, with one enclosing pair (m,tau), by

    m>=1536, 0<tau<=1/4,
    lambda=1+sqrt(2), beta=1-tau/m,
    c=lambda(1+tau/m), a=beta/c,
    H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+a*c^j,H_j), j<m,
    S_j=sum H_j, M_j=1+S_j, n_m=2^m-1.

Every child keeps these same enclosing parameters. A chronological
selection writes the original word as

    (block_0,b_0,block_1,b_1,...,block_(k-1),b_(k-1),block_k),
    s_i=sum block_i.

There is ONE common pair kappa>0, 0<epsilon<=1/2 for the whole selection.
It may depend arbitrarily on m; different stages do not get separate
pairs. Ordinary blocks retain all physical entries. The candidate's
general geometric construction also permits zeros and arbitrarily large
ordinary steps.

At advertised smoothness L=1, the payoff is

    E=2(f(x_(n_m))-min f)/dist(e_0,argmin f)^2,
    H_base=1/(1+2*S_m).

The audit covers every eligible ordered selection of every count. The
claimed bounds are MAIN nonempty E<(441/500)H_base and SOURCE nonempty
E<(9801/10000)H_base. The main and combined optima include their empty
selection and equal H_base; the source-only optimum is
1/[2(1+S_m)]. The original-B deficits and the fixed-tau limit are checked
below. Stage-dependent pairs, retained-circle components, unrestricted
W_n, and the original minimax question are outside this candidate.

## 2. Interface table

All references below are to the frozen source unless a fact is named.

| General-L wording, outside the fixed-L=1 formula | PASS for assigned theorem; editorial clarification | Section1 line38 and Section12 lines1096-1097. Define E_L with denominator L*dist^2 when scaling the function. |

No substantive interface is marked FAIL or GAP. The following details
record the attempted attacks and why they did not expose a failure.

## 3. Geometry, chronology, and true radius

Put theta_sp=arctan(1/epsilon) and

    C=sqrt(1+epsilon^2)*exp(kappa*theta_sp),
    R(theta)=C*exp(-kappa*theta),
    e(theta)=(cos theta,-sin theta),
    t(theta)=(-sin theta,-cos theta),
    v(theta)=R(theta)e(theta)=(p(theta),-q(theta)).

The supporting normal is n(theta)=e(theta)+kappa*t(theta). For
Delta=phi-theta, the derivative of
exp(-kappa*Delta)(cos Delta+kappa*sin Delta) is
-(1+kappa^2)exp(-kappa*Delta)sin Delta. It changes sign in the correct
direction at0 for the entire permitted angle interval. This validates
support against both earlier and later arc points, not merely adjacent
sampled slopes. Support against0 is immediate from its smaller value.

For one local chronological ordinary list, the backward endpoint is
(epsilon,-1). The source's cap test is

    y<=kappa*(u+(alpha-1)*C).

If it holds, mu=u+(alpha-1)C>=y/kappa>0 and

    r_old-v(0)=mu*n(0)+(0,kappa*mu-y).

Both coefficients are nonnegative. The resulting state satisfies
y_old<=kappa*(u_old-C), which proves persistence of every preceding
cap even for alpha=0. If the cap test fails, the root in(6) exists
uniquely: its left side increases strictly with theta, equals1 at the
current projected angle, and is below1-alpha at0. The root is allowed
to equal the current angle for a zero step. Its normal coefficient is
nonnegative because the state's polar angle is no smaller than the
root angle.

The uncapped normal representation is

    r=R(theta)*[(1+nu)e(theta)+kappa*nu*t(theta)], nu>=0.

The exact relation(8) proves the two stated bounds for nu. For
kappa<1 its exponential comparison telescopes; for kappa>=1 the
coefficient A_D is at most1 and the B_D contributions sum to at most
theta_sp/kappa. Thus the proof does not extrapolate the singular-looking
first branch through kappa=1. Its limit at1 is theta_sp, agreeing with
the second branch there.

For every local output P,Q, the pointwise inequalities p>=epsilon*q,
p<=C, p+q>=1 imply

    P>=epsilon*Q, 0<=P<=C*s_loc,
    (epsilon+P)^2+(1+Q)^2 >= (1+epsilon+s_loc)^2/2.

The derivative of q locates its maximum at the endpoint when
kappa<=epsilon and at arctan(1/kappa) otherwise, giving the stated
qmax including the equality case. A negative horizontal normal can
occur only when p<kappa*q. Since u>=epsilon, it gives
u-p>=epsilon-kappa*qmax>=-N_sp. Otherwise u>=p. This checks the
correction used by the main threshold for all kappa, including kappa>1.

Both endpoint planes are present in every finite local maximum. Every
other slope satisfies

    (p,-q)=((p-epsilon*q)/C)*(C,0)+q*(epsilon,-1),

with nonnegative coefficients. This proves the complete zero wedge,
not just a convenient subset of minimizers. Conditions(11) make the
pre-activation quadrant and the entire post-checkpoint vertical ray
part of this wedge. The proximal subgradient test uses all sampled
planes through the supporting-normal inequality just checked.

For the SOURCE sum of local envelopes, the coefficient1/2 is essential:
the actual h_t update implements alpha=h_t/2. Its next amplitude is
D'_i*sigma'_i*b_i/2. Its shifts use Q_i, and its final hinge has the same
coefficient1/2. The derived exact terminal numerator is
(D'_k)^2/[2(1+s_k)]. All preceding components are at minima and all
future components are at minima along the stated physical trajectory.
Each coordinate belongs to at most two half-weighted 1-smooth terms,
including the terminal hinge. This proves global 1-smoothness. The
resulting global interpolation inequality applies to every ordered
pair, the terminal sample, and every minimizer; physical vectors also
give a positive semidefinite Gram matrix without an added assumption.

The SOURCE minimum set is exactly(19). Let
z_bar=(c'_(0,1),c'_(0,2),0,...) and w=e_0-z_bar. Its first-row normal
representation is

    w=sigma'_0*(P_0-epsilon*Q_0)*e_0
       +sigma'_0*(1+Q_0)*(epsilon*e_0-e_1).

Both coefficients are nonnegative. Every point x in the full minimum
set satisfies <w,x-z_bar><=0 even if z_bar violates later constraints.
Hence

    ||e_0-x||^2
      =||w||^2+||x-z_bar||^2-2<w,x-z_bar> >=||w||^2.

This is exactly the direction needed for an upper payoff bound. It
does not reuse the larger, generally invalid one-source-checkpoint
radius. With the half-step mass, ||w||^2 is at least
(sigma'_0)^2*(s_0+2)^2/8. Cancellation with the exact first transfer
gives precisely(23), including its factor1 rather than an extra2 or1/2.

The exact all-row source identity(20) is also consistent: each
nonnegative independent-row multiplier gives a dual distance lower
bound, and the true projection has a normal generated by active rows.
An independent active subcollection attains the distance. The argument
does not require every maximizing candidate center to be feasible.

For the MAIN envelope, ordinary steps use their full physical values.
The proximal first coordinate is at least U_i>=0 because
epsilon*(b_i-1)>=N_sp. Its proximal second coordinate is between0 and
sigma_i*(1+Q_i). These inequalities respectively preserve the previous
zero wedge and the next pre-activation wedge. Farther terms vanish as
well. The current local slope is therefore an active subgradient of
the maximum defining J. The checkpoint leaves next amplitude
D_i*sigma_i*(b_i-1) and shift D_i*sigma_i*(1+Q_i), as stated.

Here the first center z_bar IS feasible: its second coordinate is
ell_1, so the next local input is(0,0); every subsequent input is in its
pre-activation wedge. The same nonnegative normal proves exact closest
distance, giving(43). This difference between the two realizations is
preserved throughout the proof. The terminal gradients and values follow
from D_k-(u+1)*delta>=0 for every tail prefix mass u<=s_k.

## 4. Actual upper functionals and the high-C/source comparisons

Use r_h=c(c-2), v_h=beta/r_h, d_j=a*c^j, and u_j=d_j/M_j. Direct
substitution in the mass recurrence gives

    M_j=v_h*c^j+(1-v_h)*2^j,
    0<v_h<1, 1<=M_j<=c^j, a<=u_j<=c-2<21/50.

Every globally minimum selected level j has both full H_j children
unselected. Its adjacent ordinary sums satisfy s,t>=M_j-1, including
first/last selections and ties. Removing the checkpoint reclassifies
its full physical value b=1+d_j as ordinary. No trajectory is preserved
by this operation; only an already proved upper functional is compared.

For SOURCE, the remaining denominators in(23) are exact transfer
denominators C*s_i+2*A+epsilon*b_i. The first deletion reduces to(28).
At s=t=M-1 it has the difference(29) when C>=r_h, with first coefficient
C-u(2+u)>=0 and the other terms positive. The interior difference is
(32); q=2A-C>=3C makes its remaining coefficients positive. If C<r_h,
Section8 proves A>2*C*M_max, and(30),(33) prove these same deletions.
The last deletion has ratio at most5/8 by(34)-(35), with no dichotomy
needed. All monotonicity reductions use positive derivative signs over
the original domain s,t>=M-1, rather than at only one selected word.

The SOURCE dichotomy is valid on the full parameter domain. If C<r_h,
then epsilon<1/16, theta_sp>23/16 and kappa<1/768. In particular kappa<1
has been proved before using its exponential T branch. The exponent
X_sp=(1-kappa^2)*theta_sp/kappa exceeds m, and

    1+(1+kappa)*T=(exp(X_sp)-kappa)/(1-kappa)>=exp(X_sp).

This yields A>16*C*exp(m)>2*C*M_max. It does not imply any corresponding
lower bound by A for an actual MAIN transfer denominator.

For MAIN, differentiating p+q/epsilon shows either monotone decrease
or a single interior maximum. Its minimum is therefore the smaller
endpoint C_floor=min(C,A_floor), where A_floor=epsilon+epsilon^(-1).
Consequently the ACTUAL denominator is at least C_floor*s_i+A_floor.
When C>=r_h, C_floor>=r_h and A_floor>=C_floor. The three deletion
calculations in Section9 use those actual lower bounds with the
exceptional first radius bounded by(s_0+1)^2/2. Their first/interior
differences have nonnegative leading coefficient
C_floor-u(2+u), and the last ratio is at mostu^2*(3+2u)<=1.

After either source or main deletion reaches one checkpoint, X=1+s
and Y=1+t are independently at least M. The relevant ratio decreases
in each. SOURCE eligibility gives d>=9, hence

    (d+1)/(M+1)<=10*u/(9+u)<70/157<9/20,

and its ratio is less than 2*(121/50)*(9/20)^2=9801/10000.
MAIN eligibility gives d>=4 and M>200/21>19/2. Its ratio is at most

    2*u^2*[2+u+(1+u)/(2*M-1)] < 2*u^2*(5/2)<441/500.

All strict comparisons hold at the eligibility thresholds themselves.

## 5. Attack of Section9A: discrete angles and caps

This is the central new interface. Assume C<r_h and write
eta_turn=999/1000 and Delta_C=r_h-C. The proven bounds include

    epsilon<1/16, theta_sp>23/16, kappa<1/768,
    C<769/768, 0<Delta_C<2/m<1/4,
    kappa*m*theta_sp<44/25, log c<9/10.

Run the actual backward process on one complete H_j, j<=m. Immediately
before a step alpha, let y_mass=1 plus the actual mass strictly after
it within that child. A step of level ell has its full right H_ell
after it, so

    alpha/y_mass=(1+d_ell)/y_mass
        <=c-2+1/y_mass<3/2.

For x=alpha/y_mass, log(1+x)>=2*x/(2+x). Thus

    x<=(c/2+1/(2*y_mass))*log(1+x).

Moreover

    log(1+x)/y_mass <= x/y_mass
       =(1+x)*(1/y_mass-1/(y_mass+alpha))
       <=(5/2)*(1/y_mass-1/(y_mass+alpha)).

Both expressions telescope in the exact reverse order. This proves
Hharm_j=sum alpha/y_mass <= (c/2)*log M_j+5/4 without a flow or an
order-free model. The empty child is included.

Let the pre-update state have radius rho_loc and polar angle phi_pol,
and let theta be the slope angle actually added. Set gamma=phi_pol-theta.
It lies in[0,pi/2) in both branches. The uncapped root equation gives

    sin gamma=kappa*cos gamma
                 +kappa*(alpha-1)*R(theta)/rho_loc.

The cap test gives exactly this inequality with <= instead of equality
and R(theta)=C. Thus there is no omitted sign or branch in(46e).
The crude radius bound rho_loc>=y_mass/sqrt(2), already supplied by
p+q>=1, gives sin gamma<(11/2)*kappa<1/100. Therefore
cos gamma>eta_turn. Projection of the exact vector addition onto the
old radial direction then proves

    rho_new>=rho_loc+eta_turn*alpha,
    rho_loc>=eta_turn*y_mass

by induction starting from the endpoint radius sqrt(1+epsilon^2)>=1.
This induction is cap-inclusive.

For z=alpha*R(theta)/rho_loc, the exact polar-angle decrement delta is

    tan delta=z*sin gamma/(1+z*cos gamma).

The branch-independent inequality above gives sin gamma<=kappa*(1+z).
Since 1+z*cos gamma>=cos gamma*(1+z),

    delta<=kappa*z/cos gamma
      <=(C/eta_turn^2)*kappa*alpha/y_mass
      <=(101/100)*kappa*alpha/y_mass.

The post-update state has theta>=phi_new-kappa: for an uncapped state
this follows from arctan(kappa*nu/(1+nu))<=kappa; at a cap it follows
from y_new<=kappa*(u_new-C). Thus the inequality remains usable even
at a putative first cap. Summing the actual decrements gives

    theta>=theta_sp-kappa*((101/100)*Hharm_j+1)
      >theta_sp-1221/(625*theta_sp)>1/16.

The quantitative margins check: the bracket divided by m is less
than111/100 using j<=m, log c<9/10, c<121/50 and m>=1536; and
(23/16)*(22/16)=253/128>1221/625. Only now are caps ruled out on a
complete H_j. They are not ruled out on arbitrary preceding blocks.

Every slope processed on H_j therefore has q>1/32 and p+q>=1. Since
epsilon^(-1)-1>15,

    p+q/epsilon>47/32,
    P(H_j)+Q(H_j)/epsilon-C*S_j>=S_j/4.

This is a lower estimate on actual discrete outputs. At j=0 the
statement is weak equality, so no positive surplus is invented for
an empty child.

## 6. Attack of Section9A: arbitrary blocks and changing adjacent levels

Let lev_i be the level of selected checkpoint i. For i>=1 set
j_i=min(lev_(i-1),lev_i). At position t, the tree level is the exponent
of2 in t+1. Two positions of level at least j_i differ by at least
2^(j_i), and the final 2^(j_i)-1 positions before the later checkpoint
are exactly H_(j_i). The previous selected checkpoint is no later than
the position immediately before that child. Thus the trailing child
actually lies inside block_i, with unchanged enclosing parameters.

The backward construction processes this child first from the same
endpoint(epsilon,-1). Its contribution is exactly the one just bounded.
Earlier steps may have different angles or may cap. For every one of
them p+q/epsilon>=C, because C<A_floor. Hence

    P_i+Q_i/epsilon-C*s_i>=S_(j_i)/4.

The constant reserve A_floor-C>1/4 and Delta_C<1/4 now imply

    M_i^tr>=C*(s_i+1)+Delta_C*M_(j_i)=:D_i^cmp.

This retains the actual max in M_i^tr; it only lowers the denominator.
It does not replace M_i^tr by the much larger fixed A_sp. Inserting
this inequality into the exact payoff(43) gives Psi_low(46k).

For deletion, choose a globally minimum selected level j, and set
M=M_j, d=d_j, u=d/M, X=1+s>=M, Y=1+t>=M. The merged block mass plus
one is X+Y+d.

For a FIRST removal, the next comparison denominator is C*Y+Delta_C*M.
The required inequality is

    d*(X+Y+d)<=X*(C*Y+Delta_C*M).

Its difference increases in X and Y since C>u. At X=Y=M it is
M^2*(r_h-u*(2+u))>=0.

For an INTERIOR removal, both old comparison denominators use M_j.
The newly adjacent selected levels can be larger. Their new complete
trailing child is contained in the merged block, so its mass plus one
is at most X+Y+d. Consequently the NEW denominator is at most
r_h*(X+Y+d). It is therefore sufficient to compare

    d*r_h*(X+Y+d)
       <=(C*X+Delta_C*M)*(C*Y+Delta_C*M).

The X derivative of the difference is at least
r_h*M*(C-u)>0, and similarly for Y. At X=Y=M the difference is
r_h*M^2*(r_h-u*(2+u))>=0. This explicitly pays for every increase of
the new adjacent level. It never preserves the old P,Q or old M_j
for the new block.

For a LAST removal, the local selected-to-deleted ratio is at most

    d^2*(1+2*(s+b+t))/((s+1)^2*(1+2*t)).

It decreases in s,t. At their lower bounds it is
u^2*((4+2*u)*M-1)/(2*M-1), bounded by u^2*(3+2*u)<=1. The first
cross-multiplied slack is2*(1+u)*(M-1)>=0. The last comparison uses
u<=1/2. Ties in the minimum level do not affect any of these arguments.

Every remaining subset has its own recalculated comparison functional.
The deletions terminate at one checkpoint, where the Section9 bound
uses only d>=4, u<21/50 and X,Y>=M. It does not require C>=r_h there.
Thus the low-C proof closes the entire missing band, with the same
441/500 constant. There is no finite-count restriction hidden in it.

## 7. Boundary attacks, benchmark evidence, and final optima

The source's two-checkpoint test epsilon=kappa=1/2, b_0=b_1=10 and zero
ordinary mass correctly has squared true radius5*sigma^2/4 and actual
payoff250*sigma^2. Formula(23) gives625*sigma^2 in the required upper
direction. It does not use the invalid50*sigma^2 estimate from extending
the one-stage radius. Its full polyhedron caps x_2, not x_1.

The reordered local lists(A_*,1) and(1,A_*) in Section10 have the same
mass but respectively Q=q_*>0 and Q=0. Their written cap tests and
actual projections agree. Nothing in the deletion proofs equates these
outputs. A huge immediately capped step likewise has P=C*alpha,Q=0;
all inequalities used outside the complete-child surplus still hold.
These attacks distinguish the permitted arbitrary-prefix caps from the
impossible caps on a whole H_j in the small-C band.

For the benchmark I used fact04219554e41d9750, not a new recurrence
calculation. Its Section8 certifies every row of the table

    /opt/danus/runtime/projects/gd-exact-profile-20260926/research/audit_c78/input_interval_table.csv
    SHA256 2a7585bc0bade9f85461456c13d60bbe618f85c7cc816e1ddc5b4c1a1d611e68.

The ray enclosure is for the original B recurrence and normalization

row is below15910*D80, lambda^10=3363+2378*sqrt(2)>6725,
sqrt(15910)<127, and15918/6725<237/100. No different benchmark,
balanced-only dominant recurrence, or approximate phase profile is used.

The finite physical mass formula is

    (1+2*S_m)/lambda^m
      =2*v_h*(1+tau/m)^m+2*(1-v_h)*(2/lambda)^m-lambda^(-m).

The stated bounds v_h>255/256, lambda^(-m)<1/256 and
(1+tau/m)^m>=1+tau+tau^2/4 give a value greater than5/2 at tau=1/4,
and greater than12/5 throughout[1/5,1/4]. Combined with Z_1<237/100
and B_(2^m-1)/lambda^m<=Z_1, they give respectively237/250 and79/80.
All arithmetic here is a written symbolic comparison of the displayed
constants; no arithmetic program or interval-table replay was executed.

For k=0, the MAIN hinge gives exactly H_base. The SOURCE half-weighted
hinge gives exactly1/[2(1+S_m)], whose ratio to H_base is
1-1/(2*M_m). Since M_m>=2^m>=32, this ratio is at least63/64>9801/10000.
Thus every nonempty source selection lies below its own k=0 member,
not merely below Huber. Every nonempty main selection lies below
Huber by the uniform441/500 factor. These uniform pointwise bounds
establish the stated suprema even when the parameter pair and selection
depend on the horizon. The source and main factors are never mixed.

For each fixed tau, v_h tends1 and the displayed physical mass divided
by lambda^m tends2*exp(tau). The accepted ray tendsZ_1. Exact family
identities have already been established at every m, so their scaled
limits are Z_1/(2*exp(tau)); for the source-only value its ratio to
Huber tends1. No exchange of a parameter supremum with a limit occurs.

# Consolidated notation recommendation; frozen source unchanged

Source: bfff4aa387a9d818, 53449 bytes,
SHA256 bb0fb6c31e6f2711493fec2558266e896b5c0c42a8f70c76e83c4bcf7d767ca2.

The independent analytical audit found no substantive repair needed
for the assigned L=1 two-family theorem. This file records the sole
editorial clarification and does not propose a changed theorem or
authorize another review/submission round.

In Section1, E is displayed with denominator dist(x_0,argmin f)^2 and
is explicitly introduced at advertised smoothness1. The next scaling
sentence says that multiplying f by L and using h_t/L preserves E.
Section12 repeats the scaling claim. Taken literally with the displayed
L-free definition, the numerator and hence E are multiplied by L.

Make the intended normalized extension explicit:

    E_L(f,h)=2(f(x_n)-min f)/(L*dist(x_0,argmin f)^2),
    x_(t+1)=x_t-(h_t/L)*grad f(x_t).

Then use the sentence:

    For any L>0, replacing the constructed function f by L*f and
    using the physical update h_t/L preserves every point and gives
    E_L(L*f,h)=E_1(f,h).

With the original L=1 iteration notation instead, the literal identity
is E_1(L*f,h/L)=L*E_1(f,h), where the formula for E_1 is viewed just as
a raw gap/distance ratio. For example the candidate's own main k=0
Huber member has E_1(f,h)=1/(1+2*S_m); doubling the function and halving
the physical updates preserves the points and doubles that raw ratio.
Division by the advertised L restores the invariant normalized payoff.

This clarification is outside the fixed-L=1 comparisons. It requires
no change to the parameters, eligibility thresholds, static functions,
closest radii, finite constants, or family optima. No source file was
edited and no repaired candidate was subjected to another audit.
