# C78. Explicit selected-ray separation conditional on an actual primitive seed

Status: COMPLETE JOINT PROOF CANDIDATE, UNVERIFIED. Seventh max3 launch,
second and final authorized round, cumulative round 17. The original
unrestricted target is OPEN. No actual seed is constructed here.
No fact_submit, recurrence replay, seed search, optimizer, or installation
was performed. The four-run DP allocation remains CLOSED.

This joint document reproduces the scalar enclosure proof and the complete
original-function composition algebra, with a direct rate and a sharper
finite rational comparison. It imports no unverified intermediate claim
as a fact. The only non-elementary external input is the explicitly matched
balanced primitive scalar theorem in Section 2. The finite interval table
and its enclosing algorithm remain part of the candidate to be checked.

Sections 1--8 reproduce the audited scalar component, retaining its equation
numbers. Sections 10--12 reproduce and audit the full-function component;
its integer equation labels (4)--(11) are local to that component and differ
from the scalar labels such as (4.1). The resulting conditional theorem is
stated last in Section 13. Section 14 records validation and unresolved work.

## 1. Definitions and exact scope

Use the exact recurrences in accepted facts 0be8b54e0a33381c and
b98bb807d514e80d. Set

    P_n = 1+S_circle(n),  B_n = 1+2S_bullet(n),  P_0=B_0=1,
    Omega(a,b) = (a+b+sqrt((a+b)^2+4ab))/2,
    G(a,d) = 2a+d/2+sqrt(d^2+8ad)/2,
    P_n = max_(k+l=n-1) Omega(P_k,P_l),
    B_n = max_(k+l=n-1) G(P_k,B_l).

All maxima run over nonnegative integer k,l, with EVERY original split
retained. Write lambda=1+sqrt(2), rho=log_2(lambda), s=sqrt(lambda), and
q_n=sqrt(B_n). In this document q_n is a square-root mass, not an objective
coefficient. To discuss leaf counts, also write U_N=P_(N-1) and V_N=B_(N-1)
for N>=1, and U_0=0. The source paper's W_N equals V_N, not the project's
worst-case functional W_n(h).

The target scalar claim is

    Z_b = lim_(j->infinity) lambda^(-j) B_(b*2^j-1),
    Z_15 < (773/100) Z_3.                                  (E5)

The proof below derives existence of these limits as well as their enclosure.
It does not need the source's qualitative objective phase theorem.

The stronger conjecture B_(2n)<=lambda B_n for all n is NOT used. The
argument allows a rigorously bounded positive defect beyond the finite base.
The source's global one-pivot support envelope and the zero-prefix loophole
in C75 are not used.

## 2. Precisely matched literature input

The only non-elementary external input is balanced optimality of the
SYMMETRIC scalar recurrence, as proved in Corollary 4.2 of:

Yu Liu, Kang Chen, Rujun Jiang, Tianyu Wang (2026), *Optimal Recursive
Composition and Dyadic Phase Laws for Gradient Descent with Predetermined
Stepsizes*, arXiv:2609.11788v1, https://arxiv.org/html/2609.11788v1 .

Its complete statement used here is: for U_0=0, U_1=1 and
U_N=max_(1<=m<N) Omega(U_m,U_(N-m)), the sequence is increasing and

    U_(2a)=lambda U_a,
    U_(2a+1)=Omega(U_a,U_(a+1)),  a>=0.                    (2.1)

The second identity includes U_1=Omega(U_0,U_1). The source defines its
kernel K by exactly our Omega. Proposition 2.5 and identical initial values
match its U_N to P_(N-1), by induction. No assertion about equality of ambient
primitive and s-composable certificate classes is imported. In particular,
balanced splitting is NEVER assumed for the asymmetric B recurrence.

The exact paper was downloaded in the prior round; its local text is
downloads/liu_chen_jiang_wang_2609.11788v1.txt. Section 2, Theorem 3.1 and
its proof, Theorem 4.1/Corollary 4.2 and their proofs, and the adjacent-ratio
proof in Appendix C.2 were read. Matlas returned unrelated statements;
this reference is grounded directly in the primary arXiv paper, not falsely
represented as a Matlas hit. No external_refs submission is made because no
fact_submit is authorized. The subsequent scalar estimates are proved here.

## 3. Elementary growth and adjacent-value bounds

First, 1<rho<4/3: lambda>2 and lambda^3=7+5sqrt(2)<16. Thus s>3/2
and lambda^(3/2)<4.

For completeness, Omega(x,y)<=(x^(1/rho)+y^(1/rho))^rho for x,y>=0.
For positive x,y, put A=x^(1/rho), C=y^(1/rho), and T=(A+C)^rho.
Let D(t)=(1+t)^rho-1. For g(z)=log D(exp z), differentiation gives

    g''(z) = rho*t*(1+t)^(rho-2)
             *((1+t)^rho-1-rho*t)/D(t)^2 > 0,  t=exp z.

The last bracket has value zero at t=0 and strictly positive derivative for
t>0. Hence D(t)D(1/t)>=D(1)^2=(lambda-1)^2=2. It follows that
(T-x)(T-y)>=2xy, or T^2-(x+y)T-xy>=0. Since T>=x+y and Omega is the
positive root of that polynomial, T>=Omega(x,y). Zero arguments follow by
continuity. Induction in the full primitive recurrence gives U_N<=N^rho.
Balanced dyadic joins give U_(2^j)=lambda^j; monotonicity then yields

    N^rho/lambda <= U_N <= N^rho,  N>=1.                  (3.1)

Also G(a,d)>=Omega(a,d) for a,d>=0. Indeed, after multiplying their
difference by two, the required inequality is
3a+sqrt(d^2+8ad)>=sqrt(a^2+6ad+d^2); squaring the nonnegative sides
leaves 8a^2+2ad+6a sqrt(d^2+8ad)>=0. Induction gives V_N>=U_N.

Here is the adjacent primitive estimate derived from (2.1). Let
R_N=U_(N+1)/U_N, Delta(r)=sqrt(r^2+6r+1), and

    T0(r)=(1+r+Delta(r))/(2lambda),
    T1(r)=lambda*(Delta(r)-r-1)/2.

Then R_(2N)=T0(R_N), R_(2N+1)=T1(R_N). Both maps fix 1 and have positive
derivative at most 1/2 on [1,lambda]: their derivatives are
(1+(r+3)/Delta(r))/(2lambda) and lambda*((r+3)/Delta(r)-1)/2;
(r+3)/Delta(r) has derivative -8/Delta(r)^3. Starting at R_1=lambda,
binary induction gives

    0<R_N-1 <= (lambda-1)/2^floor(log_2 N) <= 2sqrt(2)/N.

Using (3.1), U_(N+1)-U_N<=2sqrt(2) N^(rho-1). With U_0=0 this implies
U_m-U_(m-1)<=2sqrt(2) m^(rho-1) for every m>=1.

The full dominant recurrence now gives

    0 < V_(N+1)-V_N <= 8sqrt(2) N^(rho-1).                (3.2)

For the upper bound, take a maximizing prefix m for V_(N+1). If m>=2,
compare it to the admissible split (m-1,N+1-m) for V_N. If m=1, use
G(U_0,V_N)=V_N. The derivative of G with respect to its first variable is
2+2d/sqrt(d^2+8ad)<=4. Since m<=N, the bound follows. Strict increase
follows by choosing prefix 1 in the recurrence.

Consequently, with Delta_n=q_(n+1)-q_n and barq_n=(q_n+q_(n+1))/2,

    Delta_n <= 4sqrt(2lambda) (n+1)^(rho/2-1),
    delta_n := Delta_n^2/(4barq_n)
             <= 8lambda^(3/2) (n+1)^(rho/2-2)
             < 32 n^(-4/3),  n>=1.                       (3.3)

The denominator bound used here is q_n>=sqrt(U_(n+1))
>=(n+1)^(rho/2)/sqrt(lambda). No concavity of q_n is asserted.

## 4. Exact merge inequality and contraction along maximizing trees

Let a,b,d>0, write Q=sqrt(d), u=T_b(Q), t=T_a(u), where

    T_a(v)=(v+sqrt(v^2+8a))/2,  T_a(v)^2=G(a,v^2).

If a<=d, then

    G(a,G(b,d)) < G(Omega(a,b),d).                        (4.1)

To prove this, u>Q and Q(u+Q)>2Q^2>=2a. Since t is the positive
solution of t^2-ut=2a, this implies t<u+Q. Set r=t/u and c=a+br.
The polynomial defining Omega gives

    c^2-(a+b)c-ab = ab*(t-u-Q)/u < 0.

Here r(r-1)=2a/u^2 and 2b/u^2=1-Q/u were substituted to obtain the
identity. Thus c<Omega(a,b). Finally,
t^2-Qt=2a+(u-Q)t=2(a+bt/u)=2c<2Omega(a,b), so T_Omega(a,b)(Q)>t.
Squaring proves (4.1).

Consider two successive right-continuation joins in ANY maximizing B tree.
Let their primitive leaf counts be m1,m2 and the remaining leaf count be R.
Their values are a=U_m1, b=U_m2, d=V_R. The same total leaf count permits
merging the two primitive blocks: Omega(a,b)<=U_(m1+m2). Therefore (4.1)
would strictly improve the maximizing tree if a<=d. Necessarily a>d.
By (3.1) and V_R>=U_R,

    m1^rho >= a > d >= R^rho/lambda,
    R < 2m1,
    R < (2/3)(m1+m2+R).                                  (4.2)

Thus the remaining leaf count decreases by a factor less than 2/3 every
TWO edges along a maximizing right spine. This is an optimality argument
for the exact full DP, not a restriction imposed on allowed splits.

## 5. A one-step rounding defect inequality

Extend P_-1=0. Let v(x) be linear interpolation of q_n on nonnegative
integers: v(n+theta)=(1-theta)q_n+theta q_(n+1), 0<=theta<=1. Define

    e_t = max(0, q_t/s - v(t/2)),  t>=0.                  (5.1)

For a maximizing split k+l=t-1, the claim is

    e_t <= e_l                              if t is even,
    e_t <= e_l+delta_floor(t/2)              if t is odd. (5.2)

Only t>4095 will require the odd formula, so delta_0 need not be bounded.

The primitive bounds from (2.1) are

    P_(2a+1)/lambda=P_a,
    P_(2a)/lambda<= (P_(a-1)+P_a)/2.                      (5.3)

For a>=1 the second follows from
Omega(x,y)<=lambda(x+y)/2, obtained using 4xy<=(x+y)^2. For a=0,
P_0/lambda<=1/2=(P_-1+P_0)/2 because lambda>2.

The exact dominant split inequality can be written without a radical:

    2P_i+q_j q_m <= q_m^2,  i+j=m-1.                    (5.4)

It is equivalent to T_(P_i)(q_j)<=q_m. We additionally permit the formal
boundary i=-1,j=m, where (5.4) is equality.

If t=2n and k=2a+1,l=2b, then a+b=n-1. Use (5.4) directly. If
k=2a,l=2b+1, then a+b=n-1; average (5.4) at the two splits
(a-1,b+1) and (a,b), both with parent n. Using (5.3) gives a root at
most q_n in either case before adding the continuation defect e_l.

For t=2n+1, set Qbar=(q_n+q_(n+1))/2. If k=2a+1,l=2b+1,
then a+b=n-1. Average the inequalities with parent/split
(n;a,b) and (n+1;a,b+1). For u=P_a and w=(q_b+q_(b+1))/2, this gives

    2u+Qbar*w-Qbar^2 <= (Delta_n^2-Delta_n*Delta_b)/4
                       <= Delta_n^2/4.

If k=2a,l=2b, then a+b=n. Average parent/split
(n;a-1,b) and (n+1;a,b). With u=(P_(a-1)+P_a)/2 and w=q_b,
the corresponding upper bound is Delta_n^2/4. Boundary a=0 uses the
formal equality in (5.4). In both cases w<=Qbar. The derivative of
y^2-wy-2u for y>=Qbar is at least Qbar. Its positive root is therefore
at most Qbar+Delta_n^2/(4Qbar)=Qbar+delta_n.

Finally, T_a is increasing and 1-Lipschitz in its nonnegative argument:
T_a'(v)=(1+v/sqrt(v^2+8a))/2<=1. Homogeneity and
q_l/s<=v(l/2)+e_l imply

    q_t/s = T_(P_k/lambda)(q_l/s)
           <= T_(P_k/lambda)(v(l/2))+e_l.

Combining this with the preceding parity cases proves (5.2).

## 6. The exact finite base and the resulting uniform error

The saved interval calculation checks the following FINITE certificate:

    e_t=0 for every integer 0<=t<=4095.                  (6.1)

The integer tests and reproducibility information are given in section 8.
Assuming this explicit finite certificate, follow a maximizing right spine
in (5.2) until its index is at most 4095. For visited indices t_i>4095,
put N_i=t_i+1 and n_i=floor(t_i/2)>=2048. By (4.2), whenever i and i+2
are both visited, N_i>(3/2)N_(i+2). Consequently

    n_i >= (N_i-2)/2 > (3/4)N_(i+2)-1
        >= (3/2)n_(i+2)-1/4 > (4/3)n_(i+2).

The last step uses n_(i+2)>=2048. Split the visited nodes into the two
parities of their spine position. Applying (3.3) backward from the last
node of each parity yields

    e_t <= sum_i delta_(n_i)
        < 64*2048^(-4/3)/(1-(3/4)^(4/3))
        < 256*2048^(-4/3)
        < 1/100.                                        (6.2)

For the final exact comparison, 2048^4=17592186044416 exceeds
25600^3=16777216000000. Thus (6.2) holds at every t, with (6.1) handling
the finite base. In particular,

    q_(2n) <= s*(q_n+1/100) for every n>=0.              (6.3)

This is the required uniform estimate. The unproved zero-defect shifted
doubling conjecture has been replaced by an explicit permitted defect.

## 7. Uniform ray enclosure and the oriented comparison

Directly from the original recurrences,

    P_(2n+1)>=lambda P_n,  B_(2n+1)>=lambda B_n.         (7.1)

The primitive bound follows from the balanced join of two copies. For the
dominant bound, B_1=4>=lambda B_0. Induct on n and double both child leaf
counts in any maximizing split of B_n; homogeneity of G and the previous
primitive/dominant lower bounds give (7.1). Therefore the sequence
lambda^(-j)B_(b2^j-1) is increasing for every integer b>=1.

Iterating (6.3) gives

    s^(-r)q_(2^r N)
      <= q_N+(1/100)*sum_(i=0)^(r-1)s^(-i)
      < q_N+3/100,                                     (7.2)

because s>3/2. Monotonicity B_(2^r N-1)<=B_(2^r N) bounds each increasing
ray, proving existence of Z_b without invoking a qualitative phase limit.
Applying (7.2) at N=b2^j yields the uniform enclosure

    lambda^(-j)B_(b2^j-1)
      <= Z_b
      <= lambda^(-j)(sqrt(B_(b2^j))+3/100)^2             (7.3)

for EVERY integer b>=1 and j>=0.

Choose upper ray b=15,j=8 (so the upper finite index is 3840) and lower
ray b=3,j=10 (finite index 3071). These two depths are used for the proved
enclosure; the discovery screen separately compared SAME-j ray ratios.
The finite certificate shows B_3840<90000, hence

    (sqrt(B_3840)+3/100)^2 < B_3840+19.

It also gives the exact strict inequality

    lambda^2*(B_3840+19) < (773/100)*B_3071.             (7.4)

Since 10-8=2, (7.3) and (7.4) establish the proposed E5 inequality,

    Z_15/Z_3 < lambda^2*(B_3840+19)/B_3071 < 773/100.    (7.5)

The computed rational upper quotient in (7.5) is approximately
7.719573399222938; the displayed exact integer slack in section 8 is the
certificate, not this decimal. In fact the same endpoints certify the
slightly stronger comparison

    Z_15/Z_3 < 193/25 = 7.72.                            (7.6)

## 8. Finite computation, integer certificate, and source preservation

Public ledger: research/max3_phase_ratio_discovery_budget.json.

1. Run 1: n<=31, binary64, all original F/G splits, small calibration.
2. Run 2: n<=4095, binary64, all original F/G splits, same-j and adjacent rays.
3. Run 3: n<=63, Decimal with 80 significant digits, independently evaluated
   using the ORIGINAL phi/psi and S_circle/S_bullet transformations.
4. Run 4: n<=4095, EXACT integer interval endpoints on denominator D=2^80,
   all original splits, integer square roots rounded outward.

The source for runs 1--3 is research/c76_phase_dp.py. The source for run 4
is research/c77_interval_dp.py. No NumPy was present and none was installed.
Only the existing Python standard library was used, in one process.

Here is the enclosing arithmetic for run 4. If a,b are endpoint numerators
on denominator D, let

    R_Omega=(a+b)^2+4ab, c_Omega=a+b,
    R_G=b^2+8ab,         c_G=4a+b.

For a lower endpoint, return floor((c+floor(sqrt(R)))/2); for an upper
endpoint, return ceil((c+ceil(sqrt(R)))/2). The result is again an integer
numerator on denominator D. Starting with exact D for P_0 and B_0, apply
these formulas to lower endpoints and upper endpoints separately, and
maximize each over ALL original k=0,...,n-1. Positivity and monotonicity
of Omega,G prove by induction that each stored rational interval encloses
the exact recurrence value. Integer sqrt is checked by its defining
r^2<=R<(r+1)^2 condition. No original split is pruned and no computed
argmax is assumed exact. All possible maximizing splits are retained as
those whose upper bound is at least the largest lower bound.

The complete table is research/c77_dp_run04.csv, SHA256
2a7585bc0bade9f85461456c13d60bbe618f85c7cc816e1ddc5b4c1a1d611e68.
Its largest interval widths for P and B are below 2.768e-20 and 5.920e-20,
respectively. These widths are diagnostics, not additional hypotheses.

The exact saved-table postprocessor research/c77_postprocess_certificate.py
does NOT evaluate the recurrence again. It encloses q_i by the floor/ceiling
integer square roots of D times the saved B endpoint numerators. With

    D = 1208925819614629174706176,
    s_lower_numerator = 1878397475050142397719609,
    lambda_squared_upper_numerator = 7046136038847924384392699,

it checks, for all fine indices t=0,...,4095, positivity of

    s_lower_numerator*q_lower_(t/2) - D*q_upper_t
                                                    (t even),
    s_lower_numerator*(q_lower_floor(t/2)+q_lower_(floor(t/2)+1))
                          -2D*q_upper_t             (t odd).

The minimum positive slack is
33709536743225496543599832619768805273186776891, at t=4094.
All 4096 slacks, not just this minimum, are retained in
research/c77_finite_base_slacks.csv, SHA256
dfeb3c318e08fba6a889ff78154cf7b762029b86ffa73b64d20fd6f99aa9fb73.
This is the finite certificate (6.1).

For (7.4), the saved endpoint numerators are

    B_3071 lower = 77954524439662110401113475160,
    B_3840 upper = 103225412920462918384305104511.

Writing these as L and H, and the lambda-squared upper numerator as A,
the integer cross-multiplication gives

    773*L*D - 100*A*(H+19D)
      = 98261575859406148591490057041393620527049740462983180 > 0.

For (7.6), replace 773 by 772 in that expression. Its exact positive value is
4020338508518990670593580566018450094572713188395020. The separate saved
addendum research/c77_stronger_threshold.json retains this comparison and
the P_4 upper endpoint, which also proves P_4<77/10. Thus the new sufficient
capacity threshold 193/25 exceeds the finite primitive baseline by more
than 1/50. These deductions use the existing table, with zero recurrence
evaluations; their negligible measured postprocessing is charged in the ledger.

The certificate JSON is research/c77_finite_certificate.json. Every run
and postprocessing source/output hash is in the public ledger. Numerical
floating-point ties were retained in the earlier runs; mathematical ties
are not inferred from them. The independent 80-digit original recurrence
agreed with binary64 to below 4e-16 relative error on its prefix. The exact
interval run independently encloses the full original recurrence and is
the only finite arithmetic used in (6.1) and (7.4).

## 9. A sharper rational endpoint comparison and a direct convergence rate

Continue the scalar derivation in Sections 1--8. All arguments in those
sections are reproduced in this joint candidate; C77 is not a predecessor
fact. Define the following fixed positive integers from its saved certificate:

    D80 = 1208925819614629174706176,
    L3071 = 77954524439662110401113475160,
    H3840 = 103225412920462918384305104511,
    Lambda2up = 7046136038847924384392699.

They have the enclosing meanings

    L3071/D80 <= B_3071,
    B_3840 <= H3840/D80,
    lambda^2 <= Lambda2up/D80.

Set

    Theta = Lambda2up*(H3840+19D80)/(D80*L3071).

The two exact integer comparisons needed below are

    19299*L3071*D80-2500*Lambda2up*(H3840+19D80)
      = 6267225362087608843943037675086081931840802435287340 > 0,
    L3071-64000D80 = 583271984325843219918211160 > 0.

Thus

    Theta < 19299/2500 = 193/25-1/2500,
    B_3071 > 64000.                                      (9.1)

These are two fixed integer checks using the saved endpoints, not another
recurrence computation. Their output is in the public file
research/c78_fixed_endpoint_audit.json. Section 7 now gives

    Z_15/Z_3 < lambda^2*(B_3840+19)/B_3071
              <= Theta < 19299/2500.                     (9.2)

Here the initial strict inequality follows from B_3840<90000 and the
strict bound (sqrt(B_3840)+3/100)^2<B_3840+19. Both positive ray limits
exist by Section 7. No theorem about a qualitative objective phase or its
nonconstancy enters this argument.

An elementary global upper bound makes the convergence error explicit:

    B_n <= 4P_n <= 4(n+1)^rho,  n>=0.                    (9.3)

Indeed, for a,b>=0,

    G(a,4b)=2a+2b+2sqrt(b^2+2ab)
           <=2a+2b+2sqrt(a^2+6ab+b^2)=4Omega(a,b).

The initial bound B_0<=4P_0 holds. If it holds at every smaller index,
then each ORIGINAL dominant split obeys

    G(P_k,B_l)<=G(P_k,4P_l)<=4Omega(P_k,P_l)<=4P_n

when k+l=n-1. Maximizing gives the first assertion of (9.3). The second
was proved in Section 3. Balanced splitting is not assumed for B.

For every positive integer b and integer j>=0, put M=b*2^j and

    E(b,j)=8sqrt(2)*b^(rho-1)*2^(-j)
           +(3/25)*(b+1)^(rho/2)*lambda^(-j/2)
           +(9/10000)*lambda^(-j).

Then the enclosure in (7.3) implies the explicit bound

    0 <= Z_b-lambda^(-j)B_(M-1) <= E(b,j).               (9.4)

To prove it, subtract the lower endpoint in (7.3) from its upper endpoint:

    Z_b-lambda^(-j)B_(M-1)
      <=lambda^(-j)[B_M-B_(M-1)+(3/50)sqrt(B_M)+9/10000].

The adjacent bound (3.2) gives B_M-B_(M-1)<=8sqrt(2)M^(rho-1).
By (9.3), sqrt(B_M)<=2(M+1)^(rho/2). Substitution, lambda=2^rho,
and b+2^(-j)<=b+1 give exactly (9.4).

If d is a fixed nonnegative integer and M-1-d>=0, monotonicity and the
same adjacent bound give

    0 <= B_(M-1)-B_(M-1-d) <= 8sqrt(2)*d*M^(rho-1).

Every increment on the left has its index at most M-1, which justifies
using the displayed common upper bound. Consequently

    0 <= Z_b-lambda^(-j)B_(b*2^j-1-d)
      <= E(b,j)+8sqrt(2)*d*b^(rho-1)*2^(-j).             (9.5)

In particular this shifted sequence converges to the SAME Z_b. This
supplies the precise bounded-offset step needed for actual substitution
without importing the source objective-phase theorem. For b ranging over
a finite set and d bounded by a fixed integer, the explicit bound is
uniform. No conclusion that the error is zero at a finite index is made.

The next three sections reproduce and audit the full original-function
seed and composition identities. They are part of this one candidate,
not uses of the unverified C75 draft as a mathematical building block.

## 10. Full original-space seed condition

For a nonnegative word h of length n, W_n(h) denotes the supremum of
2(f(x_n)-f(x_star))/(L||x_0-x_star||^2), over every finite-dimensional
Euclidean space, every convex differentiable f with L-Lipschitz gradient
and a minimizer x_star, and every x_0!=x_star, under
x_(i+1)=x_i-(h_i/L)grad f(x_i). Here L>0, and U_n^ZJ=1/B_n.

Normalize L=1, translate a selected minimizer to zero, and subtract f_star
so that its value is also zero. For a finite
nonnegative word h of length r, let s=sum h_i, A=1+s, and retain every
formal gradient g_0,...,g_r, including gradients at zero steps. Put

    x_i=x_0-sum_(t<i)h_t g_t,
    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2,
    V_h=2sum_(i<r)h_i(f_i-f_r)
          +sum_(i<r)h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2-s(s+1)||g_r||^2.

Every actual smooth convex trajectory has Q_ij<=0. We call the seed
primitive when V_h>=0 on every such trajectory in every finite dimension.
A concrete sufficient certificate retains ALL ordered finite multipliers
Lambda_ij>=0, i!=j, and Lambda_ii=0. Let e be the all-ones column, and set

    T_ii=1, T_it=h_t for t<i, T_it=0 for t>i,
    L_flow=diag(Lambda e)-Lambda,
    omega=(-2h_0,...,-2h_(r-1),2s)^T,
    D_prim=diag(h_0(2-h_0),...,h_(r-1)(2-h_(r-1)),-s(s+2)).

The feasibility conditions are

    L_flow^T e=omega,
    M_prim=D_prim+(L_flow T+T^T L_flow^T)/2 >=0.            (4)

Indeed substituting the GD equations and collecting function coefficients
and gradient inner products gives the exact identity

    V_h+sum_(i!=j)Lambda_ij Q_ij=<M_prim,Gram(g_0,...,g_r)>.

To audit the matrix formula, the position terms of sum Lambda Q contribute
sym(L_flow(T-I)); the squared-difference terms contribute
sym(L_flow)-diag(omega)/2. Adding V_h's diagonal yields (4).
Thus (4) proves the required actual primitive inequality. It has no support,
rank, symmetry, quotient, or terminal-edge restriction.

Adding optimum multipliers cannot supply a missing positive x_0 diagonal:
that diagonal is zero. Its mixed coefficient with g_i is
-Lambda_(i,star), so PSD forces each Lambda_(i,star)=0. Summing the finite
function-flow equations then forces sum_i Lambda_(star,i)=0, and their
nonnegativity makes all these multipliers zero. Therefore the original
formal space (x_0,g_0,...,g_r) is retained, with its x_0 row zero.
No equality between formal gradients at duplicated query positions is
imposed. For the implication used here, sufficiency of (4) is enough;
no unproved dual attainment is invoked.

## 11. Complete primitive and dominant composition identities

Primitive join: let a,b be primitive, with capacities A=1+sum a and
C=1+sum b. Choose d>0 by d(A+C+d)=AC, put T_mass=A+C+d and
kappa=(T_mass/C)^2, and use the physical word h=(a,1+d,b).
Let p be its bridge query, z its terminal query, and j range over the
nonterminal queries of b. Direct expansion gives

    V_h=V_a+kappa V_b-(kappa-1) E_PP,
    E_PP=sum_j b_j Q_(j,p)+Q_(z,p)+d Q_(p,z)
                                      +sum_j b_j Q_(j,z).       (5)

For a coefficient audit, the value part of E_PP is
2sum b_j f_j+(d-C)f_p+(2-C-d)f_z. Its gradient part is

    sum_j b_j(1-b_j)||g_j||^2
       +[(C-d)/2-d^2]||g_p||^2+(C+d)||g_z||^2/2.

The remaining coefficient identities are
(kappa-1)(C-d)=2(A+d), kappa d^2=A^2, and kappa C^2=T_mass^2.
They follow from d T_mass=AC. Also 0<d<C and kappa>1, so all
added multipliers are nonnegative. If the child certificates have arbitrary
PSD remainders, the full parent remainder is their original-coordinate sum
M_a+kappa M_b, embedded on the actual child-gradient indices. The resulting
capacity is exactly Omega(A,C).
The physical bridge obeys 1+d=phi(A-1,C-1), as required by the recurrences in Section 1.

Dominant join: for a word h with terminal z, put D=2sum h+1. For
nonnegative weights u_i summing to D define

    J_(h,u)=||x_0||^2+2sum_i u_i Q_(i,star)-2D f_z
                                  -||x_0-sum_i u_i g_i||^2.    (6)

The empty word has u=1 and J=0. A primitive word is dominant with
u=(h,s+1), since direct expansion gives J=V_h. In general J>=0 implies
2f_z<=||x_0||^2/D on every actual trajectory.

Let a be a primitive left child of capacity A and b a dominant right child
with mass D and weights v. Define

    mu=2A/D,  zeta=(1+sqrt(1+8A/D))/2,
    gamma=A+1,  beta=1+A/zeta.

Use the physical parent word (a,beta,b), and weights u=(a,gamma,zeta v).
Their mass is G(A,D)=D zeta^2=2A+zeta D=2sum(parent)+1. With p the
bridge query, all right-child queries i>p, including its terminal, give

    J_parent=V_a+zeta^2 J_right
                         -2mu sum_(i>p)v_i Q_(i,p).             (7)

The physical bridge is exactly beta=psi(A-1,(D-1)/2); multiply
zeta^2-zeta=2A/D by D/zeta to check this against the radical in psi.

This is an equality on the full original formal space. Function
coefficients use mu D=2A. Expanding all squares and the GD bridge uses

    (A-1)A+mu D+gamma=gamma^2,
    zeta^2=zeta+mu,  zeta^2 beta=mu+gamma zeta;

these cancel the mixed terms and give (7). The child remainder is pulled
back from the actual right-child starting position x_p-beta g_p; hence the
original x_0 and all inherited gradients remain in the certificate.
For an additional coefficient check, put y=x_p-beta g_p,
xi_i=x_i-y on the right child, and R_v=sum_(i>p)v_i g_i. Expanding
J_parent-V_a-zeta^2 J_right gives precisely

    -2mu sum_i v_i(f_i-f_p)
    -mu sum_i v_i||g_i||^2-mu D||g_p||^2
    +2mu sum_i v_i<g_i,xi_i>-2mu(beta-1)<R_v,g_p>.

Indeed the left-prefix displacement cancels between the position terms
and the final square; its remaining diagonal is V_a's diagonal. The
bridge diagonal difference is -2A=-mu D. The right-child local quadratic
has coefficient zeta-zeta^2=-mu, and the remaining bridge cross coefficient
is 2zeta(beta-gamma)=-2mu(beta-1). As

    Q_(i,p)=f_i-f_p+(beta-1)<g_i,g_p>-<g_i,xi_i>
                         +(||g_i||^2+||g_p||^2)/2,

the displayed expansion is exactly the last term in (7). This also checks
the endpoint i=z and the empty-right-child case without deleting either.
All extra interpolation coefficients are nonnegative. Induction on the
actual tree supplies a full PSD objective certificate: divide (6), after
inserting its nonnegative interpolation representation, by D. Its PSD
remainder is the retained final square plus the embedded child remainders,
all divided by D. This is an all-function objective bound, not a bound on
a quadratic, projection or temporal subclass.

## 12. The exact physical substitution and the untouched dominant leaf

Fix an ACTUAL seed a satisfying Section 10, of length r=m-1 and capacity
A=1+sum a>=1. For each n choose a fully expanded maximizing dominant tree
for B_n in Section 1, for example the smallest maximizing split at each node.
The unique dominant right spine ends at one dominant empty leaf. Every
other leaf is primitive: there are exactly n of them, by induction on those scalar recurrences.

Replace each primitive empty leaf by the actual word a, retaining every
original node, and recompute each physical bridge by (5) or (7). Leave the
last dominant empty leaf empty, with mass 1. Call the resulting word H_n
and its true mass D_n=2sum H_n+1. There are n old bridge steps and nr
inserted seed steps. Thus

    length(H_n)=mn,  W_(mn)(H_n)<=1/D_n.                       (8)

Every primitive branch capacity is multiplied by A, by homogeneity of
Omega. If the last dominant leaf were ALSO changed from mass 1 to A in
an auxiliary scalar evaluation, homogeneity of G would make the root
A B_n. This last change is scalar only, not an additional physical block.
The square-root join is

    sqrt(G(a,d))=(sqrt(d)+sqrt(d+8a))/2.

Fact 0be8b54e0a33381c proves that it is increasing and 1-Lipschitz in
sqrt(d). Propagating the final-leaf difference along the whole spine gives

    sqrt(A B_n)-(sqrt(A)-1)<=sqrt(D_n)<=sqrt(A B_n).            (9)

Writing c_A=sqrt(A)-1 and ell_n=c_A/sqrt(A B_n), this implies

    (1-ell_n)^2<=D_n/(A B_n)<=1,
    0<=A B_n-D_n<=2c_A sqrt(A B_n)-c_A^2.                    (10)

The lower square-root bound is positive because B_n>=1. The accepted
bound B_n>=2n+1 already makes ell_n tend to zero; the elementary lower bound
B_n>=P_n>=(n+1)^rho/lambda in Section 3 also gives
ell_n=O(n^(-rho/2)). The bound is independent of tree depth,
split ties and multiplier choices.

In fact (8) is equality. The actual nonnegative word H_n has sum
(D_n-1)/2. The one-dimensional Huber function in fact 0be8b54e0a33381c,
started at x_0=1 with threshold 1/D_n and minimizer 0, attains normalized
error 1/D_n on that entire word. Its initial distance is genuinely 1.
Together with the full upper certificate this proves, within the joint
candidate,

    W_(mn)(H_n)=1/D_n.                                      (11)

Restoring any L>0 by multiplying the normalized objective by L leaves
the specified h_i/L GD updates and the ratio 2gap/(L distance^2) unchanged.

## 13. The explicit selected-ray consequence, including a finite horizon

All assertions in this section are CONDITIONAL on an actual primitive
seed as specified in Section 10. The original problem requires existence
or a different unconditional route, and no such seed is supplied here.

Fix a nonnegative primitive seed of length r=m-1 and capacity A. Write
H_n and D_n for the actual substituted word and its mass from Section 12.
The full certificate and Huber witness in that section give

    W_(mn)(H_n)=1/D_n,
    (sqrt(A B_n)-(sqrt(A)-1))^2 <= D_n <= A B_n.          (13.1)

For every fixed positive integer b, let n_j=b*2^j-1. The true output
horizon is mn_j=mb*2^j-m. In (9.5), use mb in place of b and d=m-1
to obtain

    lambda^(-j)B_(mn_j) -> Z_(mb).                       (13.2)

Meanwhile (9.3) and (13.1) imply

    0<=lambda^(-j)(A B_(n_j)-D_(n_j))
      <=4(sqrt(A)-1)sqrt(A)*b^(rho/2)*lambda^(-j/2).

This tends to zero. Together with (9.4) and Z_b>=B_(b-1)>0 it proves

    B_(mn_j) W_(mn_j)(H_(n_j)) -> Z_(mb)/(A Z_b).        (13.3)

This is the exact comparison for each fixed integer ray, derived from
the scalar enclosure and actual unchanged final leaf. It is not an
assertion that an integer b optimizes a continuous phase. It does not
claim necessity for all possible subsequences or all possible seed uses.
It proves the selected-ray implication with no qualitative phase input.

For m=5 and b=3 the comparison is stronger than an eventual limit claim.
Let n_j=3*2^j-1 and N_j=5n_j=15*2^j-5. For j>=10, the exact dyadic
lower scaling (7.1) and monotonicity toward the upper ray limit give

    B_(n_j)>=lambda^(j-10) B_3071,
    B_(N_j)<=B_(15*2^j-1)<=lambda^j Z_15
                         <lambda^(j-8)(B_3840+19).

Divide these inequalities and use (9.1):

    B_(N_j)/B_(n_j)<Theta,  j>=10.                       (13.4)

The last dominant leaf has NOT been physically replaced. Let

    ell_j=(1-A^(-1/2))/sqrt(B_(n_j)).

For A>=1 and n_j>=1 one has 0<=ell_j<1/sqrt(B_(n_j))<1. Equations
(13.1) and (13.4) yield the fully finite estimate

    B_(N_j)W_(N_j)(H_(n_j))
      < Theta/[A(1-ell_j)^2]
      <=Theta/[A(1-B_(n_j)^(-1/2))^2],  j>=10.          (13.5)

The last displayed weak inequality also covers A=1. There is no loss of
the original function certificate, of any gradient, or of the terminal
offset in this estimate.

Here is an explicit rational conditional theorem. Suppose the seed has
length four and

    A>=A0:=193/25.

For j>=25, (9.1) and the elementary inequalities lambda>2 and lambda^2>5
give

    B_(n_j)>=lambda^(j-10)B_3071
             >64000 lambda^15
             >64000*2*5^7=10000000000.

Hence B_(n_j)^(-1/2)<1/100000. Define delta=1/50000. From (9.1),

    Theta/A <=Theta/A0 <1-1/19300 <1-2delta,
    (1-B_(n_j)^(-1/2))^2
          >(1-1/100000)^2>1-delta.

Since 0<delta<1,

    (1-2delta)/(1-delta)<1-delta

by multiplication by 1-delta: the right side contributes the additional
positive term delta^2. Substitution into (13.5) proves

    W_(N_j)(H_(n_j)) < (1-1/50000) U_(N_j)^ZJ
    for EVERY integer j>=25,
    n_j=3*2^j-1,  N_j=15*2^j-5.                          (13.6)

This conclusion includes A=A0. The strict scalar margin pays for the
untouched final leaf, so strict inequality A>A0 is unnecessary for this
particular sufficient interface. For any A>Theta, (13.5) likewise supplies
an explicit finite starting depth after choosing a smaller positive margin.
No near-optimality of the displayed depth 25 or margin 1/50000 is claimed.

The completely explicit missing object can therefore be stated as follows:
four nonnegative steps h_0,...,h_3 with sum at least 168/25, and all twenty
ordered finite multipliers Lambda_ij>=0 for distinct i,j in {0,...,4},
whose full five-gradient matrix in Section 10 is positive semidefinite
and whose exact finite-node flow is omega=(-2h_0,...,-2h_3,2sum h_i).
The full x_0 row remains zero, as derived there. Neither existence of that
matrix and word nor impossibility is proved. No seed search was performed.

## 14. Audit, provenance, and the exact remaining obligations

This candidate uses accepted fact 0be8b54e0a33381c for the original
benchmark, the all-schedule Huber witness, and the square-root join
contraction. Its full proof was reread. The equivalent recurrence also
appears in accepted fact b98bb807d514e80d. All other mathematical steps
are reproduced here, including the C77 scalar tail argument and C75
composition identities, so neither unverified draft is treated as a fact.

The ONLY external theorem used here is the precisely matched primitive
balanced recurrence from Corollary 4.2 of Liu--Chen--Jiang--Wang,
arXiv:2609.11788v1, fully stated in Section 2. The underlying four-point
rearrangement and balanced-composition proofs and adjacent-ratio proof
were reread in the exact downloaded text. Matlas again returned unrelated
statements; none was adopted. The reference is grounded in the primary
arXiv page, not falsely claimed as a Matlas theorem hit. The source's
asymmetric phase/nonconstancy/support-envelope results are unnecessary
for (9.2), (9.4)--(9.5), or (13.6).

Symbolic checks this round covered the power inequality, both adjacent
ratio maps, the dominant adjacent bound, the exact merge polynomial,
strict optimal-spine contraction, all four parity cases including the
P_-1=0 boundary, the two parity classes in the tail sum, all ray index
offsets, the primitive matrix sign/flow convention, and both physical
composition identities. These are author audits, not a verifier verdict.

The exact finite certificate has 4096 base inequalities and the complete
all-split interval table and enclosing source. All 16 files in the public
C77 manifest matched their recorded hashes. The C75 local document and
public mirror matched byte for byte with SHA256
bb6fc50e9ec42e12f7e6aac4320adc69b5426875e94a9ae1d2d383980782134f.
The C77 proof and raw outputs were not changed. No recurrence was rerun.
The two new constant comparisons and file-hash audit took
0.049172388993611094 measured seconds and were charged as saved-output
postprocessing to the existing fourth run. Thus the ledger remains CLOSED
at four of four runs, now 32.393933162988105 measured seconds. This is no

The q_n concavity counterexample and zero-prefix support-envelope

The deliverable is a COMPLETE JOINT UNVERIFIED candidate and its explicit

来源：D:\Danus\runtime\projects\gd-exact-profile-20260926\research\audit_c78\finite_certificate_proof.md

证据标签：source_document_no_new_verification。以下内容原样收录，不因汇编而获得新的证明地位。

<!-- BEGIN_SOURCE_BODY DOC-002313 -->
## 8. Explicit finite certificate and its independent exact check

The denominator is D80=2^80=1208925819614629174706176. Four arrays
pl,pu,bl,bu enclose respectively C_n from below/above and B_n from
below/above. Their index-zero values are all D80. At every n from 1
through 4095 they use EVERY original split k+l=n-1, k,l>=0.

For positive integer numerators a,b, the primitive radical numerator is
R=(a+b)^2+4ab with linear numerator c=a+b. The dominant radical
numerator is R=b^2+8ab with c=4a+b. Thus each exact join is
(c+sqrt(R))/(2D80). A lower numerator is
floor((c+floor(sqrt(R)))/2); an upper numerator is
ceil((c+ceil(sqrt(R)))/2). These expressions bound that join because
both rounding operations have the indicated orientation. Positivity
and coordinatewise monotonicity of both kernels, followed by taking
the maximum over every split, prove enclosure by induction on n.
No argmax is assumed exact and no split is removed.

Here is a complete deterministic defining program for the finite
certificate. Python integers denote arbitrary-precision integers;
isqrt(v) is the unique nonnegative integer r with r^2<=v<(r+1)^2.
The program's finite loop bounds and all asserted integer values are
part of the certificate. No floating-point quantity enters it.

```python
from math import isqrt
D80 = 1 << 80
nmax = 4095
def enclosed_join(a,b,dominant,upper):
    rad = b*b+8*a*b if dominant else (a+b)*(a+b)+4*a*b
    c = 4*a+b if dominant else a+b
    r = isqrt(rad)
    if upper:
        r += (r*r != rad)
        return (c+r+1)//2
    return (c+r)//2
pl=[D80]; pu=[D80]; bl=[D80]; bu=[D80]
for n in range(1,nmax+1):
    pl.append(max(enclosed_join(pl[k],pl[n-1-k],False,False) for k in range(n)))
    pu.append(max(enclosed_join(pu[k],pu[n-1-k],False,True) for k in range(n)))
    bl.append(max(enclosed_join(pl[k],bl[n-1-k],True,False) for k in range(n)))
    bu.append(max(enclosed_join(pu[k],bu[n-1-k],True,True) for k in range(n)))
qlo=[]; qhi=[]
for n in range(nmax+1):
    lo=isqrt(D80*bl[n]); rf=isqrt(D80*bu[n])
    assert lo*lo <= D80*bl[n] < (lo+1)**2
    assert rf*rf <= D80*bu[n] < (rf+1)**2
    qlo.append(lo); qhi.append(rf+(rf*rf != D80*bu[n]))
root2=isqrt(2*D80*D80)
assert root2*root2 <= 2*D80*D80 < (root2+1)**2
lamlo=D80+root2; lamhi=lamlo+1
slo=isqrt(D80*lamlo)
assert slo*slo <= D80*lamlo < (slo+1)**2
lam2hi=(lamhi*lamhi+D80-1)//D80
assert slo == 1878397475050142397719609
assert lam2hi == 7046136038847924384392699
slacks=[]
for t in range(nmax+1):
    n=t//2
    slack=(slo*qlo[n]-D80*qhi[t] if t%2==0 else
           slo*(qlo[n]+qlo[n+1])-2*D80*qhi[t])
    assert slack>0
    slacks.append(slack)
assert min(slacks)==33709536743225496543599832619768805273186776891
assert slacks.index(min(slacks))==4094
L3071=bl[3071]; H3840=bu[3840]
assert L3071==77954524439662110401113475160
assert H3840==103225412920462918384305104511
assert H3840<90000*D80
assert 773*L3071*D80-100*lam2hi*(H3840+19*D80)==98261575859406148591490057041393620527049740462983180
assert 772*L3071*D80-100*lam2hi*(H3840+19*D80)==4020338508518990670593580566018450094572713188395020
assert 19299*L3071*D80-2500*lam2hi*(H3840+19*D80)==6267225362087608843943037675086081931840802435287340
assert L3071-64000*D80==583271984325843219918211160
assert 2048**4>25600**3
```

In this program qlo[n]/D80<=sqrt(B_n)<=qhi[n]/D80,
slo/D80<=sqrt(lambda), and lambda^2<=lam2hi/D80. Consequently
positivity of the even and odd slacks proves, respectively,
sqrt(B_(2n))<=sqrt(lambda)*sqrt(B_n) and
sqrt(B_(2n+1))<=sqrt(lambda)*(sqrt(B_n)+sqrt(B_(n+1)))/2,
for every fine index from 0 through 4095. In the notation of Section 5,
these are exactly all 4096 inequalities e_t=0. This includes t=0 and
the final odd index4095, whose two coarse indices are2047,2048.

The full four-array table with a lower witness split for each n is
the public artifact
/opt/danus/runtime/projects/gd-exact-profile-20260926/research/audit_c78/input_interval_table.csv,
SHA256 2a7585bc0bade9f85461456c13d60bbe618f85c7cc816e1ddc5b4c1a1d611e68.
Its identity is fixed by this hash; correctness is justified by the
enclosure induction and the following independent exact check, not
by the hash. This check does not need to assert that the table entries
are the narrowest possible enclosures or that any stored maximizing
split is a true maximizer.

For each n>=1 and every split k+l=n-1, let a=pu[k],b=pu[l],H=pu[n].
The checker verifies

    2H-a-b>=0,  (2H-a-b)^2 >= (a+b)^2+4ab.

This is exactly Omega(a/D80,b/D80)<=H/D80, by squaring two
nonnegative sides. For the dominant recurrence it puts
a=pu[k],b=bu[l],H=bu[n] and checks

    2H-4a-b>=0,  (2H-4a-b)^2 >= b^2+8ab.

These are sufficient upper bounds for ALL original splits. For each
lower endpoint it checks a single stored admissible split with the
lower child endpoints. If L is the parent lower endpoint numerator
and c,R are the corresponding linear/radical numerators, it verifies
either 2L-c<=0 or (2L-c)^2<=R. In the first case sqrt(R)>=2L-c
because sqrt(R)>=0; in the second case the same inequality follows
by squaring. Hence the actual maximum is at least L/D80. Starting
from all four exact index-zero values, induction certifies EVERY
stored interval without any reliance on the original generator's
execution. This proves sufficiency of the independent checker.

The checker then forms new qlo,qhi using integer square roots,
checks the defining inequalities for every root, recomputes all4096
base slacks, and checks the endpoint comparisons in this section.
It retains all slacks in independent_base_slacks.csv. The new file
has SHA256 ab3ece1dc47d512fe9399b1832e649ee09e59beec3e299e85839d9dd3334d5a7.

The independent source is
/opt/danus/runtime/projects/gd-exact-profile-20260926/research/audit_c78/independent_certificate_check.py,
SHA256 5c79d614a1a98617a5f978f740ef0937f3e5dd773dd9cf052b8afda90add6244.
Its complete successful result is
/opt/danus/runtime/projects/gd-exact-profile-20260926/research/audit_c78/independent_certificate_result.json,
SHA256 bc8e00fffc534b8cecb12181b09bb104e865ecb935b68f4514b80ac36bce26be.
It checked 8,386,560 original upper splits for EACH sequence,
8,190 lower witnesses, and all4,096 base inequalities, completing
with exit status0 in5.589835043007042 seconds. All16 original public
manifest files matched their hashes. The old phase-discovery ledger
was not modified. This is one independently checked finite certificate,
not a discovery scan and not an assumption of zero defect at larger
indices.

<!-- BEGIN_SOURCE_BODY DOC-002314 -->
# Full primitive certificates: cut slack and quantitative prefix control

Status: complete joint proof DRAFT, UNVERIFIED. This is max4's final
authorized round23. Fact04219554e41d9750 supplies the precise full seed
interface and its conditional asymptotic consequence, but does not verify
the new statements below. No seed existence or nonexistence is claimed.
No optimizer, numerical evaluation, finite replay, or fact_submit is used.

Novelty relative to the shared record: f7cee3034df8cb7f already derived the
exact saturated-cut structure and the signed tail credit. The new content
here is an exact positive-slack budget, including steps below1, its full
matrix consequences, and explicit quantitative control of a prefix when
a cut is near equality. The equality case is rederived as a consistency
check; the old claim of automatic nonnegative tail restriction is not used.
All new implications are proved together here; no unverified finding is a
premise. The accepted inputs are04219554e41d9750 and1252b8823bb5a290.

## 1. Full formal interface and the gradient-difference matrix

Let r>=1, h_0,...,h_(r-1)>=0, P_i=sum_(t<i)h_t for0<=i<=r,
s=P_r, and C=1+s. Set h_r=0 only for formulas. Indices of the formal
gradients range over0,...,r. Let e be the all-ones column, and define

    T_ii=1, T_it=h_t for t<i, T_it=0 for t>i,
    omega=(-2h_0,...,-2h_(r-1),2s)^T,
    D=diag(h_0(2-h_0),...,h_(r-1)(2-h_(r-1)),-s(s+2)),
    L=diag(Lambda e)-Lambda,  M=D+(LT+T^T L^T)/2.

Assume Lambda_ii=0, all off-diagonal Lambda_ij>=0, L^T e=omega,
and M>=0 on the entire original space R^(r+1). Thus this is precisely
the full finite certificate in fact04219554e41d9750, with arbitrary r.
No multiplier support, rank, or equality between formal gradients is imposed.

For x_i=x_0-sum_(t<i)h_t g_t, define

    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2,
    V_h=2sum_(i<r)h_i(f_i-f_r)+sum_(i<r)h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2
                                               -s(s+1)||g_r||^2.

The full coefficient identity from fact04219554e41d9750 is

    V_h+sum_(i!=j)Lambda_ij Q_ij = <M,Gram(g_0,...,g_r)>.       (1)

The residuals Q_ij are nonpositive on every actual convex differentiable
1-smooth trajectory by fact1252b8823bb5a290. Hence this interface implies
V_h>=0 on all such trajectories with a minimizer.

Here is an algebraic proof of the constant-gradient kernel, needed below.
Since Te=e+(P_0,...,P_r)^T and
2sum_(i<r)h_i P_i=s^2-sum_(i<r)h_i^2, we have

    e^T D e=-s^2-sum h_i^2,
    e^T LT e=omega^T Te=s^2+sum h_i^2.

Thus e^T M e=0. Positive semidefiniteness implies Me=0: otherwise for
some y the linear term in (e+t y)^T M(e+t y) would be negative for one
sign of sufficiently small t.

For0<=j<r let u^j be the indicator of indices0,...,j. Let U have these
r columns and put K_jump=U^T M U. For any scalar formal vector g,

    g=e*g_r+sum_(j<r)u^j(g_j-g_(j+1)),
    g^T M g=sum_(i,j<r)(K_jump)_ij(g_i-g_(i+1))(g_j-g_(j+1)). (2)

In particular K_jump>=0. Conversely, under Me=0, its PSD property is
equivalent to full M>=0 by(2). This is a congruence identity retaining
all formal gradients, not a deletion of zero-step coordinates.

## 2. A legal cut trajectory for every positive step

Fix j with b=h_j>0. In this section define

    P=P_j, R=s-P_(j+1), A=1+P, B=1+R, d=b-1,
    v=1-b                 if 0<b<=1,
    v=(1-b)/B             if b>=1,
    w=1-v>0.

The two formulas agree at b=1. There is one legal scalar convex1-smooth
function with a minimizer whose GD trajectory has gradients1 at all
indices i<=j and v at all indices k>j, starting from x_0=A. Its positions
are

    x_i=1+P_j-P_i                         (i<=j),
    x_k=1-b-v(P_k-P_(j+1))                (k>j).             (3)

All sampled values can be chosen as f_i=x_i g_i-g_i^2/2.

For b>1, use the derivative clip(x,[v,1]) and f(0)=0. The bridge lands
at -d=vB, the suffix remains at or below v, and its terminal point is v.
The derivative is continuous, nondecreasing and1-Lipschitz, and0 is a
minimizer. On each plateau the stated values follow by integration.

For0<b<=1, v=1-b>=0. Put zeta=v(1-R)-1, strictly below every suffix
sample and below v. Define a derivative on the whole real line by

    f'(x)=0                  for x<=zeta-v,
          x-zeta+v           for zeta-v<=x<=zeta,
          v                  for zeta<=x<=v,
          x                  for v<=x<=1,
          1                  for x>=1.

Choose the integration constant by f(v)=v^2/2. This derivative is again
continuous, nondecreasing and1-Lipschitz, and its zero region contains
minimizers. The bridge lands at v. The suffix stays in[zeta,v], so its
gradient is v. Integration gives the stated sampled values on both
plateaus. Degenerate intervals when v=0 cause no difficulty. The minimum
value need not be zero; V_h and every finite Q are invariant under adding
a constant to f.

For any scalar data satisfying f_i=x_i g_i-g_i^2/2, telescoping the GD
squares gives

    V_h=x_0^2-(x_r+s g_r)^2.

On(3), x_r=-d-vR, hence the exact energy is

    E_j=A^2-[(A+d)v-d]^2.                                (4)

The same sampled-value identity gives Q_ik=(g_i-g_k)(x_k-g_k).
Define, for k>j,

    t_jk=d+v(1+P_k-P_(j+1)).

These numbers are nonnegative, with the more transparent forms

    t_jk=v(P_k-P_(j+1))          if0<b<=1,
    t_jk=(-v)(s-P_k)             ifb>=1.

Every within-block residual is zero. Every cross residual is exactly

    -Q_(i,k)=w*t_jk,
    -Q_(k,i)=w*(P_j-P_i),              i<=j<k.             (5)

Since g=v e+w u^j and Me=0, substitution into the complete identity(1)
gives the exact slack budget

    E_j=w^2(K_jump)_jj
       +w sum_(i<=j<k)[t_jk Lambda_(i,k)
                                      +(P_j-P_i)Lambda_(k,i)]. (6)

Every term on the right is nonnegative. Equation(6) keeps every original
cross multiplier, including both directions and both endpoints.

## 3. Step-only matrix bounds and a quadratic compatibility test

For each positive h_j define beta_j=E_j/w^2 using the preceding formulas.
A feasible full certificate necessarily has beta_j>=0 and

    0<=(K_jump)_jj<=beta_j.                              (7)

When b>=1, the expression simplifies to

    beta_j=[A^2 B^2-C^2 d^2]/(B+d)^2,
    C=A+B+d.                                             (8)

For0<b<=1 it is

    beta_j=[A^2-(1-b)^2(A+b)^2]/b^2.                     (9)

No negative-v trajectory is used in deriving(9). In particular the
small-step case is not obtained by applying a wrong-sign Huber residual.

For this section suppose all steps are positive. Set beta_(-1)=beta_r=0
and Drow_i=sqrt(beta_(i-1))+sqrt(beta_i) for0<=i<=r.
For a PSD matrix, its2-by-2 principal minors give
|(K_jump)_ik|<=sqrt(beta_i beta_k). Equation(2), applied to a standard
coordinate vector, therefore gives

    M_ii<=Drow_i^2,
    |(M u^j)_i|<=Drow_i sqrt(beta_j).                    (10)

The second inequality is the PSD Cauchy--Schwarz inequality; it also
follows by requiring (e_i+t u^j)^T M(e_i+t u^j)>=0 for every real t.
For every real vector z of length r,

    0<=z^T K_jump z<=(sum_(i<r)|z_i|sqrt(beta_i))^2.      (11)

An additional necessary condition involves no unknown multipliers.
Put p_0=1, p_(i+1)=(1-h_i)p_i. The actual quadratic f(x)=x^2/2,
started at1, has x_i=g_i=p_i and every finite Q_ik=0. Consequently

    V_h=1-C^2 p_r^2,
    0<=1-C^2 p_r^2
          <=(sum_(i<r)|h_i p_i|sqrt(beta_i))^2.           (12)

The upper inequality follows from(2),(11), since p_i-p_(i+1)=h_i p_i.
Equation(12) is only a necessary compatibility test. Neither it nor the
scalar inequalities beta_j>=0 are asserted sufficient.

## 4. Explicit stability at a cut with b>1

Assume all h_i>0 and fix a cut j with b=h_j>1. Continue using the local
notation P,R,A,B,d above, and set

    a=d/B, w=1+a, delta=beta_j,
    m=min_(i<r)h_i>0, Delta_cut=AB-Cd.

Equation(8) implies Delta_cut>=0 and d<B: if d>=B, then Cd>=CB>AB.
Write u=Lambda_(j,r), v_k=Lambda_(k,j) for k>j, and set
c_k=h_k for j<k<r, c_r=1; thus sum_(k>j)c_k=B.

The ideal cross array has only the entries

    Lambda^0_(j,r)=d q, Lambda^0_(k,j)=q c_k (k>j),
    q=2(A+d)/(B-d),                                     (13)

with every other cross entry zero. At exact cut equality this is the
usual primitive-join array. Away from equality it is just an explicitly
defined comparison array; it is not claimed to be a certificate.

Here are step-only error bounds, with empty sums interpreted as zero:

    Fbd=w delta/(a m) if j<r-1, and Fbd=0 otherwise,
    Bbd=w delta/m     if j>0,   and Bbd=0 otherwise,
    Ubd=[2 sqrt(delta)sum_(i<j)Drow_i+P Bbd]/B,
    eta=Fbd+Bbd+Ubd,
    Ebd=2 sqrt(delta)sum_(k>j)Drow_k+2C(r-j)eta,
    Lbd=[2B/(B-d)] [eta+Ebd/d].                         (14)

Then the sum of the absolute differences of ALL crossing coefficients
in the supplied full array and(13) is at most Lbd:

    sum_(i<=j<k)(|Lambda_(i,k)-Lambda^0_(i,k)|
                      +|Lambda_(k,i)-Lambda^0_(k,i)|)<=Lbd. (15)

Here is a derivation, keeping track of the off-support terms.
First (6) with t_jk=a(s-P_k) yields

    Factual=sum_(i<=j,j<k<r)Lambda_(i,k)<=Fbd,
    Bactual=sum_(k>j,i<j)Lambda_(k,i)<=Bbd.              (16)

The weights s-P_k and P_j-P_i in these sums are at least m. The
remaining potentially unwanted cross terms are u_i=Lambda_(i,r),i<j.
For i<j, summing the complete mixed matrix entries gives

    2(M 1_right)_i
      =-B u_i-sum_(j<l<r)(1+P_l-P_(j+1))Lambda_(i,l)
       -sum_(k>j)Lambda_(k,i)
       +h_i sum_(k>j,l<=i)Lambda_(k,l).                 (17)

In(17), 1_right=e-u^j. By Me=0 and(10), the absolute value of its left
side is at most2Drow_i sqrt(delta). The two subtracted sums are
nonnegative. Thus

    B u_i<=2Drow_i sqrt(delta)+h_i Bactual.

Summing gives Uactual=sum_(i<j)u_i<=Ubd. Hence the total mass of all
cross entries absent from(13) is at most eta.

For completeness, the full off-diagonal formula for i<k is

    2M_ik=-(Lambda_ik+Lambda_ki)
          -h_k sum_(l>k)Lambda_il+h_i sum_(l<=i)Lambda_kl. (18)

It follows directly by multiplying L and T; set h_r=0 in this formula.
For fixed k>j, summing absolute values in(18) over i<j gives

    sum_(i<j)|2M_ik|
       <=(1+h_k)(Factual+Uactual)+(1+P)Bactual<=C eta.

Since (M u^j)_k=M_kj+sum_(i<j)M_ki, equation(10) gives
|2M_jk|<=2Drow_k sqrt(delta)+C eta.
Formula(18) at i=j gives, for k<r,

    2M_jk=d v_k-h_k u
        -Lambda_jk-h_k sum_(k<l<r)Lambda_jl
                                  +b sum_(l<j)Lambda_kl,

and at k=r gives 2M_jr=d v_r-u+b sum_(l<j)Lambda_rl.
The terms after d v_k-c_k u have total absolute value at most C eta.
Therefore, for every k>j,

    |d v_k-c_k u|<=e_k,
    e_k=2Drow_k sqrt(delta)+2C eta,
    sum_(k>j)e_k=Ebd.                                  (19)

Sum the original value-flow equations over the left block. Its outward
flow is u+Uactual+Factual and its inward flow is sum v_k+Bactual. Thus

    |sum_(k>j)v_k-u-2(A+d)|<=eta.

Together with(19) this implies

    |u-d q|<=[d eta+Ebd]/(B-d)=:Uerr,
    |v_k-q c_k|<=[c_k Uerr+e_k]/d.

Adding these bounds and the off-support mass eta gives

    eta+(1+B/d)Uerr+Ebd/d
        =[2B/(B-d)] [eta+Ebd/d]=Lbd,

which proves(15). In particular this controls the entire cross array,
not a selected sparse ansatz. No assertion about the internal arrays'
signs after tail restriction has entered the calculation.

## 5. Quantitative primitive-energy bound for the actual prefix

Let h_left=(h_0,...,h_(j-1)), of sum P and capacity A. Under the
hypotheses of Section4, EVERY actual convex1-smooth trajectory of this
prefix obeys

    V_(h_left)>=-[Rbd+(2P+b+1/2)Lbd] Gmax^2,             (20)
    Rbd=(A+d)Delta_cut/(B-d),
    Gmax=max_(0<=i<=j)||g_i||.

The energy of an empty prefix is0. For a nonempty prefix, no initial
distance normalization or bounded minimizer distance is assumed in(20).

To prove the claim, take any actual prefix data. Translate positions
and shift values so x_j=0 and f_j=0. Append formal tail data by setting
every g_k=f_k=0 for k>j and x_k=-b g_j for k>j. These tail data are NOT
asserted to arise from an actual function. They are permitted for
evaluating the full formal PSD identity(1). All tail-internal Q vanish.

Using the ideal cross array(13), direct substitution yields

    V_h+sum_cross Lambda^0_ik Q_ik
                      =V_(h_left)+Rbd||g_j||^2.         (21)

For an explicit coefficient check before the f_j=0 shift, V_h-V_left
has value coefficient2(P+b)f_j and gradient coefficient
b(1-b)+P(P+1) on ||g_j||^2. The ideal cross sum contributes
q(d-B)f_j+q[B/2-d(b-1/2)]||g_j||^2. The value coefficients cancel
because q(B-d)=2(A+d)=2(P+b). The remaining coefficient is

    A^2-(1+q)d^2
      =[(A+d)(AB-d(A+B+d))]/(B-d)=Rbd.

The actual prefix inequalities Q_ik<=0 and Lambda_ik>=0 make their
contribution in(1) nonpositive. Every cross residual on these formal
data has absolute value at most(2P+b+1/2)Gmax^2: actual convexity gives
|f_i-f_j|<=Gmax||x_i-x_j||<=P Gmax^2, GD gives
||x_i-x_j||<=P Gmax, and the formulas are

    Q_(i,k)=f_i+<g_i,-b g_j-x_i>+||g_i||^2/2,
    Q_(k,i)=-f_i+||g_i||^2/2,                    i<=j<k.

Equations(1),(15),(21) and full M>=0 now give(20).
This proof uses the unobserved formal extension only inside a PSD
identity; it never claims those data constitute a legal GD witness.

The error Rbd is linear in the scalar cut gap:

    delta=Delta_cut(AB+Cd)/(B+d)^2,
    Rbd=delta (A+d)(B+d)^2/[(B-d)(AB+Cd)].              (22)

Consequently, along a bounded positive-step family with m and d bounded
away from0, B-d bounded away from0, and all beta_i bounded, the right
side's coefficient in(20) is O(sqrt(delta)). This asymptotic notation
is only a consequence of the explicit bounds(14),(22).

An actual prefix witness with V_left=-nu Gmax^2, nu>0, therefore forces

    nu<=Rbd+(2P+b+1/2)Lbd.                              (23)

This is an explicit necessary condition on the parent steps. It supplies
a quantitative route from a prefix obstruction to a positive gap at the
parent cut, rather than assuming an exactly saturated cut.

For a concrete symbolic instance, take a parent whose first step is3/2
and select j=1, assuming its selected bridge h_1>1 and all steps positive.
For its one-step prefix, f(x)=x^2/2 started at1 has gradients1,-1/2,
Gmax=1 and V_left=1-(5/2)^2(1/2)^2=-9/16. Therefore every such parent
full certificate must satisfy

    Rbd+(b+7/2)Lbd>=9/16.                              (24)

In any bounded family meeting the positivity conditions after(22),
this prohibits approaching delta=0 with that fixed first step. It does
not exclude a parent with a positive cut gap or any unrelated seed.

## 6. Equality, zero steps, and exact symbolic checks

At delta=0, every quantity in(14) vanishes and Delta_cut=0. Thus all
cross entries are exactly(13). Their mixed gradient block is zero by
(18),(19). The left internal multipliers are nonnegative and give a
full certificate for V_left, since(21) now has Rbd=0 and the full left
matrix is the corresponding principal PSD block. This recovers the
old saturated-prefix restriction by a new limiting-compatible estimate.

Writing kappa=(C/B)^2 at equality gives q=kappa-1. The accepted full
primitive-join identity then shows that the tail has a certificate for

    V_tail-(1-1/kappa)sum_(k<r)h_k Q_(k,r),

where this sum is over nonterminal TAIL indices. Removing this signed
credit would subtract q h_k from its actual coefficient Lambda_(k,r).
Nothing in the preceding estimates asserts that those differences are
nonnegative. Thus no automatic ordinary primitive-tail restriction is
inferred; the old counterexample remains relevant.

Zero steps require care. The formulas(1),(2),(6) at a POSITIVE cut hold
even if other steps vanish. But(7)--(15) for every coordinate are not
claimed for an original word containing zeros. A full certificate can
have arbitrarily large curvature in an unused zero-step gradient direction.
For example h=(0), Lambda_01=Lambda_10=t>=0 satisfies the complete
flow equations and has M=t[[1,-1],[-1,1]]. Its jump matrix is[t], with
no finite step-only bound on t. This is an exact counterexample to
extending beta=2-b^2 continuously to a formal coordinate at b=0.

For necessary schedule tests one may instead compress zero steps in a
justified way. Retain the original index of each positive step and the
original terminal index. Identify formal jets within each repeated-query
group only to RESTRICT a given full certificate. The GD formulas and
V_h become exactly those of the positive-step compressed word. An edge
within one group vanishes after restriction; every other edge becomes
the corresponding compressed Q with its nonnegative coefficients summed.
Thus a full original certificate implies a full compressed certificate.

Conversely, a full compressed primitive certificate embeds into the
original formal space by assigning its edges only to the retained indices
and assigning zero to other edges. Zero steps contribute nothing to V_h
or to any queried displacement, and the original terminal is retained.
Therefore the embedded identity is the pullback of the compressed PSD
matrix and is PSD on ALL original independent gradients. No equality
between zero-step gradients is needed for this converse. The all-zero
word has the zero certificate. This proves feasibility equivalence while
also explaining why the original matrix entries need not be bounded.

In particular the tests above may be applied to the compressed word,
and the exact saturated-prefix conclusion may be lifted to the original
prefix by this embedding. The quantitative constants for a compressed
word refer to its compressed coefficient array, not an unjustified bound
on every possible original zero-step coefficient.

For one positive step b, the only value-flow and constant-kernel solution
is Lambda_01=2, Lambda_10=2b+2. Direct expansion gives

    V_(b)+2Q_01+(2b+2)Q_10=(2-b^2)||g_0-g_1||^2.

Both versions(8),(9) reduce exactly to beta_0=2-b^2. Thus they recover
the complete one-step interface and its endpoint b=sqrt(2), including
the b<1 branch, without a numerical evaluation.

## 7. Scope and remaining missing object

For the C78 problem set r=4 and require sum h_i>=168/25, retaining all20
original nonnegative multipliers and the full5-gradient matrix. The new
constraints(6),(12),(15),(20) are necessary for that complete interface.
They complement scalar cut bounds and can support exact rejection or
reconstruction of a proposed seed. No candidate word has been evaluated
numerically here; no witness with the required capacity has been found,
and no separator for the entire feasible set has been established.

The original target remains open. The sole accepted scalar/composition
bridge is fact04219554e41d9750. This draft has no verifier allocation and
may not be used as a fact. The final-cut equality subgoal in the round23
plan was already present in shared work; the positive-slack estimates,
small-step trajectories and zero-coordinate warning are the additions.

Justification: explicit actual-function construction, coefficient
identities and elementary PSD inequalities. Main fragility checks are
the direction of cross residuals, small-step plateau legality, the full
off-diagonal signs, and the distinction between a formal extension and
an actual trajectory. Each is expanded above. Confidence before independent
verification:0.97; no verifier acceptance is claimed.

# Candidate exact coordinates and necessary tests for the C78 seed

Status: UNVERIFIED follow-up algebra. The one selected joint C78 gate
was accepted and persisted as fact04219554e41d9750. THIS follow-up is
not part of that accepted fact and has no additional submission allocation.
The accepted fact supplies the exact seed interface and its conditional
asymptotic application. Every new algebraic consequence below is derived
in this one draft, not adopted from an unverified finding. No actual seed
is constructed and no nonexistence claim is made. No numerical evaluation,
optimizer, parameter sweep, or additional verification request was used.

The following is rederived for the exact full primitive certificate. It
resembles earlier shared constant-gradient kernel and objective-certificate
coordinates, but does not import those unverified findings as premises.

## 1. Complete formal setting

Let r>=1 be an integer, h_0,...,h_(r-1)>=0, s=sum h_i, and A=1+s.
Set h_r=0 only as a notational convention. Let P_i=sum_(t<i)h_t for
0<=i<=r; thus P_0=0, P_r=s, and this is the prefix-sum P_i already used
in the project. Let e be the all-ones column of length r+1. All r+1
formal gradients are retained, including at zero steps.

Let Lambda_ij>=0 for every distinct finite pair i,j in{0,...,r}, with
zero diagonal, and let L_flow=diag(Lambda e)-Lambda. Put

    omega=(-2h_0,...,-2h_(r-1),2s)^T,
    T_ii=1, T_it=h_t for t<i, T_it=0 for t>i,
    D_prim=diag(h_0(2-h_0),...,h_(r-1)(2-h_(r-1)),-s(s+2)),
    M_prim=D_prim+(L_flow T+T^T L_flow^T)/2.

Assume the finite value-flow equation L_flow^T e=omega. Feasibility of
the actual primitive interface additionally requires M_prim positive
semidefinite on the whole space R^(r+1).

For completeness, this is the full certificate for the primitive energy

    V_h=2sum_(i<r)h_i(f_i-f_r)+sum_(i<r)h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2
                                             -s(s+1)||g_r||^2.

Use x_i=x_0-sum_(t<i)h_t g_t and
Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2. The value coefficients
in V_h+sum Lambda_ij Q_ij cancel by the stated flow equation. The
position terms in the sum are sym(L_flow(T-I)); the norm terms are
sym(L_flow)-diag(omega)/2. Adding the diagonal of V_h gives exactly
M_prim. Therefore V_h+sum Lambda_ij Q_ij=<M_prim,Gram(g_0,...,g_r)>.
Here sym sends a square matrix to half its sum with its transpose, and the last pairing is the trace pairing.
Accepted fact1252b8823bb5a290 proves Q_ij<=0 on every actual smooth
convex trajectory, including repeated sample positions. Thus a feasible
full matrix implies V_h>=0 on all such trajectories.

## 2. Forced constant-gradient kernel and all row equations

The scalar identity sum_(i<r)h_i P_i=(s^2-sum_i h_i^2)/2 follows by
expanding s^2 and collecting every unordered pair of distinct steps.
Since T e=e+(P_0,...,P_r)^T and sum_i omega_i=0,

    e^T D_prim e=-s^2-sum_i h_i^2,
    e^T L_flow T e=omega^T T e=s^2+sum_i h_i^2.

Consequently e^T M_prim e=0 for every array satisfying the value flow.
For a symmetric PSD matrix this forces M_prim e=0: for any real vector y,
nonnegativity of (e+t y)^T M_prim(e+t y) for both signs of sufficiently
small real t forces y^T M_prim e=0.

For i<r,

    (T^T omega)_i=-2h_i+2h_i(P_i+h_i),
    (M_prim e)_i=h_i(1+P_i)+(1/2)sum_j Lambda_ij(P_i-P_j).

At the terminal index,

    (M_prim e)_r=-s(s+1)+(1/2)sum_(j<r)Lambda_rj(s-P_j).

Hence every feasible seed has the exact row-moment equations

    sum_j Lambda_ij(P_j-P_i)=2h_i(1+P_i),  0<=i<r,       (S1)
    sum_(j<r)Lambda_rj(s-P_j)=2s(s+1).                    (S2)

For h_i>0, (S1) requires at least one actual outgoing coefficient to an
index j>i with P_j>P_i. This is because all earlier/equal-prefix terms
have nonpositive displacement, whereas the right side is positive.

## 3. Exact elimination of terminal multipliers

Choose all r(r-1) off-diagonal queried-to-queried entries
Rseed_ij=Lambda_ij>=0, 0<=i,j<r, i!=j, with Rseed_ii=0. Write

    out_seed_i=sum_(j<r)Rseed_ij, in_seed_i=sum_(j<r)Rseed_ji,
    u_seed_i=Lambda_(i,r), v_seed_i=Lambda_(r,i), tail_i=s-P_i.

If tail_i>0, (S1) determines the outgoing terminal coefficient:

    u_seed_i=[2h_i(1+P_i)-sum_(j<r)Rseed_ij(P_j-P_i)]/tail_i.     (S3)

The value flow at node i determines the incoming terminal coefficient:

    v_seed_i=2h_i+out_seed_i+u_seed_i-in_seed_i.                           (S4)

Both u_seed_i and v_seed_i must be nonnegative. There is no upper-triangular,
lower-triangular, symmetric, rank, or support restriction on Rseed.

If tail_i=0, no division is made. Instead retain u_seed_i>=0 as a free
variable and require

    sum_(j<r)Rseed_ij(P_j-P_i)=0.                            (S5)

Here h_i=0 and P_i=s, since the steps are nonnegative. Thus (S5)
is equivalent to Rseed_ij=0 whenever P_j<s. Equation(S4) still defines
v_seed_i and still requires v_seed_i>=0. No formal gradient is removed.

Equations(S3) or(S5) give every nonterminal row of M_prim e=0. Summing(S4)
gives sum_i v_seed_i-sum_i u_seed_i=2s, which is precisely terminal value flow.
With all value-flow equations in place, e^T M_prim e=0 was proved in
Section2; therefore the sum of the entries of M_prim e is zero. Its first
r entries are already zero, so its terminal entry is also zero.
Equivalently, (S2) follows automatically. Thus this reconstruction
preserves every row condition, including the terminal one.

Let K_seed be the leading r-by-r principal block of the resulting full M_prim.
As M_prim e=0, the complete matrix is exactly

    M_prim = [ K_seed,           -K_seed*1 ;
          -1^T*K_seed,       1^T*K_seed*1 ],                       (S6)

where 1 is the all-ones vector of length r. For every scalar coordinate
of the original gradients its quadratic form is

    (g_0-g_r,...,g_(r-1)-g_r)^T K_seed(g_0-g_r,...,g_(r-1)-g_r).

Therefore full M_prim PSD is EQUIVALENT to K_seed PSD: necessity follows by
restriction to g_r=0, sufficiency by this identity. The identity retains
all original formal coordinates. It does not assume that any repeated
gradients agree.

Every entry of K_seed is explicit. For i<r,

    K_seed_ii=h_i(2-h_i)+out_seed_i+u_seed_i
                         -h_i*(sum_(i<j<r)Rseed_ij+u_seed_i),    (S7)

and for 0<=i<j<r,

    2K_seed_ij=-(Rseed_ij+Rseed_ji)-h_j*(sum_(j<k<r)Rseed_ik+u_seed_i)
                                      +h_i*sum_(k<=i)Rseed_jk.  (S8)

To derive (S7)--(S8), use (L_flow T)_ij=L_ij+h_j sum_(k>j)L_ik
and distinguish whether that sum includes the diagonal of L_flow.
The diagonal D_prim affects only(S7). This is a coefficient identity
on all independent formal gradients.

For r=4 and h_3>0 all four tail_i are positive. The original twenty
finite multiplier variables are thus parameterized exactly by the
twelve entries Rseed_ij, the eight sign constraints u_seed_i,v_seed_i>=0, and the
full K_seed PSD condition. If h_3=0 use(S5) for zero tails; the statement
does not exclude such seeds. Conversely every set of parameters
meeting these displayed conditions reconstructs a feasible full seed
certificate. This is a lossless reformulation, not a restricted ansatz.

## 4. Concrete endpoint and last-edge necessary conditions

Assume s>0. Both endpoint steps obey

    0<=h_0,h_(r-1)<=2(s+1)/(s+2)<2.                     (S9)

For the first endpoint, if h_0<=1 this follows because the upper bound
in(S9) is greater than1. If h_0>1, put out_0=sum_(j>0)Lambda_0j.
Equation(S1) gives2h_0=sum_(j>0)Lambda_0j P_j<=s*out_0.
The full first diagonal is

    M_prim_00=h_0(2-h_0)+(1-h_0)out_0>=0.

Since h_0-1>0, the last two inequalities imply
s*h_0(2-h_0)>=2h_0(h_0-1). Divide by h_0>0 and rearrange to get(S9).
This derivation also rules out h_0>=2 without a separate assumption.

For the final endpoint, the case h_(r-1)=0 is immediate. If it is
positive, then s-P_j>=h_(r-1) for every j<r. The terminal diagonal
and(S2) give

    sum_(j<r)Lambda_rj >=s(s+2),
    2s(s+1)>=h_(r-1)*sum_(j<r)Lambda_rj.

Division by s>0 proves the second endpoint bound. In terms of the
sum t of all other steps, either endpoint b must therefore satisfy

    b^2+t b<=2(t+1),
    b<=(-t+sqrt(t^2+8t+8))/2.                           (S10)

The right side is the original empty-leaf primitive bridge phi(0,t).
This does not imply that the remainder of the word is primitive.
It therefore supplies no induction proving an all-length capacity cap.

If h_(r-1)>0, its row in(S1) gives the sharper exact last-edge identity

    Lambda_(r-1,r)=2(1+P_(r-1))
      +sum_(j<r-1)Lambda_(r-1,j)(P_(r-1)-P_j)/h_(r-1), (S11)

so in particular Lambda_(r-1,r)>=2(1+P_(r-1)). If that last step
is zero, do not divide; its row equation instead forces every outgoing
edge to a strictly smaller prefix sum to vanish, as in(S5).

## 5. All-curvature necessary polynomial and saturation

For 0<=tau<=1, take the actual scalar convex1-smooth function
f_tau(x)=tau*x^2/2 with x_0=1. Define

    Poly_i(tau)=product_(0<=k<i)(1-tau*h_k), Poly_0=1.

These are exactly its actual iterates, with gradients tau*Poly_i(tau).
The update telescopes as

    1-Poly_r(tau)^2
       =sum_(i<r)tau*h_i*(2-tau*h_i)*Poly_i(tau)^2.

Inserting its values and gradients into the displayed V_h yields

    V_quad(tau)=1-tau*(1-tau)*sum_(i<r)h_i Poly_i(tau)^2
        -[1+s*tau+s(s+1)*tau^2]*Poly_r(tau)^2 >=0         (S12)

for EVERY tau in[0,1]. To verify the equality, the coefficient of
Poly_i^2 from the first two terms in V_h is
tau*h_i+tau^2*h_i*(1-h_i). This equals
tau*h_i*(2-tau*h_i)-tau*(1-tau)*h_i, so the telescope gives(S12).
Feasible full M_prim implies V_h>=0, as proved in Section1. No quadratic
test is asserted sufficient for a certificate or for primitivity.

At tau=1 all finite interpolation residuals Q_ij vanish exactly and

    A^2*product_(i<r)(1-h_i)^2<=1.                       (S13)

If equality holds in(S13), the vector p=(Poly_0(1),...,Poly_r(1))^T
has p^T M_prim p=V_quad(1)=0. PSD therefore forces M_prim p=0 by the same
two-sign argument used for e. For s>0 this vector is not proportional
to e: proportionality and Poly_0=1 would make every Poly_i=1, forcing
every h_i=0 from the update. Thus rank(M_prim)<=r-1; for the four-step
seed it is at most3. In the lossless coordinates(S6), the nonzero
vector(Poly_0(1)-Poly_r(1),...,Poly_(r-1)(1)-Poly_r(1)) lies in ker K_seed.
This extra null constraint is conditional on exact saturation; it
must not be imposed on an unsaturated candidate.

## 6. Symbolic boundary calibration

For one step h_0=b>0, there are no interior Rseed entries. Equations(S3)--(S4)
give u_seed_0=2 and v_seed_0=2b+2, and(S7) gives K_seed=[2-b^2]. Therefore these exact
coordinates recover feasibility precisely for0<b<=sqrt(2). The empty-value
case b=0 is recovered with every multiplier zero. These are symbolic
identities, not sampled numerical checks.

For h=(0,b), set both queried Rseed entries zero. The formulas give u_seed=(0,2),
v_seed=(0,2b+2), and K_seed=diag(0,2-b^2). For h=(b,0), use Rseed=0, u_seed=(2,0),
v_seed=(2b+2,0), and K_seed=diag(2-b^2,0); the second outgoing terminal coefficient
is selected using the zero-tail branch(S5). Both full matrices retain all
three independent formal gradients, and both recover exactly the same
one-step nonzero spectrum without equating the two gradients at the
repeated actual query. This checks the intended interpretation of the
zero-step boundary.

## 7. Relevance and limitations

For the proposed four-step capacity at least193/25, these equations
give explicit necessary signs, endpoint bounds, all-curvature tests,
and exact full-matrix coordinates for an eventual constructive search.
They do not prove that the feasible set is empty or nonempty. All
twelve interior directed coefficients remain available; zero steps
remain allowed; no optimizer, sweep, or additional fact_submit was run.

Justification type: calculation and logical equivalence. Confidence:
0.98 before verifier review. Fragility assessment: the sign convention
and zero-tail branches are the critical algebraic interfaces, explicitly
expanded above; no numerical evidence or unsupported asymptotic limit
is used. The draft remains unverified and may not be used as a fact.

<!-- BEGIN_SOURCE_BODY DOC-002316 -->
Uniform enclosure of the original Zhang--Jiang denominator rays and a conditional full-certificate four-step seed lift.

For a nonnegative integer n and a finite nonnegative schedule h=(h_0,...,h_(n-1)), let W_n(h) be the supremum of 2(f(x_n)-f(x_star))/(L||x_0-x_star||^2), over all finite dimensions, all convex differentiable functions f with L-Lipschitz gradient and at least one minimizer x_star, and all x_0!=x_star, with x_(i+1)=x_i-(h_i/L)grad f(x_i). Here L>0 is arbitrary and fixed. The empty schedule is included. This is the W=2R normalization.

For a,d>=0 define Omega(a,d)=(a+d+sqrt((a+d)^2+4ad))/2 and G(a,d)=2a+d/2+sqrt(d^2+8ad)/2. Set C_0=B_0=1 and, for every integer n>=1, C_n=max_(0<=k<n)Omega(C_k,C_(n-k-1)) and B_n=max_(0<=k<n)G(C_k,B_(n-k-1)). These are the original all-split benchmark recurrences, with C_n=1+S_circle(n), B_n=1+2S_bullet(n), and U_n^ZJ=1/B_n. Put lambda=1+sqrt(2).

For every positive integer b, the positive finite limit Z_b=lim_(j->infinity)lambda^(-j)B_(b*2^j-1) exists. For every integer j>=0,

    lambda^(-j)B_(b*2^j-1) <= Z_b <= lambda^(-j)(sqrt(B_(b*2^j))+3/100)^2.

The strict oriented comparison is Z_15/Z_3<19299/2500<193/25. For every fixed nonnegative integer d, lambda^(-j)B_(b*2^j-1-d) also converges to Z_b, over indices where it is defined.

The following further assertions are conditional. Suppose that a=(a_0,a_1,a_2,a_3) consists of four nonnegative real steps with s=sum_(i=0)^3 a_i>=168/25, and put A=1+s. Suppose there exist twenty nonnegative real multipliers Lambda_ij, for distinct i,j in {0,1,2,3,4}, with Lambda_ii=0, satisfying the following FULL five-gradient certificate. Let e=(1,1,1,1,1)^T, let T_ii=1, T_it=a_t for t<i, and T_it=0 for t>i, and define

    L_flow=diag(Lambda e)-Lambda,
    omega=(-2a_0,-2a_1,-2a_2,-2a_3,2s)^T,
    D_prim=diag(a_0(2-a_0),a_1(2-a_1),a_2(2-a_2),a_3(2-a_3),-s(s+2)).

Assume L_flow^T e=omega and M_prim=D_prim+(L_flow T+T^T L_flow^T)/2 is positive semidefinite on all of R^5. In particular no equality between formal gradients is imposed at zero steps, and no coordinate or interpolation-pair support is deleted.

For each n>=0 choose any fully expanded maximizing tree of the ORIGINAL B_n recurrence, with every C child expanded by the original C recurrence. Such a tree has n primitive empty leaves and one terminal dominant empty leaf. Replace each primitive empty leaf by the actual four-step word a, retain every internal node, and leave the terminal dominant leaf empty. Recompute each actual bridge as follows: for a primitive--primitive join with child capacities A_left=1+sum(left) and A_right=1+sum(right), choose d_pp>0 solving d_pp(A_left+A_right+d_pp)=A_left*A_right and concatenate (left,1+d_pp,right); for a primitive--dominant join with left capacity A_left and right mass D_right=1+2sum(right), put zeta=(1+sqrt(1+8A_left/D_right))/2 and concatenate (left,1+A_left/zeta,right). Denote the resulting actual schedule by Hsub_n and its mass by Dsub_n=1+2sum Hsub_n.

Then length(Hsub_n)=5n, its full all-function objective certificate gives W_(5n)(Hsub_n)=1/Dsub_n, and

    (sqrt(A B_n)-(sqrt(A)-1))^2 <= Dsub_n <= A B_n.

The equality in W is attained by a one-dimensional Huber function whose initial distance to its unique minimizer is1. For every positive integer b,

    lim_(j->infinity) B_(5(b*2^j-1)) W_(5(b*2^j-1))(Hsub_(b*2^j-1)) = Z_(5b)/(A Z_b).

In particular, for EVERY integer j>=25, with n_j=3*2^j-1 and N_j=5n_j=15*2^j-5,

    W_(N_j)(Hsub_(n_j)) < (1-1/50000) U_(N_j)^ZJ.

This theorem does not assert existence of the four-step word and multipliers in its hypothesis, does not assert their impossibility, and does not resolve the original unrestricted asymptotic minimax question.
