<!-- BEGIN_SOURCE_BODY DOC-002321 -->
# Independent audit of gradient-projection simplex universality

**UNVERIFIED ANALYTICAL AUDIT. No fact submission or verifier acceptance.**

Project: `gd-exact-profile-20260926`.
Allocation: `8ef0262318ccf848`, ONE native analytical invocation.
Requested model/effort: `gpt-6-astra/ultra`. The parent dispatch owns serving
metadata; this invocation does not independently expose backend metadata.
Original exact-profile minimax Goal: **OPEN**.

## Verdict and exact evidence boundary

No blocking mathematical gap was found in the frozen candidate's equality of
finite-word suprema. In particular, its strict-margin lift, independent-coordinate
simplex perturbation, and normalization by the complete polar minimum cone
survive the checks below. This is an independent UNVERIFIED assessment and
self-contained analytical argument, not a new accepted project fact.

The elementary quantitative bridge is valid: a bound at the single terminal
parameter `d=1`, uniform over the required simplex instances and all starts,
would imply the stated full-function `W` bound. An all-`d` propagation theorem
is not needed for that implication. Neither endpoint nor all-`d` inequality is
proved here. The representation alone supplies no rate or benchmark separation.

The complete frozen source read was
`research/direct_sparse_polytope_lift_20261003/RESULT_UNVERIFIED.md`, 27346 bytes,
SHA256 `29095a40ec7ffc4a27a2a5ada51719542f8b9359ca978b768970dd37eb855f72`.
Its older allocation and its self-description are evidence, not authorization
or correctness authority. The exact current `ALLOCATION.json`, current task,
operator profile, complete problem, and relevant public shared material were
read. `TASK.md` has SHA256
`2a1ed0be536e5f157448681e17ad74e4462c1cf34ab43f9c2d7930c7f7952239`.

Accepted local premises were read at the scopes used:

- `1252b8823bb5a290`: interpolation necessity in proof paragraph 1. The scaled
  inequality needed below is also derived directly; no SDP duality, terminal
  deletion, or attainment conclusion is imported.
- `931b9653ce0f7201`: its full statement and proof were read. Only its precise
  same-enclosing-`m` family and accepted bound `B_(n_m)/D_m<47/50` are used for
  the conditional implication in Section 10.
- `d8b59c7d92425121`: its statement and defining gradient formula were checked
  to keep its different representation class separate. It is not a premise
  of the simplex construction.

## 1. Statement being audited and the correct quotient

Fix a finite integer `n>=0` and a predetermined word
`h=(h_0,...,h_(n-1))` with finite `h_i>=0`. Put

    S=sum_(i<n) h_i,       p=#{i<n:h_i>0}.

At advertised smoothness one, define `W_n(h)` exactly as in the original
problem, including all finite dimensions, all convex differentiable functions
with gradient Lipschitz constant at most one and an attained minimum, and
every selected minimizer in the denominator.

The audited restricted class consists of

    D=conv{0,w_1,...,w_m} in R^m,
    1<=m<=p+1, with w_1,...,w_m linearly independent,
    F_D(x)=max_(g in D){<x,g>-||g||^2/2}.

The simplex is full-dimensional in its stated Euclidean space. No uniform
angle, aspect ratio, vertex norm, inradius, or bound on the start is imposed.
Its entire minimum set is retained. The audited assertion is

    W_n^simplex(h)=W_n(h).                              (R)

This is equality of suprema for EACH fixed finite word. It does not assert
exact finite-parameter representation of every original run, attainment in
the simplex class, or a single approximation uniform over words or instances.

Let `C_D=argmin F_D`. For an initial point outside `C_D`, the largest
nonnegative payoff over choices of minimizer is attained by a closest one:

    2F_D(T_h x)/dist(x,C_D)^2.

Indeed all selected-minimizer denominators are at least this squared distance,
and a closest point exists. If `x in C_D`, every actual GD iterate stays at
`x` and has zero value. Such starts contribute only zero whenever a legal
nonidentical selected minimizer exists; no quotient `0/0` is formed. Thus
the positive part of the restricted supremum can be taken using the exact
closest-minimum distance without changing the original definition.

The full fixed-word supremum is finite. After translating a selected optimum
to zero, `||grad f(x)||<=||x||` and `2f(x)<=||x||^2`; hence

    W_n(h)<=product_(i<n)(1+h_i)^2<infinity.

There are positive legal restricted scores for every word: on `D=[0,1]`,
start at `1+S`. All sampled positions are at least one, every gradient used
is one, and the terminal value is `1/2`. This also covers the empty word.

## 2. Projection gradient and exact minimum cone

For a nonempty compact CONVEX set `D`, completing the square shows that the
unique maximizer defining `F_D(x)` is the Euclidean projection `P_D(x)`.
For `v in D`, the exact characterization is

    P_D(x)=v iff <x-v,w-v><=0 for every w in D.           (2.1)

Necessity follows by differentiating squared distance along segments in `D`.
For sufficiency expand `||x-w||^2` about `v`. When `D` is a finite convex
hull it suffices to test every generator, since the normal expression is
affine in `w`.

Adding the two projection inequalities at `x,x'` gives

    <x-x',P_D(x)-P_D(x')>>=||P_D(x)-P_D(x')||^2.

Therefore `P_D` is 1-Lipschitz. Evaluating each affine maximum at the other's
optimizer gives

    0<=F_D(x')-F_D(x)-<P_D(x),x'-x>
      <=<P_D(x')-P_D(x),x'-x><=||x'-x||^2.

This proves differentiability with gradient `P_D`, and hence global
1-smoothness. Convexity follows from the maximum of affine functions.

When `0 in D`, the exact minimum value is zero and

    C_D={a:<a,g><=0 for every g in D}.                  (2.2)

If `a` obeys these inequalities, every defining term is nonpositive and the
zero generator attains zero. Conversely, if `<a,g>>0`, convexity and `0 in D`
allow `theta*g in D` for arbitrarily small positive `theta`; its objective
term is positive for sufficiently small `theta`. Thus no other point is a
minimizer. `C_D` is the closed convex POLAR CONE, with threshold zero.

Every `x` has a closest point in `C_D`: a distance-minimizing sequence can be
confined to a bounded ball, then compactness and closedness give a minimizer;
strict convexity gives uniqueness. Throughout this audit the exact radius is

    r_D(x)=dist(x,C_D),

not distance to the origin, not a body polar with threshold one, and not
distance to a single selected minimizer unless that point is actually closest.
Convexity is essential to the projection-gradient and minimum-cone claims;
the task's definition of `U_d` makes sense for any compact set, but its
smooth-function application here is to compact convex sets, especially
simplices. No claim about arbitrary nonconvex compact sets is being added.

## 3. Strict interpolation rows and degeneracies

Take a convex differentiable `ell`-smooth source with `0<ell<1`, translated
so that `f(0)=min f=0`. Retain its ACTUAL samples `i=0,...,n`, with

    x_(i+1)=x_i-h_i g_i,      g_i=grad f(x_i),
    f_i=f(x_i),              (x_*,g_*,f_*)=(0,0,0).

Define, with this orientation,

    Q_ji=f_j-f_i+<g_j,x_i-x_j>+||g_j-g_i||^2/2,
    gamma=(1-ell)/(2ell)>0.

To check the sign independently, apply the `ell`-smooth descent inequality
to `k(x)=f(x)-<g_j,x>` from `x_i` with displacement
`-(g_i-g_j)/ell`. Convexity and `grad k(x_j)=0` give

    k(x_j)<=k(x_i-(g_i-g_j)/ell)
           <=k(x_i)-||g_i-g_j||^2/(2ell),

so

    Q_ji<=-gamma||g_j-g_i||^2.                         (3.1)

This holds for every ordered pair, including both directions involving the
optimum and the original terminal sample. Put

    eta_i=<x_i,g_i>-f_i-||g_i||^2/2,   eta_*=0,
    H_i=sum_(t<i) h_t eta_t.

The two optimum rows are, distinctly,

    Q_(i,*)=-eta_i,
    Q_(*,i)=-f_i+||g_i||^2/2.

Consequently

    eta_i>=gamma||g_i||^2,
    f_i-||g_i||^2/2>=gamma||g_i||^2.                    (3.2)

Every nonzero gradient therefore has positive height. If `g_i=0`, convexity
makes `x_i` a minimizer, so `f_i=eta_i=0`; subsequent positions remain at
that possibly nonzero minimizer and all subsequent gradients are zero.

If `g_i=g_j=g`, the two ordinary convexity inequalities yield
`f_i-f_j=<g,x_i-x_j>`. Thus `eta_i=eta_j` and `Q_ji=0`. Equal gradients
need NOT imply equal positions. Identifying their vertex label is valid;
discarding their distinct physical time rows would not be valid. This check
does not use strong convexity or injectivity of the source gradient.

## 4. Moving-coordinate lift and all strict normal margins

For a finite positive `R`, set

    v_i=(g_i,eta_i/R),       v_*=0,
    z_i=(x_i,-R-H_i/R),     a_R=(0,-R),
    D_R=conv{0,v_0,...,v_n}.

Direct expansion, without changing either ordered index, gives

    <z_i-v_i,v_j-v_i>
      =<x_i-g_i,g_j-g_i>-(eta_j-eta_i)
         -(H_i+eta_i)(eta_j-eta_i)/R^2
      =Q_ji-(H_i+eta_i)(eta_j-eta_i)/R^2.             (4.1)

This includes the reference zero vertex. The sign of the added term is
not automatically favorable, so a simultaneous finite guard is required.
For different gradients define

    A=max_(0<=i<=n, j in {*,0,...,n}, g_i!=g_j)
          (H_i+eta_i)(eta_i-eta_j)_+/(-Q_ji),

and set the maximum of an empty set to zero. Nonnegative steps give
`H_i+eta_i>=0`; all included denominators are strictly positive by (3.1).
There are finitely many finite rows, so `A` is finite.

For `R^2>=A`, all normals in (4.1) are nonpositive. Equal-gradient rows
are identically zero, including repeated zero gradients. Thus
`P_(D_R)(z_i)=v_i` for EVERY physical index, and

    z_(i+1)=z_i-h_i v_i

holds exactly because `H_(i+1)=H_i+h_i eta_i`. These are actual iterates
of one static function, not a sequence of independently selected faces.
The vertical coordinate moves; freezing it would invalidate this proof.

The selected nearby minimizer is legal:

    <a_R,v_i>=-eta_i<=0,
    ||z_0-a_R||=||x_0||.

Evaluation at the actual maximizing vertex gives

    F_(D_R)(z_i)=f_i-C_i/R^2,
    C_i=eta_i H_i+eta_i^2/2>=0.                        (4.2)

For the simplex step, assume some sampled gradient is nonzero. List the
distinct nonzero gradients as `g^1,...,g^m`, use their common heights
`eta^1,...,eta^m`, and set `g^0=eta^0=0`. Let `a(i)` denote each sample's
label and put

    delta=min_(0<=a<b<=m)||g^a-g^b||>0,
    v^b=(g^b,eta^b/R).

Choose `R^2>=max(1,2A)`. The possible positive correction in (4.1) is at
most half of `-Q_ji`; if its sign is favorable this estimate remains true.
For every physical `i` and every different label `b`, therefore,

    N_ib:=<z_i-v^(a(i)),v^b-v^(a(i))>
         <=-gamma*delta^2/2.                          (4.3)

For the same label the row is exactly zero. Choosing any representative of
label `b` is legitimate because the normal expression depends only on its
gradient and height; ALL physical source indices `i` are still retained.
For `a(i)=0,b!=0`, the correction in (4.1) is `-H_i eta^b/R^2<=0`.
Thus zero-gradient rows introduce no unprotected equality against another
vertex. In particular strictness has not been inferred from genericity.

## 5. Nonnegative chronological perturbation and simplex geometry

Append mutually orthogonal coordinates `e_1,...,e_m`, set `e_0=0`, and
choose a strictly positive `epsilon`. Define

    w^b=(v^b,epsilon e_b),
    D=conv{0,w^1,...,w^m},
    b_i=sum_(t<i)h_t e_(a(t)),
    Z_i=(z_i,-epsilon b_i),       A_R=(a_R,0).

The zero-gradient label remains the zero vertex and receives no nonzero
perturbation. Every repeated nonzero gradient receives one fixed coordinate,
shared by all its physical occurrences. The proposed trajectory satisfies

    Z_0=(x_0,-R,0),
    Z_(i+1)=Z_i-h_i w^(a(i)).                          (5.1)

With `a=a(i)`, the exact new normal is

    <Z_i-w^a,w^b-w^a>
      =N_ib-epsilon^2<b_i+e_a,e_b-e_a>.                (5.2)

There is no linear-in-`epsilon` term. Write `b_(i,a)=<b_i,e_a>` for
`a>0` and `b_(i,0)=0`. The coefficient of `epsilon^2` in (5.2) is

    0                       for a=b;
    b_(i,a)-b_(i,b)+1        for a,b>0 and a!=b;
    b_(i,a)+1               for a>0, b=0;
    -b_(i,b)                for a=0, b>0.

Every `b_(i,a)` is between zero and `S`. Thus this coefficient is at most
`S+1`. This is where nonnegative steps protect the chronological estimate,
and the sample-to-zero row is explicitly among the potentially positive
perturbations. If

    0<epsilon^2<=gamma*delta^2/[4(S+1)],                (5.3)

every different-label normal is at most `-gamma*delta^2/4`, whereas every
same-label normal remains exactly zero. The projection test proves

    P_D(Z_i)=w^(a(i)) for every i=0,...,n.

Together with (5.1), this proves the actual GD trajectory of the single
fixed `F_D`, including its terminal value. It retains the entire original
horizontal strict-source trajectory before compression.

The vectors `w^1,...,w^m` are independent: a relation projected to the
new coordinate block has coefficients `epsilon` times the original ones.
Hence the body is an `m`-simplex in its span, with zero a vertex. No claim
that the unperturbed `v^b` are independent was used.

The minimizer and value formulas are exact:

    <A_R,w^b>=-eta^b<=0,
    ||Z_0-A_R||=||x_0||,
    F_D(Z_i)=f_i-C_i/R^2-epsilon^2 beta_i,
    beta_i=<b_i,e_(a(i))>+||e_(a(i))||^2/2.             (5.4)

If `a(i)=0`, then `beta_i=C_i=0`; otherwise `0<beta_i<=S+1/2`.
For any positive desired value loss `tau`, the simultaneous choices

    R^2>=max(1,2A,2C_n/tau),
    0<epsilon^2<=min{
       gamma*delta^2/[4(S+1)], tau/[2(S+1/2)]}

are finite and possible and give terminal value at least `f_n-tau`.
Every normal guard still holds. The required choices may be arbitrarily
ill-conditioned from one instance to another; this does not obstruct their
existence for the fixed finite transcript.

## 6. Compression and the exact polar-minimum radius

Let `E=span{w^1,...,w^m}`. The body lies in `E`, so for every ambient `Z`

    F_D(Z)=F_D(P_E Z),
    P_D(Z)=P_D(P_E Z),

by orthogonal decomposition in both the inner product and squared distance.
Set `q_i=P_E Z_i` and `a=P_E A_R`. Projection of the exact updates gives
the actual same-word trajectory in `E`, with identical gradients and values.
An ORTHONORMAL identification of `E` with `R^m` makes `D` a full-dimensional
simplex without changing any Euclidean quantity. An arbitrary affine
identification with a standard simplex would not suffice.

Let

    C_E={a in E:<a,w^b><=0 for every b}.

The FULL ambient minimum cone is exactly `C_E+E^perp`: the constraints
depend only on the `E` component. Consequently

    dist(Z_0,C_E+E^perp)^2
      =inf_(a in C_E,u in E^perp)
          {||P_E Z_0-a||^2+||P_(E^perp)Z_0-u||^2}
      =dist(q_0,C_E)^2.                               (6.1)

This is the exact closest-minimum radius before and after compression.
The explicit selected minimizer satisfies

    <a,w^b>=-eta^b<=0,
    ||q_0-a||=||P_E(Z_0-A_R)||<=||x_0||.               (6.2)

Suppose `tau<f_n`. The resulting terminal value is positive. A start in
the minimum cone has zero projection gradient and stays a minimizer for
every step, so such a positive terminal value forces

    r:=dist(q_0,C_E)>0.

Equations (6.1)-(6.2) show `r<=||x_0||`. Therefore

    2F_D(q_n)/r^2>=2(f_n-tau)/||x_0||^2.               (6.3)

The lower numerator is positive BEFORE the smaller denominator is used.
The source does not assert `r=||x_0||`, or convergence of `r` to that
number. Neither is required. Ignoring the polar cone or using the large
coordinate `R` in the denominator would destroy the intended transfer.

Finally, at most `p+1` positions are sampled: each zero step leaves the
position unchanged and each positive step adds at most one position.
The number `m` of distinct nonzero sampled gradients therefore satisfies
`m<=p+1`, including the terminal gradient. The simplex has exactly `m+1`
vertices and dimension exactly `m`. The old large ambient source dimension
disappears under this orthogonal compression.

## 7. Regularization, order of quantifiers, and boundary cases

Normalize any original source by translation and division by `L`, using
its selected minimizer, not necessarily its closest one. Now `f(0)=0`,
smoothness is at most one, the selected radius is `r_0=||x_0||>0`, and the
word is unchanged. Consider first a source with positive terminal value.

For `0<c<1`, use `f_c=c f` and its actual same-word run `x_i^c` from the
same start. Its advertised strict smoothness is `ell=c`, and its minimum
set is unchanged. With the ORIGINAL finite samples put

    M=max_(0<=i<=n)||grad f(x_i)||,
    Pi_i=product_(t<i)(1+h_t),
    B_i=(1-c)M(Pi_i-1).

Subtracting the two actual updates gives

    ||x_(i+1)^c-x_(i+1)||
      <=(1+h_i)||x_i^c-x_i||+(1-c)h_i M,

so finite induction proves `||x_i^c-x_i||<=B_i`. The original terminal
convexity and smoothness inequalities give

    |f_c(x_n^c)-f(x_n)|
      <=(1-c)f(x_n)+c||grad f(x_n)||B_n+c B_n^2/2
      ->0 as c increases to 1.                        (7.1)

No derivative with respect to the trajectory or uniform stability across
words is assumed. The coefficient `Pi_n` may be enormous but is finite.

Fix any original positive score and any score tolerance `zeta>0`. Choose
`0<d<min(f(x_n),zeta*r_0^2/2)`. First choose `c<1` with the value error
in (7.1) less than `d/2`. Then this particular strict-source transcript
has positive terminal value. Choose `0<tau<d/2`, and choose `R` and a
positive `epsilon` using its finite constants in Section 5. Compress last.
By (6.3) the resulting legal simplex score satisfies

    2F_D(q_n)/dist(q_0,C_E)^2
      >=2(f(x_n)-d)/r_0^2
      >2f(x_n)/r_0^2-zeta.                             (7.2)

The precise order is

    fixed n,h;
    arbitrary finite-dimensional source and selected minimizer;
    tolerance; c and its actual finite run;
    finite R; positive epsilon; orthogonal span compression.

The approximation parameters and number of distinct labels may depend on
the source, word, and tolerance. The dimension bound `m<=p+1` does not.
For each original score, the SAME restricted supremum bounds the right
side of (7.2) for every tolerance. Letting that scalar tolerance decrease
to zero and then taking the supremum of original scores proves
`W_n(h)<=W_n^simplex(h)`. The converse follows because every restricted
function is a legal original function. This proves (R) at the analytical
candidate level, without interchanging a dimension supremum with a limit
or requiring one uniform approximation of all sources.

At general `L>0`, multiply the constructed objective by `L`. Its gradient
is `L P_D`; steps `h_i/L` give the same trajectory and the normalized
payoff remains `2F_D(q_n)/r^2`. The factor `W=2R` is unchanged.

Boundary audit:

- Empty and all-zero words have `p=0`. A positive score has at most one
  nonzero gradient label, and the construction is a one-dimensional
  segment after compression. Both chronological sums are zero.
- Zero steps inside a word preserve positions and labels; they change
  neither chronological sum. The terminal sample is still retained.
- Equal gradients at different positions have equal heights; one vertex
  suffices, but the row for EACH occurrence and its own accumulated sums
  was checked. No false strictness is assigned to the identical vertex.
- If a zero gradient occurs, all subsequent source and lifted gradients
  are zero and the runs are stationary. Positive-terminal runs therefore
  avoid this case; its normal rows remain valid for the general formulas.
- An all-zero-gradient transcript has zero score. Its sparse body `{0}`
  need not be a positive-dimensional simplex: it is unnecessary for the
  positive-score approximation, and the restricted supremum is nonnegative.
- Arbitrarily large finite steps, small gradient separation, and small
  strict-smoothness margin are allowed. They may worsen `R` and `epsilon`,
  but no lower conditioning bound or upper scale bound appears in the
  definition of the restricted supremum.

No blocking inference was found. A bound proved only for well-conditioned
simplices, bounded starts, a fixed dimension independent of `n`, orthogonal
vertices, or a selected subset of physical time rows would NOT transfer
through this representation. The candidate does not make any such restriction.

## 8. Separation from the accepted affine-height class

Here the objective and gradient are

    F_D(x)=max_(g in D){<x,g>-||g||^2/2},
    grad F_D(x)=P_D(x),
    argmin F_D=C_D.

Accepted `d8b59c7d92425121` instead uses

    Phi_(K,lambda)(X)
      =min_(Y in K){<lambda,Y>+||X-Y||^2/2},
    grad Phi_(K,lambda)(X)=X-P_K(X-lambda),

with strictly positive heights on every nonzero point of `K`, complete
minimum set `{0}`, and an orthogonality condition on its start. Its gradient
subtracts the shifted projection from `X`. These are different function families,
different dynamics, and different minimum sets. Equality of their separate
suprema to full `W`, at their respective evidence status, does not identify
their individual trajectories or transfer an unproved `U` law between them.
Neither representation implies a convergence rate by itself.

## 9. Elementary d=1 endpoint-to-full-W bridge

For a fixed compact convex `D` containing zero and any scalar `d>0`, define

    U_d^D(x)=max_(g in D){2<x,g>-d||g||^2}.

The superscript is kept in this section to emphasize that ONE body is used
for both the actual trajectory and both sides of any proposed inequality.
The maximization is attained; `U_d^D>=0`, and

    U_1^D(x)=2F_D(x).                                   (9.1)

Let `a` be the actual closest point to `x` in `C_D`. For EVERY `g in D`,
`<a,g><=0`, and completion of a square gives

    2<x,g>-d||g||^2
      <=2<x-a,g>-d||g||^2
      =||x-a||^2/d-d||g-(x-a)/d||^2
      <=dist(x,C_D)^2/d.

Thus

    U_d^D(x)<=dist(x,argmin F_D)^2/d.                   (9.2)

This is an exact elementary upper bound, not an assertion that every `D,x`
attains it. It uses the complete actual polar minimum cone. In particular,
it does not require `0` to be the closest minimizer.

For a fixed word `H` of length `n`, let

    T_H^D=(I-h_(n-1)P_D) composed with ...
                         composed with (I-h_0 P_D),
    S_H=sum H,           Delta_H=1+2S_H.

The empty composition is the identity. Assume, as an UNPROVED HYPOTHESIS,
that a finite constant `C>=0`, independent of the simplex and start, obeys

    for every full-dimensional origin-vertex simplex D in R^k,
      1<=k<=p(H)+1, and every actual start x in R^k,
    U_1^D(T_H^D x)<=C U_(Delta_H)^D(x).                (E_H)

All allowed simplex shapes and all starts must be included. For `x` outside
`C_D`, (9.1)-(9.2) give

    2F_D(T_H^D x)/dist(x,C_D)^2<=C/Delta_H.

For starts in `C_D`, the terminal value is zero. For any selected minimizer
the denominator can only be larger than the closest-distance denominator.
Taking the restricted supremum, then using (R), proves the CONDITIONAL
full-function estimate

    W_n(H)<=C/(1+2S_H).                                 (9.3)

Thus YES: the `d=1` endpoint hypothesis suffices. It is enough to prove
`(E_H)` on precisely the dimensions required by (R); a uniform all-dimension
simplex theorem would also suffice. The lack of a uniform lift condition
number is harmless only because `(E_H)` is quantified over ALL those bodies
and starts BEFORE taking their supremum.

The corresponding stronger all-`d` propagation conjecture would require,
on the same actual composition,

    U_d^D(T_H^D x)<=C U_(d+2S_H)^D(x)
    for every d>=1, every permitted D, and every x.     (P_H)

This includes `(E_H)` by setting `d=1`. The bridge above does not prove
`(P_H)`, does not recover other `d` from `(E_H)`, and does not require them.
For a recursive proof one may WANT control of trailing parameters at
internal nodes, but that is a method-specific stronger burden, not a
logical prerequisite for the final full-`W` implication. If an induction
uses factors at child nodes, its final accumulated factor must still
satisfy `(E_H)`; no per-level loss is silently discarded here.

This audit attempted neither `(E_H)` nor `(P_H)` for the all-depth family.
They remain missing quantitative inequalities, not consequences of (R).

## 10. Correct conditional minimax separation and eventual C0 alternative

Use accepted `931b9653ce0f7201` with its exact enclosing parameter. For each
integer `m>=1536`, set

    lambda=1+sqrt(2),
    epsilon_m=1/(4m), beta_m=1-epsilon_m,
    c_m=lambda(1+epsilon_m), a_m=beta_m/c_m,
    H_0^[m]=(),
    H_(j+1)^[m]=(H_j^[m],1+a_m*c_m^j,H_j^[m]), 0<=j<m,
    H_m=H_m^[m], n_m=2^m-1,
    S_m=sum H_m, D_m=1+2S_m,
    B_n=2S_bullet(n)+1=1/U_n^ZJ.

All children use the SAME `m,epsilon_m,beta_m,c_m,a_m`. This is not a
recursion that reselects the enclosing parameter at a child. Each word is
strictly positive, so the needed simplex dimensions at horizon `n_m` are
`1<=k<=n_m+1=2^m`. The accepted predecessor gives

    B_(n_m)/D_m<47/50 for every integer m>=1536.        (10.1)

Now assume the representation (R), and the following still-UNPROVED
uniform endpoint hypothesis:

    there exists a fixed C with 0<=C<=17/16 such that
    for every integer m>=1536, every origin-vertex full-dimensional
    simplex D in every dimension 1<=k<=2^m, and every x in R^k,
    U_1^D(T_(H_m)^D x)<=C U_(D_m)^D(x).                (10.2)

It would also be enough to have constants `C_m<=17/16`, each uniform over
the entire required instance class, for every such `m`. By Section 9,
`W_(n_m)(H_m)<=17/(16D_m)`. Admissibility of the word and (10.1) yield

    W_(n_m)^*/U_(n_m)^ZJ
      =B_(n_m) W_(n_m)^*
      <=B_(n_m) W_(n_m)(H_m)
      <=(17/16) B_(n_m)/D_m
      <(17/16)(47/50)=799/800.                         (10.3)

Since `n_m` tends to infinity, it follows CONDITIONALLY that

    liminf_(n->infinity) W_n^*/U_n^ZJ<=799/800<1.

This would be a negative resolution of the original limit-one question,
with the full function-class supremum controlled. At present (10.2) has
not been established, and (R) remains an unverified candidate, so this
audit does not establish that separation.

The correctly uniform vanishing-loss alternative is the following. Assume
there exist ONE absolute finite `C0>=0` and ONE finite integer `m0`, both
independent of simplex shape, dimension, start, and every other instance
parameter, such that for every integer `m>=max{1536,m0}` the SAME-`m` word obeys

    U_1^D(T_(H_m)^D x)<=(1+C0/m) U_(D_m)^D(x)

for all required simplices and starts. Then the endpoint bridge gives

    W_(n_m)(H_m)<=(1+C0/m)/D_m
      for all m>=max{1536,m0}.                         (10.4)

The same logical conclusion follows if (10.4) is proved directly as a
uniform full-class bound. Define the common integer cutoff

    M0=max{1536,m0,1,ceil(16C0)}.

For every `m>=M0`, `1+C0/m<=17/16`, so (10.3) holds for all such `m`.
There is no requirement that the bound already hold at `m=1536`, and the
audit does not assert that it does. An eventual uniform tail suffices for
the conditional liminf separation.

In fact this stronger vanishing-loss hypothesis also gives

    limsup_(m->infinity)
      [W_(n_m)^*/U_(n_m)^ZJ]<=47/50,
    hence liminf_(n->infinity) W_n^*/U_n^ZJ<=47/50<1,

because each sufficiently late term is less than
`(47/50)(1+C0/m)`. The limit inequality is non-strict at `47/50`.

An instance-dependent `C0(D,x,...)` or an instance-dependent threshold is
insufficient: it cannot be moved outside the supremum defining `W`, and
the adverse simplex dimension itself grows with `m`. Likewise a result
for fixed depth, a fixed body, a fixed start, a well-conditioned subfamily,
or separately reset child parameters does not supply (10.2) or (10.4).

This invocation used public text reads, text artifact writes, and file hashes.
No mathematical program, arithmetic/trajectory/recurrence replay, solver,
formal verifier call, fact submission, installation, consult, or subagent

Only `AUDIT.md` and `HANDOFF.md` in this allocation directory were written.

The requested one-shot analytical audit is complete. No blocking gap was

# Handoff: independent simplex-universality audit

**UNVERIFIED. ONE allocation complete. Original minimax Goal OPEN.**

Project `gd-exact-profile-20260926`; allocation `8ef0262318ccf848`.
Requested native model/effort `gpt-6-astra/ultra`; serving metadata belongs to
the parent dispatch. There was no formal gate, fact submission, or acceptance.
No renewal is authorized by this handoff.

## Result and frozen provenance

The independent analytical audit found **no blocking gap** in the frozen
candidate's finite-word equality

    W_n(h)=W_n^simplex(h)

for every fixed finite nonnegative word `h`. With `p` positive steps, the
restricted objectives are

    F_D(x)=max_(g in D){<x,g>-||g||^2/2},
    D=conv{0,w_1,...,w_k} in R^k,
    1<=k<=p+1, w_1,...,w_k linearly independent.

The full minimum cone is retained. The statement is equality of SUPREMA,
not exact finite representation of every source run or attainment inside
the restricted class. No rate follows from this representation alone.

Complete proof and audit: `AUDIT.md` in this directory, SHA256
`94c5941b661f0bd2d5a7090b34d53562ac5172644fa07cf1c2b1faf8174f2b59`.
The full audit was read back, followed by rereading its final small wording
corrections and hashing the corrected file.

Frozen source, preserved:
`research/direct_sparse_polytope_lift_20261003/RESULT_UNVERIFIED.md`,
27346 bytes, SHA256
`29095a40ec7ffc4a27a2a5ada51719542f8b9359ca978b768970dd37eb855f72`.
The full source was read. Current `TASK.md` SHA256:
`2a1ed0be536e5f157448681e17ad74e4462c1cf34ab43f9c2d7930c7f7952239`.
The exact `ALLOCATION.json` was read; no `.md` allocation was substituted.

## Mathematical checks that survived

For a strict source with smoothness `0<ell<1`, set

    Q_ji=f_j-f_i+<g_j,x_i-x_j>+||g_j-g_i||^2/2,
    gamma=(1-ell)/(2ell),
    eta_i=<x_i,g_i>-f_i-||g_i||^2/2,
    H_i=sum_(t<i)h_t eta_t.

Every ordered physical/optimum row satisfies
`Q_ji<=-gamma||g_j-g_i||^2`. Both optimum directions were checked:
`Q_(i,*)=-eta_i` and `Q_(*,i)=-f_i+||g_i||^2/2`.
Equal gradients force equal heights and a zero row, even at different
positions. Zero gradients have zero value and height and yield stationary
future samples. Strong convexity is not needed.

The moving-coordinate lift

    v_i=(g_i,eta_i/R),
    z_i=(x_i,-R-H_i/R)

has the exact normal

    <z_i-v_i,v_j-v_i>
      =Q_ji-(H_i+eta_i)(eta_j-eta_i)/R^2.

A finite data-dependent guard `A` controls every possibly positive
correction. `R^2>=max(1,2A)` gives a uniform strict margin over all
different gradient labels for this fixed transcript. All physical time
rows, including the terminal and both optimum directions, remain present.

Append one coordinate `epsilon e_a` per distinct nonzero gradient label,
with `e_0=0`. The actual appended positions are
`-epsilon b_i`, where `b_i=sum_(t<i)h_t e_(a(t))`. The exact new normal
correction is `-epsilon^2<b_i+e_(a(i)),e_b-e_(a(i))>`. Its coefficient is
at most `S+1`, including the sample-to-zero row. Equal-label rows stay
identically zero. Choosing a strictly positive sufficiently small
`epsilon` retains every normal guard and makes the nonzero vertices
linearly independent. Both the old and new added coordinates MOVE under
the real chronological updates.

The actual terminal value is

    f_n-C_n/R^2-epsilon^2 beta_n,
    C_n=eta_n H_n+eta_n^2/2>=0,
    0<=beta_n<=S+1/2.

Hence it can be made arbitrarily close from below, with positive terminal
value fixed before using any denominator comparison.

For `E=span{w_1,...,w_k}`, orthogonal compression preserves the values,
projection gradients, and actual same-word dynamics. With

    C_E={a in E:<a,w_j><=0 for every j},

the ambient minimum set is EXACTLY `C_E+E^perp`; therefore

    dist(Z_0,C_E+E^perp)=dist(P_E Z_0,C_E).

The true closest radius is positive for a positive terminal score and
at most the original selected radius, thanks to the legal nearby
minimizer `(0,-R,0)` before compression. It need not equal or converge to
the original radius. The dimension is at most `p+1`, because there are
at most `p+1` distinct sampled positions, including the terminal.

For an arbitrary 1-smooth source, run `c f`, `0<c<1`, under the SAME
word and start. Its actual finite terminal value tends to the original
one as `c` increases to one. The valid order is: fix word and source;
choose tolerance; choose `c` and its actual transcript; choose finite
`R`; choose positive `epsilon`; compress. No source-uniform conditioning
or interchange of supremum and horizon limit is used. Empty/all-zero
words, repeated gradients, zero steps, zero-gradient samples, and huge
finite steps were separately covered.

## The exact quantitative interface now required

For compact convex `D` containing zero, write

    C_D=argmin F_D={a:<a,g><=0 for all g in D},
    U_d^D(x)=max_(g in D){2<x,g>-d||g||^2}, d>0.

Then `U_1^D=2F_D`. If `a` is the actual closest minimum to `x`, completing
the square and using `<a,g><=0` proves

    U_d^D(x)<=dist(x,C_D)^2/d.

For a fixed word `H`, let `T_H^D` be its ACTUAL GD composition using
`P_D`, let `S=sum H`, and put `Delta=1+2S`. The sufficient UNPROVED
hypothesis is only

    U_1^D(T_H^D x)<=C U_Delta^D(x)

for every origin-vertex full-dimensional simplex in every dimension
`1<=k<=p(H)+1`, and EVERY start `x`, with the same `C` for all such
instances. It implies

    W_n(H)<=C/(1+2S),

using the audited representation. All actual polar minima must be
retained; no restriction on condition number, size, or initial point
is allowed. The `d=1` endpoint condition is sufficient.

The stronger all-`d` statement

    U_d^D(T_H^D x)<=C U_(d+2S)^D(x), for every d>=1,

would imply the endpoint condition, but is not required by this final
normalization argument. Neither condition was proved or attempted for
the all-depth family in this allocation. Per-level factors would have
to be paid in the final factor; representation cannot supply that payment.

## Exact conditional benchmark conclusion

Accepted `931b9653ce0f7201` fixes, for each integer `m>=1536`,

    lambda=1+sqrt(2), epsilon_m=1/(4m),
    beta_m=1-epsilon_m, c_m=lambda(1+epsilon_m),
    a_m=beta_m/c_m,
    H_0^[m]=(),
    H_(j+1)^[m]=(H_j^[m],1+a_m*c_m^j,H_j^[m]), 0<=j<m.

Set `H_m=H_m^[m]`, `n_m=2^m-1`, `D_m=1+2sum H_m`, and
`B_n=2S_bullet(n)+1=1/U_n^ZJ`. Every child retains the enclosing `m`.
The accepted comparison is `B_(n_m)/D_m<47/50` for every `m>=1536`.

IF the endpoint estimate holds for every such `m`, all simplices of
dimensions `1<=k<=2^m`, and all starts, with a constant `C<=17/16`
uniform over these instances, THEN

    W_(n_m)^*/U_(n_m)^ZJ
      <=B_(n_m)W_(n_m)(H_m)
      <=(17/16)B_(n_m)/D_m<799/800,

and `liminf_n W_n^*/U_n^ZJ<=799/800<1`. The inequality being assumed is
full-class after the endpoint bridge, not a subclass rate.

Alternatively, ONE absolute finite `C0>=0` and ONE common threshold `m0`
giving the all-instance endpoint estimate with factor `1+C0/m` imply
the full bound `(1+C0/m)/D_m` for all `m>=max{1536,m0}`. For

    m>=max{1536,m0,1,ceil(16C0)},

the factor is at most `17/16`, so the same conditional separation holds
eventually. In fact this vanishing-loss hypothesis gives the stronger
conditional statement `liminf_n W_n^*/U_n^ZJ<=47/50<1`; the limit bound
at `47/50` is non-strict. No claim is made that the bound starts at 1536
without the required threshold. Instance-dependent constants or
thresholds cannot be passed through the worst-case supremum.

## Scope, accepted facts, and stopping state

Accepted `1252b8823bb5a290` was read for interpolation necessity only;
its required scaled inequality was rederived. Accepted
`d8b59c7d92425121` is a distinct affine-height family:
`Phi(X)=min_(Y in K){<lambda,Y>+||X-Y||^2/2}` with gradient
`X-P_K(X-lambda)` and unique minimum zero. It is not the present
gradient-projection family with its polar minimum cone. Neither
representation alone proves a rate.

Only public text reads/writes/hashes were used. Mathematical programs,

The first still-open target-level interface is the uniform all-instance

 U_1(T_H x)<=C*U_(1+2S)(x), for all actual starts x,
 would imply W_n(H)<=C/(1+2S), since U_1=2F_D and U_(1+2S)(x)<=dist(x,argmin F_D)^2/(1+2S). # Independent analytical audit of the whole fixed odd-kink candidate

**SURVIVES THIS ANALYTICAL AUDIT — UNVERIFIED. No verifier acceptance, no new fact, and no minimax resolution.**

Project: `gd-exact-profile-20260926`.
Audit allocation: `8a44323dc3460ca4`; native auditor `/root/audit_whole_odd_kink`; one invocation allocated as `gpt-6-astra` / `ultra`.
Frozen candidate: `research/shared_evidence/8a5a6aef2db4f411.md`, 36262 bytes.

Date: 2026-10-03.

## 1. Verdict and exact scope

I independently rederived and attacked the mathematical dependency chain of the completed frozen report, including its new original-radius first-landing argument. I found no blocking inference and no legal counterexample to the stated restricted-family claim. In particular, the root case partition does not assume that the root preserves the core: it isolates that exceptional step, treats its exterior landing in the core-output branch, and only invokes later core absorption when every remaining physical step belongs to the right child.

This is a positive **UNVERIFIED analytical audit**, not a Danus correctness decision. The source is unchanged. No fact submission or verifier call occurred. The original minimax Goal remains **OPEN**.

The precise claim screened here is the following. Fix one integer `m >= 1536`, and keep its parameters at every child depth:

    epsilon = 1/(4m), beta = 1-epsilon,
    lambda = 1+sqrt(2), c = lambda*(1+epsilon), a = beta/c,
    d_j = a*c^j,
    H_0 = (), H_(j+1) = (H_j, 1+d_j, H_j),
    S_j = sum H_j, A_j = 1+S_j, S = S_m, D = 1+2S.

For any fixed `0 < kappa <= 1`, finite `B >= kappa`, and real nonzero original start `s`, use the single odd gradient

    v = 1-kappa, u = v+B,
    G(r) = min(B, max(kappa*r, r-v)),
    g(x) = sign(x)*G(|x|), f(x) = integral_0^x g(t) dt.

The actual entire `H_m` trajectory is claimed to obey

    2f(x_n)/s^2 <= (1+21/(4m))/D < 17/(16D).

No `kappa=0` case is included. The denominator is the square of the actual original distance to the unique minimizer 0. The claim covers this fixed odd scalar family only; it does not cover all scalar convex smooth functions, arbitrary asymmetric caps, or the full finite-dimensional class in `PROBLEM.md`. It does not discharge the full-class hypothesis referred to in the source as accepted fact `931b9653ce0f7201`. The assertion that that fact is accepted was not independently re-audited here and supplies no mathematical premise in this audit.

## 2. Dependency and circularity screen

The following order is sufficient and noncircular:

1. The legal objective, exact energy, same-ray bound, exact masses, and one-step branches are elementary independent inputs.
2. At depth `j+1`, the discrete-grid proof uses the depth-`j` grid and crossing plateau. The crossing plateau at any depth uses grid claims only at proper-child depths. It never uses the current-depth full-family theorem.
3. The grid gives global terminal-gradient control. The crossing plateau makes the terminal strictly pre-cap, permitting the quadratic energy conversion.
4. These two conclusions prove the auxiliary Huber objective bound directly. The exterior-only comparison then uses that already established Huber bound.
5. The large-curvature and no-inner-reversal regions use only those inputs. Their complement reduces to a single reversing root.
6. The root core-output gate and original-radius first-landing partition close that remaining band without invoking each other circularly.

No prior unverified report, numerical replay, full-class minimax statement, or stronger terminal-gradient theorem is needed. The source's earlier allocations and research directions were treated as evidence, not as authorization to open another mechanism.

## 3. Objective legality, energy, and the same-ray estimate

Because `B >= kappa` and `v >= 0`, the positive-ray law is exactly

    G(r) = kappa*r       for 0 <= r <= 1,
           r-v           for 1 <= r <= u,
           B             for r >= u.

The adjacent expressions agree at 1 and `u`; if `B=kappa`, the middle interval is empty. The odd extension is continuous and nondecreasing, with slopes in `{kappa,1,0}` on its affine pieces. Partitioning an arbitrary interval at the finitely many knots proves global 1-Lipschitz continuity. Integration therefore gives a finite convex differentiable 1-smooth objective. Since `kappa>0` and `B>=kappa>0`, `G(r)>0` for `r>0`; 0 is its unique minimizer.

Direct integration gives

    2F(r) = kappa*r^2                              for r <= 1,
            (r-v)^2 + kappa*v                      for 1 <= r <= u,
            2B*r - B^2 - v*(2B-kappa)              for r >= u,

where `F(r)=f(r)`. In particular, no cap-tail area can be discarded. The displayed values match at the knots.

The actual interpolation transcript is legal as well. For `x<y` and `Delta=g(y)-g(x)`, monotonicity and smoothness give `0<=Delta<=y-x` and

    g(t)-g(x) >= max(0, Delta-(y-t)),
    g(y)-g(t) >= max(0, Delta-(t-x)).

Each right side integrates to `Delta^2/2` on `[x,y]`. These are precisely the two ordered smooth-convex interpolation inequalities. Repeated positions cause no inconsistency; the actual scalar Gram matrix is positive semidefinite.

The inequalities used later hold in their stated domains:

    G(r) <= min(r,B),
    G(r) >= kappa*min(r,1),
    2F(r) <= 2r*p-p^2,                 p=G(r),
    2F(r) <= p^2/kappa                 only when r <= u.

For the third inequality, `G(t)<=min(t,p)` on `[0,r]` and `p<=r`; integrate that upper envelope. The last inequality is equality in the core. In the middle, subtracting the exact energy from `p^2/kappa` gives

    v*(p^2-kappa^2)/kappa >= 0,

since `p>=kappa`. This conversion is not extended into the unbounded constant-gradient tail.

Before the first strict sign crossing, a nonzero trajectory stays on one ray and its radii decrease. More generally, for a whole noncrossing segment of total mass `T`, original segment radius `R`, terminal radius `r`, and terminal gradient `p`, monotonicity gives

    R >= r + T*p,
    2F(r) <= 2R*p - (1+2T)*p^2 <= R^2/(1+2T).

A zero hit is absorbing. Thus every full-word history with no strict crossing is settled with constant `1/D`, including same-side entry into the core on its last physical step.

## 4. Same-enclosing-m constants and literal suffixes

The elementary bounds needed by the argument are consistent:

    0 < epsilon < 1/1000, beta > 999/1000,
    12/5 < c < 17/7 < 5/2,
    2/5 < a < 1/2, c*a=beta<1, beta>c-2.

For example, `7/5<sqrt(2)<17/12`, and
`(29/12)*(1001/1000)<17/7`; also
`a>(99/100)*(7/17)>2/5`.

Let `eta=a/(c-2)`. The identity `lambda*(lambda-2)=1` and `c>lambda` imply

    c*(c-2)>1>beta, hence 0<eta<1.

The exact recursion `A_(j+1)=2A_j+d_j`, `A_0=1` has the solution

    A_j = (1-eta)*2^j + eta*c^j.

Consequently,

    A_j > d_j/(c-2),
    A_j < c^j                         for j>=1,
    A_j > (c-2)*d_j,
    D < 2c^m = 2d_m/a.

The strict first inequality, including at `j=0`, is the source of the strict first-landing margin. It must not be weakened to an asymptotic equality.

For `L_j=S_j/d_j`, `L_1=(1+a)/beta>7/5`; and

    L_(j+1) = (2L_j+1+1/d_j)/c > 7/5

follows from `c<17/7`. Thus `L_j>7/5` for every positive child depth under the same enclosing `m`.

The finite margins also follow without executing a recurrence. Since `a>2/5` and `c>2`,

    d_(m-1)>2^m/5>4m=1/epsilon.

The second inequality follows from `2^m>20m` at `m=8` and elementary induction. At the actual `m>=1536`, this gives `d_(m-1)>9`, `S>1000`, and `d_m>2/a`. Hence

    d_m^2>D, D/S=2+1/S<201/100.

For any physical level-`k` bridge in a complete word, its chronological prefix contains its full immediate left `H_k`, so its prefix mass is at least `S_k`. Its actual suffix consists of its immediate right `H_k` followed by the bridge and full right child at precisely the ancestors entered through their left children. Their levels increase strictly. Ancestors entered through the right child contribute no steps. This proves the literal suffix decomposition used by the plateau argument; it does not insert omitted or fictitious children.

## 5. Exact one-step interval image and crossing price

For `h=1+d`, the positive-source signed output is

    (1-h*kappa)*r        in the core,
    h*v-d*r             in the middle,
    r-h*B               in the tail.

The exact image radius of `[-R,R]` is

    E_h(R) = max( |1-h*kappa|*min(R,1),
                  [d*min(R,u)-h*v]_+,
                  [R-h*B]_+ ).

On each present affine interval, extrema of the absolute value occur at endpoints. The positive middle endpoint is bounded by the core endpoint; the negative middle endpoint gives the second term. The negative tail endpoint is already represented at `u`; its positive endpoint gives the third term. Absent pieces do not invalidate the formula:

* If `R<1`, then `dR-hv <= (h*kappa-1)R`, because their difference is `hv(R-1)<=0`.
* If `R<u`, a positive formal tail term `R-hB` is bounded by the actual signed endpoint `R-hG(R)`.

Every signed step is continuous, odd, and fixes zero. Its image of a symmetric interval is therefore the entire symmetric interval with this radius. The complete-word radius satisfies the exact image composition

    M_(j+1) = M_j o E_(1+d_j) o M_j.

This is an interval-image identity, not a substitution of `R` for a particular trajectory's true original denominator.

At a strict crossing from radius `r`, with `p=G(r)`, the landing `z` satisfies

    z=(1+d)*p-r <= d*p.

A positive same-side output from a strictly pre-cap source is possible only when `h*kappa<1`, and lies in the core. Indeed the middle expression is

    1-h*kappa-d*(r-1) <= 1-h*kappa < 1;

inside the core the claim is immediate. In both cases the output is at most
`(1-h*kappa)*min(r,1)` and at most `(1-h*kappa)*r`. A cap-knot source can be assigned to the saturated branch; both formulas agree there.

If every step in a complete word obeys `h*kappa<=1`, the closed core is absorbing, with product at most `exp(-kappa*S_j)`. Therefore a first strict crossing in such a nonempty word must have exterior source. For an exterior first source,

    r >= v+p,
    R >= r+P*p >= v+A_k*p,
    crossing implies p>v/d_k.

Here `R` and `P` are measured from the start of that complete word. In all later uses that require the original denominator, that complete word is explicitly the original word or its original left prefix. With

    Q=(c-1)/(c-2)>10/3,

the preceding inequalities and `A_k>d_k/(c-2)` imply `R>vQ` whenever `v>0`. In a nonempty core-preserving word one indeed has `v>0`; the singular `v=0` endpoint is not used in this division argument.

## 6. Simultaneous grid and post-crossing plateau

The two simultaneous claims are

    G(M_j(d_j*c^(-q)*B)) <= a*c^(-q)*B   for every integer q>=0,
    any strict crossing in a complete H_j implies final G <= aB.

Throughout this induction, `B`, `kappa`, and the core knot 1 remain fixed. The grid does not replace the law's cap by a smaller amplitude.

For the plateau at depth `j`, a level-`k` crossing lands at radius at most `d_k B`. Its immediate `H_k` gives gradient at most `aB` by the proper-child grid; when `k=0`, the landing itself has gradient at most radius at most `aB`. At each retained later ancestor, the incoming gradient is below `B`, hence its source is strictly pre-cap. If the ancestor crosses, its landing is at most `d_l aB <= d_l B` and its full right `H_l` restores the plateau. If it stays on its side, its output is in the core, the bridge has `h*kappa<1`, and every smaller step in the following child is core preserving; the gradient cannot increase. Zero is absorbing. This proves the plateau using only proper-child grids and every actual suffix step.

For the grid base `j=1`, put `w=c^(-q)B` and `R=d_1 w=beta w<B`. There is no saturated source within this interval. Crossings land at radius at most `aR`; positive stays have gradient at most

    kappa*[1-(1+a)*kappa]_+*min(R,1) <= aR,

because `kappa*[1-(1+a)*kappa]_+ <= 1/[4(1+a)] < a`. Thus the base gives even `aR<aw`.

For the parent grid, start at any actual radius at most `c*d_j*w`, write `y` for the original left-child output radius, and split the root bridge:

**Crossing.** If `q=0`, `G(y)<=B=w`. If `q>=1`, the depth-`j`, index-`q-1` grid gives `G(y)<=a*c*w=beta*w<=w`. The landing is at most `d_j*w`; the complete right-child grid gives terminal gradient at most `a*w`.

**Positive saturated stay.** A crossing in the left child would give `G(y)<=aB<B`, contradicting `y>=u`. A zero hit is also impossible. The left child is therefore noncrossing, all its query radii are at least its saturated endpoint, and all its queried gradients equal `B`. Its bridge output is

    z = R-(A_j+d_j)B
      <= c*d_j*w-(A_j+d_j)B <= d_j*w.

The last inequality uses `w<=B` and `A_j>(c-2)d_j`. This derivation rules out left-child crossings before asserting an all-tail prefix.

**Positive pre-cap stay without a left-child crossing.** Let `t=kappa*d_j`, `L=S_j/d_j`. Positive stay forces `kappa*(1+d_j)<1`, so the right child remains in the core. The left-child monotone prefix gives

    R >= y+S_j*G(y) >= (1+kappa*S_j)y,

using `G(y)>=kappa*y` only at the pre-cap `y`. The actual terminal gradient is at most

    c*t*(1-t)*exp(-Lt)/(1+Lt)*w
      <= c*t*(1-t)/(1+Lt)^2*w
      <= c/[4(1+L)]*w < a*w.

Here `0<t<1`, `exp(Lt)>=1+Lt`, and the exact identity

    (1+Lt)^2-4(1+L)t(1-t) = (1-(L+2)t)^2

proves the last maximization inequality. The constants satisfy `c/[4(1+L)]<85/336<2/5<a`.

**Positive pre-cap stay with a left-child crossing.** The same root inequality makes both children core preserving. The left-child first crossing pays `R>vQ`. The bridge output is at most `v-t`, with `0<t<v`; the complete right child gives

    p_out <= kappa*(v-t)*exp(-Lt).

Since `R<=c*d_j*w` and `R>vQ`,

    p_out/w < c*t*(v-t)*exp(-Lt)/(vQ)
              <= c*v/(4Q) <= c/(4Q) < a.

The final inequality follows from `c/(4Q)<51/280<2/5<a`. No tail lower bound of the false form `G(r)>=kappa*r` is used.

A zero bridge output is already settled. The four nonzero cases exhaust the parent. This completes a noncircular simultaneous induction and confirms both claims at every required depth.

## 7. Global gradient control and the exterior-only Huber comparison

For `0<R<=d_jB`, choose the unique grid cell

    d_j*c^(-(q+1))*B < R <= d_j*c^(-q)*B.

The grid gives

    G(M_j(R)) <= a*c^(-q)*B < beta*R/d_j.

At an exact grid boundary, use it as the upper endpoint; this preserves the strict lower inequality. For `R>d_jB`, a crossing history uses the plateau `aB<beta*R/d_j`. A noncrossing history from actual `|s|<=R` obeys

    |s| >= r+S_j*p >= A_j*p,

so `p<=R/A_j<(c-2)R/d_j<beta R/d_j`. Maximizing over actual starts yields the non-strict uniform bound

    G(M_j(R)) <= beta*R/d_j.

Moreover, a crossing history has terminal gradient at most `aB<B`, which implies terminal radius strictly below `u`. This is the needed strict pre-cap conclusion, not merely a gradient estimate at a potentially saturated endpoint.

The auxiliary unit Huber law is obtained with `kappa=B=1`. Its crossing trajectories end with radius at most `a<1`, so their twice-energy equals squared terminal gradient. For original `R<=d_m`, the score is at most `beta^2/d_m^2<1/D`; for `R>d_m`, it is at most `a^2/R^2<1/D`. Noncrossing trajectories use the ray bound. Scaling the auxiliary objective by

    J_B(x)=B^2*J_1(x/B)

leaves the physical step word unchanged and proves

    2J_B(Psi_m^Huber(s)) <= s^2/D

for every `B>0`, including scaled caps outside the original `kappa=B=1` parameter choice. This scaling is solely an auxiliary comparison and never changes the actual kink law.

Now suppose every query and the terminal of the actual kink history have radius strictly larger than 1. At a crossing, its landing is at most `d*min(r,B)`. At a positive same-side stay, an unsaturated middle source is impossible: its output is at most `1-h*kappa<1`. Thus every same-side stay in this exterior-only history is saturated and equals `r-hB`. Both cases are bounded by

    V_(h,B)(r)=max(d*min(r,B), r-hB).

This map is nondecreasing and is exactly the Huber symmetric-interval image radius. On the Huber core the absolute endpoint is `d*min(r,B)`; on its tail the additional positive endpoint is `r-hB`, while any negative tail endpoint is already bounded by `dB`.

Composing these exact image maps through every actual physical step bounds the actual kink terminal radius by the full Huber interval-image radius from `[-R,R]`. Continuity and oddness at every step ensure that this radius is attained by an actual Huber start `s_*` with `|s_*|<=R`. Since `F<=J_B` pointwise,

    2F(r_n) <= 2J_B(|Psi_m^Huber(s_*)|)
             <= s_*^2/D <= R^2/D.

This derivation imposes **no no-inner-reversal hypothesis**. Its premise is the exterior-only actual history; the hypothetical behavior of the root on core inputs is irrelevant. The auxiliary interval radius is converted back to the true original denominator by `s_*^2<=R^2`, not silently identified with it.

## 8. Large curvature mass and the no-inner-reversal region

For `kappa*S>=12`, noncrossing histories use the ray estimate. Crossing histories are strictly pre-cap and use the global gradient bound and the permitted energy conversion:

    D*2F(r_n)/R^2
      <= D*beta^2/(kappa*d_m^2)
       = (D/S)*(beta*S/d_m)^2/(kappa*S)
       < (201/100)*(17/7)^2/12
       = 58089/58800 < 1.

Here `beta*S/d_m<c` follows from `A_m<c^m=d_m/a`. This case has no restriction on earlier crossings, core visits, or cap ties.

Next assume `kappa*(1+d_(m-1))<=1`. The whole word preserves the core. Histories with no crossing use the ray bound; crossing histories never entering the closed core are exterior-only and use the Huber comparison. If a crossing history does enter the core, its first crossing has exterior source, pays `R>vQ`, and its actual terminal has `2F(r_n)<=kappa`. Therefore

    2F(r_n)/R^2 < kappa/(v^2 Q^2).

Put `d=d_(m-1)` and `t_c=c*(c-2)`. Monotonicity of `z/(1-z)^2` gives

    kappa/v^2 <= (1+d)/d^2.

Using `(c-1)^2=t_c+1`, `D<2c^m`, and `a=beta/c`,

    D*kappa/(v^2 Q^2)
      < [2*t_c^2/(beta*(t_c+1))]*(1+1/d).

The expansion

    t_c = 1+2*(lambda+1)*epsilon+lambda^2*epsilon^2

shows `1<t_c<1+8epsilon`. As `d>1/epsilon`, the preceding coefficient is less than

    (1+8epsilon)^2*(1+epsilon)/(1-epsilon).

The exact difference

    (1-epsilon)*(1+19epsilon)-(1+8epsilon)^2
      = 2epsilon-83epsilon^2 > 0

proves the upper bound `(1+19epsilon)*(1+epsilon)<=1+21epsilon`. The latter inequality uses `19epsilon<=1`, which holds with ample margin. Hence the no-inner-reversal region satisfies `(1+21epsilon)/D`, and `21epsilon<=21/6144<1/16` proves the advertised strict `17/16` envelope.

This argument includes entry into the core on the final step and uses no nonexistent contraction factor after that entry.

## 9. Exhaustive reduction to the original root

The remaining parameters satisfy

    kappa*S < 12,
    kappa*(1+d_(m-1)) > 1.

For `d=d_(m-1)`, the exact mass inequality gives

    kappa*S > S/(1+d)
      > [c*d/(c-2)-1]/(1+d) > 5.

Indeed `c/(c-2)>17/3` and `d>9` imply
`(c/(c-2)-5)d>6`, exactly the condition for the last comparison.

On `12/5<c<17/7`,

    (c-2)/c^2 < 3/40.

To check it, `3c^2-40c+80` decreases there and has value `27/49>0` at `17/7`. Since `d_m<(c-2)(S+1)`,

    kappa*(1+d_(m-2))
      < 12*[1/S+(3/40)*(1+1/S)]
       = 9/10+129/(10S) < 1.

Thus with `J=m-1`, the original root is the sole physical step that can reverse a core state. Both complete children `H_J` have individually core-preserving steps. The same enclosing `m` is used everywhere. The residual-band bounds are

    v>49/50,
    D*kappa < (201/100)*12 = 603/25 < 25.

They are uniform in `B`, the initial radius, and `kappa` within this band. Equality at the root nonreversal threshold is assigned to the previous region, and equality at `kappa*S=12` is assigned to the large-curvature region; no parameter boundary is lost.

## 10. The original left child ends in the core

Let `y=|Psi_J(s)|<=1`. Zero is settled immediately. For positive `y`, put

    d=d_J, L=S_J/d_J, t=kappa*d,
    q=kappa*(1+d)-1=t-v>0.

The root reverses and lands at actual radius `z=q*y`, which is permitted to exceed 1. Since the largest child level is nonreversing,

    v<t<=c*v,
    q<(3/2)*v,
    q<(3/5)*t,
    L>7/5.

The complete right child cannot strictly cross: if it did, its own first-crossing price would require its starting radius `z>vQ`, whereas `z<=q<3v/2<vQ`. This is a legitimate application of the price to the right child, used solely to rule out its crossing; it is not substituted for the original denominator.

That child must return to the closed core by its actual terminal. Otherwise its monotone positive radii and every queried gradient at least `kappa` would imply

    r_n <= z-kappa*S_J <= q-Lt < 0,

which is impossible. This already checks the potentially exterior root landing, including a cap or a collapsed-middle law.

A quantitative comparison retains the entire return and the entire remaining core portion. For every physical right-child step set

    alpha=h*kappa in [0,1],
    C_h(r)=r-alpha*min(r,1).

This function is nonnegative, continuous, and nondecreasing. Along the noncrossing actual child,
`r_next=r-hG(r)<=C_h(r)`. Monotonicity lets its chronological composition dominate the actual trajectory.

Define the increasing function

    b(r)=log r   for 0<r<=1,
         r-1     for r>=1.

For positive comparison output,

    b(r-alpha*min(r,1)) <= b(r)-alpha.

Inside the core this is `log(1-alpha)<=-alpha`. Outside with outside output it is equality. At an outside-to-core transition it is `log w<=w-1`. A zero output is absorbing. Since `b(z)<Lt`, summing through **every actual right-child step** gives a final comparison radius below 1 and at most

    K(z)*exp(-Lt),
    K(z)=z for z<=1, K(z)=exp(z-1) for z>=1.

For `0<=y<=1`, `K(qy)<=y*K(q)`. If `q<=1`, equality holds. If `q>1` and `qy<=1`, use `q<=exp(q-1)`. If `qy>=1`, the function `exp(qy-1)/y` has derivative with the sign of `qy-1` and is increasing up to `y=1`. Thus

    r_n <= y*Z, Z=K(q)*exp(-Lt).

The uniform estimate `Z<1/4` is valid. If `q<=1`, then `Lt>7q/3` and

    Z < q*exp(-7q/3) <= 3/(7e) < 1/4.

If `q>1`, then

    Z < exp(-1-4q/3) < exp(-7/3) < 1/4.

Only elementary differentiation and `e>2` are used, not a numerical maximization.

The original-radius payment is separate. If the original left child had no crossing,

    R >= (1+kappa*S_J)*y = (1+Lt)*y,

and `Lt>(7/5)*(49/50)>1`, so `y/R<1/2`. If it had a crossing, its absorbing core forces its first source exterior and gives the original `R>vQ>49/15>3`; as `y<=1`, `y/R<1/3`. The final radius is in the core, so

    D*2F(r_n)/R^2 < 25/64 < 1/2       in the first case,
    D*2F(r_n)/R^2 < 25/144 < 1/2      in the second case.

Thus the entire root core-output branch is covered, including root reversal out of the core and entry into the right-child core only at its terminal. No early-return surrogate is substituted for the actual output, and no quadratic law is assumed before the actual return.

## 11. Original-radius first-landing bound

Consider the first strict crossing of the original full trajectory, with exterior source radius `r`, gradient `p`, physical level `k`, original chronological prefix mass `P`, and landing `z`. All earlier radii decreased and every earlier query gradient was at least `p`. Since `P>=S_k` and `r>=v+p`,

    R >= r+P*p >= v+A_k*p,
    z=(1+d_k)*p-r <= d_k*p-v.

The strict mass inequality yields

    z <= (d_k/A_k)*(R-v)-v
       < (c-2)*(R-v)-v =: L_land(R).

The multiplier is applied to `R-v>=A_k p>0`, so its strict inequality has the correct direction. This is an inequality for the **actual first crossing and actual original start**, not for an unattained interval-envelope trajectory.

Define

    Rcrit = v+(1+v)/(c-2).

Then `L_land(Rcrit)=1`. Any first landing with `z>=1` forces `R>Rcrit`, including the landing tie `z=1`, because the landing bound is strict. In the residual band,

    Rcrit > v+(7/3)*(1+v)
          = (10/3)*v+7/3 > 28/5.

The final comparison uses `v>49/50`, with
`(10/3)*(49/50)+7/3=28/5`. Hence the exact uniform margin is

    D*kappa/Rcrit^2 < (603/25)/(28/5)^2
                    = 603/784 < 1.

This is uniform in the cap and original start. The dependence on `kappa` is explicitly retained through `v=1-kappa` before taking the uniform bounds.

If `R<=Rcrit` and every step after this crossing preserves the core, then `0<z<L_land(R)<=1`, so `r_n<=z`. Moreover

    L_land(R)/R = (c-2)-(c-1)*v/R

is increasing for `R>0` in the band, and equals `1/Rcrit` at `Rcrit`. Therefore `r_n/R<=1/Rcrit`. If instead `R>Rcrit` and a later core entry is absorbing, then `r_n<=1` gives `r_n/R<1/Rcrit`. The absorbing qualification is indispensable and is checked next.

## 12. The original left child ends exterior: exhaustive partition

Suppose `y=|Psi_J(s)|>1`. Because the left child is core preserving, it never entered the closed core. This includes the original start and every point up to the root source. A zero hit before the root would contradict `y>1`; any zero hit later settles the terminal. A history with no strict crossing is already covered by the ray bound.

For the remaining histories, exactly one of the following occurs.

### A. A crossing occurred in the original left child

Its first crossing is also the full trajectory's first. Its source and landing are both exterior: a landing in the closed core would have remained there until the left-child terminal, contradicting `y>1`. The first-landing bound therefore forces `R>Rcrit`.

If the root output or any later point enters the closed core, every step remaining after that entry belongs to the right child and preserves the core. Thus

    D*2F(r_n)/R^2 <= D*kappa/R^2
                   < D*kappa/Rcrit^2 < 603/784.

If no such entry occurs, the original history, every query, and the terminal are all exterior. The exterior-only Huber comparison gives score at most `1/D`. Arbitrarily many later exterior crossings are included in this alternative.

### B. The original left child had no crossing

The first crossing occurs at the root or within the right child. At the root its source is exterior by `y>1`. In the right child its source is also exterior, because any earlier core entry at the root output or in the right child would have been absorbing and would forbid this first strict crossing. This argument does not assume core preservation by the root.

Every step after this first crossing belongs to the right child, so each preserves the core.

* If `R<=Rcrit`, its landing is strictly below 1; the paid-radius estimate gives `r_n/R<=1/Rcrit`, and the terminal core energy yields `D*2F(r_n)/R^2<603/784`.
* If `R>Rcrit` and any point from this crossing onward enters the closed core, that entry is absorbing and gives the same `603/784` bound.
* If `R>Rcrit` and no such entry occurs, the whole history is exterior. Before the first crossing, an earlier right-child core entry would have prevented that crossing, and the original left child is already known exterior. Thus the exterior-only Huber comparison applies.

These alternatives exhaust `y>1`. A same-side saturated root entry with no earlier crossing cannot be followed by a strict crossing and is in the direct ray case. A same-side core entry at the actual terminal uses the crossing-and-core alternative if a crossing occurred earlier, and otherwise uses the ray case. No post-entry contraction is assumed when entry is terminal.

| First crossing in a left child that ends exterior | Its landing must be exterior, which pays `R>Rcrit`; this is not inferred merely from an interval radius. |

| Crossing terminal at the cap | Ruled out by `p_n<=aB<B`, not assumed. |

| Stronger terminal-gradient theorem | Not invoked or decided. |

## Exact claim screened

For the SAME enclosing `m>=1536`, define

    c=(1+sqrt(2))*(1+epsilon), a=beta/c,
    d_j=a*c^j, H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+d_j,H_j),
    S=sum H_m, D=1+2S.

For every fixed `0<kappa<=1`, finite `B>=kappa`, and real original `s!=0`, use

    v=1-kappa,
    g(x)=sign(x)*min(B,max(kappa*|x|,|x|-v)),
    f(x)=integral_0^x g(t)dt.

The audited candidate claims for the actual complete `H_m` trajectory

    2f(x_n)/s^2 <= (1+21/(4m))/D < 17/(16D).

The function has unique minimizer 0, so the denominator is its true original distance squared. No `kappa=0` extension is included. This is a result for one specified fixed odd scalar function family, not for all one-dimensional convex smooth functions or the full finite-dimensional minimax class.
